# deckd and OpenWalkieTalkie.app — the host service design

## Status (2026-09-23)

Weeks 1 to 8 of §15 are built, and §6.8's document model on top of them. **The rest of this paragraph is the week-7-era text**: it was lost when this file was reverted by mistake on 2026-09-23 and restored from the integration tree, so it understates weeks 6 to 8 — read §6.8, §15 and `docs/deck-device-checklist.md` for what those actually did. Weeks 1 to 5 of §15 are built. Weeks 1 and 2 have been run against the deck; of week 3's own seven proof rows (`docs/deck-device-checklist.md` §11, "Week 3 rows"; teardown aside) **two ran off-device on 2026-09-21** — a real `claude 2.1.278` session reaching the roster through its own hooks, and `integrations install/uninstall` leaving throwaway `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`/`CODEX_HOME` dirs byte-identical — and the other **five are still open**, because they need a hand on the deck or a logged-in CLI: the title beacon, a real permission card, the 3-question group, the snooze degrade and Codex's `/hooks` trust review. The dated record of the two sits under the rows in the checklist. One week-2 row is still open with them (the ENTER edge). The design below is unchanged except where this section says so, with one exception worth naming: **§5 was rewritten on 2026-09-20** to carry the corrections C1–C27 that week 3 measured against the installed `claude 2.1.276` and `codex-cli 0.153.4`. It had been describing shapes that do not work (an http `SessionStart` hook, a `session_id` title item, a `tool_use_id` that the event does not carry) and two that silently void a whole config file (a key named `hooks` inside our Claude block; an array-form Codex `command`). Where §5 and an older paragraph elsewhere disagree, §5 is the measured one. The code is the `host/` workspace ([host/README.md](../host/README.md) is the user-facing description), the gate is `tools/host-test.sh` (fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, 974 tests — `deck-proto` 77, `deck-wrap` 40, `deck-model` 166, `deck-client` 11, `deckd` 589, `deck-gui-core` 91, which is also the `OWT_MIN_HOST_WS_TESTS` floor and the number `deckd`'s `gate_ratchet` row holds this sentence and the script to — the byte-for-byte parity of `deckd device dry-run` with `tools/deck-sim.py --dry-run`, 74 reports, an `api` step that drives a real daemon on a private data dir through `curl`, `deckd ctl`, the Python virtual agent and websocat, and an `adapters` step that installs into throwaway `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`/`CODEX_HOME` dirs, asserts what it wrote in front of the real validators — `claude doctor`'s stdout for `Invalid settings`, and `codex app-server`'s `hooks/list` for a non-empty `hooks` array with no warnings, plus `--trust` recording a hash Codex handed back — then uninstalls and diffs the three installer-owned files byte for byte; its CLI-dependent rows (`claude doctor`, `codex hooks/list`, `codex trust`) `skip` with a printed reason when that CLI is not on `PATH`), and `tools/host-test.sh --hardware` runs the device rows, week 1's and week 2's alike.

**Built.** `deck-proto` in full (vocabulary, encoder with the §6.5 byte caps, chunker, assembler, OMD2 codec (since removed with icon sync); its contract tests mount the firmware's `src/deck/{text,msg,json,proto}.rs`). `deck-model`: the §3 types and `Store::apply`, the card/delivery/hold/lease state machines, `project()`, the schemas, plus the week-2 pieces slot policy and redaction, and the §14 property tests (barrier order, `synced` last, `card` after its `agent`, caps, card-id allocation). `deckd`: the `deckd-hid` thread with `DeviceView`, the four lanes, shadow, sync barrier and the §6.3 session machine; icon sync folded in on the same pacer (D15); a minimal core actor that applies commands and executes `Device`/`Timer`/`Persist` effects with the two asymmetric channels of §2.3; `config.toml` with the §9 defaults, `state.json`, `status.json`, the flock and the three log files; `run [--foreground] [--demo]`, `status`, `doctor`, `device flood|sync|metrics|dry-run`, `iconsync`. `--demo` registers the mockup roster through the store's API as an in-process virtual agent. `tools/install-host.sh` installs `deckd run` as `ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie.deckd` and removes the old icon-sync agent.

**Built (week 2).** The core actor in full: sessions (connection, logical and internal), `holds` + `HoldGuard`, `waiters` + mailboxes, the push/poll/focus/none/hold delivery executors with a truthful `Delivered{ok}`, guarded operations + `op.confirm_requested`, the rate limits of §11, the implicit 10 s heartbeat, logical-session expiry, the bus (broadcast 4096 + ring) and the snapshot cell. `deckd::api`: the UDS NDJSON accept loop (peer creds, hello first) and axum on `127.0.0.1:[api] port` with `POST /v1/rpc` (single and batch), `GET /v1/ws`, `GET /v1/events` (SSE), the REST mirror, `/v1/info`, `/v1/schema`, one `dispatch`, the scope table, bearer tokens (`tokens.toml`, blake3, `deckd token`), the `Host`/`Origin` checks, the 1 MiB body cap and the port-in-use degrade with the squatter probe. `deckd::trust` v1: `LOCAL_PEERPID` → `SecCode` → requirement → path, the ancestry cap and `dev_requirement`. `deck-client` (UDS and WS) with auto-reconnect and `since:rev`, and `deckd ctl|events|token|status --live|run --no-device` on top of it. `config.toml` and `tokens.toml` hot reload (notify + `ArcSwap`, invalid → keep the last good), `log.level` reload and `log.tail`. The hid writer thread S3 asked for (`deckd-hid` never blocks on `hid_write`; `write_stalls`, `writes_in_flight_max`, `answer_to_clear_host_us`/`_wire_us` in `metrics.get`). `--demo` gains the §4.13 `cohdl-api` agent with a permission card the demo holds itself. The §15 week-2 exit tests exist by name in `host/crates/deckd/tests/` and `host/crates/deck-client/tests/` (listed in the §15 row). **Not built:** the adapters (`hooks.*`, `/v1/hooks/*`, `deckd hook`) — the scope `hooks:ingest` exists and the methods answer `-32601 {available:"week 3"}`, the routes 404 — voice **engines**: week 4's seams were in and nothing was on the other end of them (`voice.say|stop|listen|cancel` are reducer methods that allocate an utterance, queue it, open a §7.4 session for the caller's own principal and session, and abort one; `perm.status` answers and `perm.request` is stubbed at the prompt with `{status:"prompt_not_shown"}` and its reason in `limits`, §10.4), and `Effect::Audio` reached a bounded channel with no thread behind it — **week 4 filled that in, see "Built (week 4)" below**, leaving `voice.engines`/`voice.test` as the one voice pair still `-32013 degraded{reason:"voice_unavailable"}` — **week 6 built those two, see below** — desktop injection (`Effect::Desktop` is a log line), the GUI, packaging, MCP, `device.raw` and `config.set` (all `-32601`, and `service.restart`/`service.stop` with them until the daemon can ask its own runtime for an exit code — a method that answers `-32601` is not in the table and so not in `/v1/schema`; **`config.set`, `service.restart` and `service.stop` are built in week 6 and are rows of the table now**).

**Built (week 3).** The adapters, and the doors and seams they needed. **The week-2 seams first**, each with the test that pins it: a registration can say what kind of pid it carries (`pid_kind`, so liveness can tell a CLI from the `sh -c` that ran the hook); the hold budget rides on the card (`hooks.<cli>.hold_s` is the adapter's input, not a store concern); `SetCardHeld` carries a principal and a session, so an adapter may hold only its own agents' cards, and a refusal reaches the caller as the store's own error instead of one flat `invalid`; `hooks.*` is answered on the tokio side and never as a `CoreMsg::Call` — a held hook parked in the core thread would stop every agent, every device line and every other request for the length of the hold; a second park on one `(agent, card)` is safe (the degraded same-id re-push is exactly that shape); parked hooks are capped by `[api] max_held_hooks = 64`, over which a hook fails open rather than queueing; the capability list is a seam adapters join; the §5.6 sanitiser and labels live in `deck-model`; and a truncated card records the shown→original `choice_map`. **Both hook doors, one handler**: `POST /v1/hooks/{claude|codex}` over TCP (bearer, scope `hooks:ingest`) and `hooks.<cli>` as a JSON-RPC method on the UDS/WS, with report-only events answered `{}` *before* the work is enqueued and held events parked for the whole budget without the door cutting them. `/v1/info.capabilities` now names `hooks.claude` and `hooks.codex`, which is what `integrations.status` reads to tell "installed" from "installed and answered". **The Claude adapter** (`adapters/claude.rs`), pure, driven by recorded fixtures under `tests/fixtures/hooks/claude/`: permission cards that are first-answer-wins and are taken off the deck by the `PostToolUse`/`Stop` that says the operator answered in the terminal, sequential `AskUserQuestion` groups under one budget with one `updatedInput.answers` written after the last card, `PermissionRequest{AskUserQuestion}` and the 30 s after a released group answered `{}` with no card, every response carrying the event name that fired, the `SessionStart` beacon as a complete OSC 2 sequence, releases (snooze, expiry, `HoldGone`) degrading to `⌨ Focus terminal` under the same card id. **The Codex adapter** (`adapters/codex.rs` on its own plan layer, `adapters/rt.rs`): held `PermissionRequest` whose deny carries its message, `notify` deduped against the `Stop` hook, `SessionEnd` releasing the agent, and an event it does not know answered `{}` with nothing changed. **`deckd hook <claude|codex|codex-notify|claude-statusline|gemini>`**: stdin to 16 MiB, the env subset, the parent walk through both spawn forms (exec and `sh -c`) with `pid_kind:"unknown"` when there is no CLI ancestor, a 500 ms connect, and `{}` + exit 0 on every failure path — a non-zero exit from a hook is a *block* signal in both CLIs, so the helper never emits one; `claude-statusline` copies the user's own command through byte for byte and reports its failures verbatim, and `codex-notify` takes its payload from the last argv entry. **`deckd::integrations`** (`status|install|uninstall|repair` for `claude`, `codex`, `icon-sync`): plan → apply → verify, where the plan is pure (`--dry-run` prints "What this writes"), the apply moves every live file into `<data>/backups/<file>.<ts>.bak` by rename before publishing, and the verify re-reads the Claude file with Claude's own fatal-settings predicate and restores the backup rather than reporting a success that voided the user's settings. It refuses to write hooks a binary without `deckd hook` could not answer, refuses a translocated path (and only warns outside `/Applications`), reports a foreign hook instead of overwriting it, appends to `allowedHttpHookUrls` and takes back only the one string it appended, writes the Codex command as a shell-quoted string, appends its Codex group last, and ends a Codex install by printing the `/hooks` review step unless `--trust` is given, in which case it reads the hash from `hooks/list` first. **Liveness**: `claude agents --json` every 5 s on `spawn_blocking`, keyed off `status` (interactive rows carry no `state`), plus `kill(pid,0)` every 2 s for `pid_kind: cli` only; a probe that cannot run is inconclusive and changes nothing, and a session must be missing from two conclusive ticks before it is released. **Not built in week 3:** `hooks.claude_statusline` (the helper chains and prints the user's line, but the daemon answers the method `-32601`, so the `session_name` title and the `rate_limits` usage feed are not wired); `hooks.gemini` and `deckd hook gemini` exist and are refused `-32601 {available:"v1.1"}`; the Ghostty resolver and the tab pin (week 5, and S3 below is what they will be built from); `integrations.pause`; the `gemini` and `claude-desktop` install targets, which report `absent`.

**Built (week 4).** The voice pipeline, and the sixteen seams it needed before an engine could exist. **The seams**: `Command::Voice(VoiceIn{Partial,Final,Route,Level,Permissions,Error})` with a `FromAudio` channel into the core, mirroring `FromHid` — without it nothing an engine learned could reach the store; `PttInfo` carries the `voice.listen` caller's session beside its principal, so a derived `op.answer` can name both; `AudioCmd::Listen` carries the input, the STT locale and §7.6's contextual strings; `set speaker`/`set mic` push an `AudioCmd::Gain` beside their `Persist` instead of being inert; `voice.say|stop|listen|cancel` are reducer methods (with their §4.9 guard deliberately re-wired through `Guard::guarded`, a different entry point from `check_listen`, so the refusal is not silently un-inherited) while `engines|test|get` stay core-answered; `Route` gained `ScoOurs` and `Reopening` and is driven from `VoiceIn::Route`; `perm.status` (scope `voice`) and `perm.request` (scope `admin`) are rows in the method table; `main` is given to a CFRunLoop once the runtime and the core are up (a turning pump rather than `CFRunLoopRun()`, because `main` is also the supervisor watching the stop flag a signal handler sets); the audio crates are pinned macOS-gated (cpal =0.18.2, rtrb =0.4.0, rubato =5.0.0, objc2-* =0.6.4/0.3.2); `OpOrder` and `AnswerInput` both carry the utterance a dictated order or answer came from; one `deck_model::voice::{intent, talk_intent}` grammar answers both CLIs (and `deck_model::guard` stays **wider** than it on purpose — an unknown word must still demand trust); `device.name` cuts on a char boundary with no ellipsis (`sanitize::cut_bare`) and title-cases; `[voice]` is hot; and the transcript has its own keep-latest ≤4/s bucket (`Key::Transcript`). **The engine**: `deckd::audio` behind the seams the measurements forced into shape (`CaptureSource`, `SttEngine`/`SttSession + Send`, `TtsEngine`/`TtsJob` returning its own format with a separate `TtsStop` handle, `RouteEvent`), each with a fake beside it; `deckd-audio` as a pure control loop; `deckd-tts` fed from the main thread; the RT-safe mixer and `Arc<Lanes>` with the consumer hand-off; the route SM with `Reopening` at the **current** nominal rate and `ScoExternal` as a first-class path; a `deckd-audio-watch` thread that re-registers its CoreAudio property listeners because the deck's AudioDeviceIDs move on every connection. **`deckd run` without `--no-device` starts all of it on macOS**: `audio::spawn` puts `deckd-audio`, `deckd-tts`, the watcher and the Apple STT/TTS engines on the other end of the `Effect::Audio` channel, so the bounded channel the seam pass shipped is no longer a channel into nothing. `--no-device` still leaves `AudioHandle::none()` in place, which is what the `api` gate step runs against — a door test may not open a microphone. **Policy**: §7.4's `Arming` liveness keyed on the first **non-zero** sample (not the first buffer, V2); the announce policy of §7.7 with `announce_questions`; per-agent voices round-robin from `default_pool` into `state.json.voices`. **Not built in week 4:** `voice.engines`/`voice.test` still answered `-32013 degraded{reason:"voice_unavailable"}` (**built in week 6**, see below); cloud STT/TTS engines (the traits exist, the implementations do not); broadcast PTT; the prompting half of §7.8, which is stubbed at the prompt and gated on week 7 (§10.4, §16 Q5) because a launchd-spawned daemon is its own responsible process and no test run from a terminal says anything about it; `[voice] mic_warm`, `speak_over_sco` and `slow_sco_ms` were configured, pushed to the engine and reported applied but **read by no code** when week 4's review found them; they are read now (`audio/engine.rs` consults `speak_over_sco` before speaking over a capture, `mic_warm` when deciding what to hold the mic open for, and `slow_sco_ms` as the bring-up patience the measurements set) — a correction that reached a config struct and stopped there is the failure mode that pass was looking for (§7.1).

**Built (week 7, the service surface).** `deckd doctor` reads launchd's last exit into a sentence and detects a dangling LaunchAgent, from any copy of the binary (§10.5, `deckd::launchd`); `deckd diagnostics` writes the one redacted file an operator can send, with a test that puts a known token, a known prompt and a known transcript line into the inputs and asserts none of the three is anywhere in the output; `deckd uninstall [--purge]` takes the hook entries back and puts the CLIs' own files back byte for byte, uninstalling **before** a purge deletes the backups it restores from (the §15 exit test, proven against throwaway `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`/`CODEX_HOME` dirs); §4.1's port degrade runs end to end against a real `deckd run` whose port this test process squats — the daemon stays up, the UDS door and the hid thread keep answering, `service.info` and `service.degraded` name the squatter by **pid**, and `config.set{api.port}` → `integrations.repair claude` → restart clears it; and `docs/deckd-api.md` is generated from `GET /v1/schema`, with the row that fails when the checked-in file is not what the schema produces. **Not claimed:** launchd's own behaviour under `{SuccessfulExit: false}` was measured by hand on 2026-09-22 against a throwaway label (the readings are in §10.5) — the parser is unit-tested against that measured output, but no test bootstraps a LaunchAgent, and no test boots one out.

**Reviewed (week 2).** The slice was then put through an adversarial review — five lenses (security/trust, protocol conformance, concurrency, model, operations), two independent refuters per finding, so a claim neither of them could reproduce was dropped — which confirmed 18 distinct defects (24 reports, six of them the same defect seen by two lenses); the fixers verified and closed a further eight of the unrefuted candidates. All of them are closed (20 commits on four branches by area, then three follow-ups), each with the test that would have caught it. What mattered: `agent.wait` answered `{"kind":"timeout"}` for an agent the session does not own, or that does not exist, instead of the ownership error (`WaitResult` had no error variant; it gained `Refused(ApiError)`). A `poll` mailbox died with its connection, so §12's "the answer waits for `mailbox_ttl_s` and a reconnecting session `agent.claim`s it" was unreachable; a closed session's mail now moves to an orphan queue the next owner adopts and the beat prunes. `op.ack` cleared a **held** permission card without releasing the hold, so the CLI stayed parked for the whole `hold_s` with nothing on its screen; §4.7 scopes `op.ack` to Ready cards and a `q` card is now `-32005 bad_state{reason:"not_ready"}`. A notification-form `agent.wait` (no `id`) popped the mailbox and the door reported `Delivered{ok:true}` although nothing was ever written — the card was cleared and the answer lost; a method whose reply delivers after the write is refused in notification form on both doors. One bearer token could mint unbounded logical sessions with unbounded `client.name`s, which is an OOM from one loop: the name is capped at 64 bytes, a token at 32 logical sessions (its own least-recently-used is closed beyond that) and the daemon at 512 (`-32008` beyond that, so no token can evict another's). `[trust] gui_requirement`/`dev_requirement` were listed hot and `config.reload` reported them applied, but the verifier was built once at startup and never looked at another config — the requirements now live in an `ArcSwap` the verifier reads per call. `MacVerifier::verify` (Security.framework plus up to 64 `proc_pidinfo` hops, 250–500 ms measured) ran inline on the UDS accept loop, so one slow peer stalled every other connection; it moved to `spawn_blocking` inside the per-connection task, still ahead of that connection's hello reply. The hid loop joined the writer thread unconditionally, so a hung BLE write wedged `deckd-hid` and `SIGTERM` never completed; every close is bounded now and an overdue writer is abandoned. `agent.register` and `agent.card.set` rejected the exact shapes §4.6 and §4.15 publish as optional (`caps.focus`, `lease_ttl_s`, `choices`, `free_text`, `origin`, and `agent.heartbeat{agent}`, `device.set{k,v}`, `agent.reply{speak}` with them), and `/v1/schema` published the same over-strict contract, so a third party following the design and a third party following the schema were both wrong; serde defaults, one per design table cell, with every validation rule intact. And `service.degraded` never carried the `listener:{pid, command}` the door had already looked up, while `service.info` reported a port the daemon did not hold. Also fixed: a Ready step dispatched on the owner's caps route was cleared against the card's own `deliver` mode instead, so a delivered step failed `timeout` 4 s later; the `Host` check compared the name and threw the port away; one malformed batch member rejected the whole batch; a batch of notifications alone answered `[]` where JSON-RPC 2.0 says nothing; `session.hello{snapshot}` was ignored over `POST /v1/rpc`; `token.list` returned an object where §3.2 names a list; `deckd events` had no test of any kind; `CoreHandle::close_session` and `HoldGuard::drop` used `try_send`, so a full core channel leaked a session or parked a hook until `hold_s` (both run off the request path and now block for up to five seconds instead of dropping); the rate-limit buckets, keyed by agent id and by principal, grew for the daemon's whole uptime and are now forgotten once a bucket is idle and full again; and the gate carried pre-merge fallbacks that could pass a regression as a skip, while `--hardware` never ran the week-2 rows of the contract's §7 (it does now, one `ok`/`FAIL` line each).

**Hardware** (`docs/deck-device-checklist.md` §11, 2026-09-19, firmware 0.1.0, macOS 27.0, hidapi 2.6.7, ChatGPT.app running throughout). Gate run: hello 12/12, misses 0, one barrier (21 lines / 46 reports in 12.7 s), `synced`, link drops 0, device `err` 0, write avg 279 ms, max 419 ms; restart: new sid, one barrier, `synced`, hello 5/5. Same-sid `device sync`: second barrier, `card_ids` unchanged, no duplicate notice. Icon sync through `deckd`: a layout flip in `$CODEX_HOME/config.toml` pushed in 1.6 s, the revert in 1.4 s, 0 errors, channel 2 still flowing. 35-min flood soak: 5900 titles (≈2.8/s, the BLE ack cadence), hello 413/413, link drops 0, reopens 0, device `err` 0, decode/encode errors 0, write avg 327 ms, max 407 ms, `power … errors=0` in every UART window. A third non-seizing client next to the daemon counted the same input reports. Every week-1 exit test of §15 passes.

**Hardware (week 2)** (`docs/deck-device-checklist.md` §11 "Week 2 rows", 2026-09-19, same machine and firmware, `deckd run --foreground --demo` on a short data dir, the old icon-sync LaunchAgent stopped for the run and verified running again afterwards; nothing flashed, no monitor attached). Boot barrier in 17.5 s (21 lines / 46 reports at the BLE ack cadence), `synced`, and `deckd ctl agent.list` showing six agents: slots 1–5 the mockup roster, slot 6 `claude/cohdl-api` with card 1 `pending`/`hold`/`permission` — the §4.13 agent. **Guarded allow**: `op.answer{choice:0}` from `deckd ctl` (an untrusted UDS peer holding `operate`) → `-32002 {need:"trusted", how:"gui_confirm"}`, exit 3 with the CLI's hint, and exactly one `op.confirm_requested` on `deckd events --kinds op.*`; `{choice:2}` (Deny, never guarded) → `{delivered:true, via:"hold"}` in **246 ms**, the demo wrote the hold, the deck cleared the card and a fresh permission card was up 3 s later. **Poll with a waiter**: `host/examples/virtual_agent.py` over TCP took slot 7 and its parked `agent.wait` was answered in **28 ms** (`{delivered:true, via:"poll"}`, §4.9's answer-after-delivery), the script printed the answer and exited 0, the deck cleared the card. **Poll with nobody waiting**: `{delivered:false, reason:"timeout"}` after **4 128 ms** (the 4 s budget), `failed` in `agent.get`, the deck showed "not delivered" and reverted; the `agent.wait` 25 s later returned the answer, the deck got its `card clear` and the card was `delivered` — §4.8's late pickup on the device. Run totals: 3 `card.answered`, 3 `card.delivered`, 1 `card.failed`, 3 `card.cleared`, 1 `op.confirm_requested`. **The writer thread** re-ran the week-1 rows over two runs and a restart: hello 27/27 and 16/16, misses 0, one barrier each, **link drops 0**, device `err` 0, decode/encode errors 0, `write_avg_ms` 271–305, `write_max_ms` 376, `writes_in_flight_max` **1** (the hid thread hands one report over and never waits), `write_stalls` 0; the restart got a new sid, one barrier and `synced`.

**Spikes** (`docs/host-research/spikes-week1.md`, measured 2026-09-18) and what they do to §16:

- S1 — holding the `PermissionRequest` http hook **does not** suppress the terminal dialog (drawn 5–22 ms after the POST; a decision returned 20 s later is still honoured within 50–500 ms; a local keypress wins at once and Claude keeps the hook connection open until its timeout). **Q1, first half: no.** §5.1 becomes "first answer wins" as its fallback says: the card is cleared from `PostToolUse` / `Stop`, not from the hold's connection; `hold_s` only bounds a card nobody answered anywhere; `HoldGone` on a reset connection is a valid release; `{}` after a hold is harmless and the `⌨ Focus terminal` re-push stands.
- S2 — `PreToolUse` allow + `updatedInput.answers` answers `AskUserQuestion` interactively with nothing drawn during the hold; releasing with `{}` fires `PermissionRequest{AskUserQuestion}` 3 ms later, as §5.1 prescribes. **Q1, second half: yes** — question cards stay real cards; a snoozed sequential group should be released quickly rather than at the 120 s budget, since nothing is on the terminal meanwhile.
- S3 — IOKit delivers every input report to every non-seizing client (byte-identical totals for two and three clients over 60 s and a 3000-report storm). **Q3: yes**, no "deck host and Codex face are exclusive" toggle is needed. The finding that matters more: hidapi's macOS `hid_write` is a synchronous, BLE-acknowledged `IOHIDDeviceSetReport` — ≈360 ms per report while the deck is idle, ≈60 ms while it transmits — so the 8 ms pacer and the overflow back-off of §6.2 are moot on macOS (still relevant on Linux hidraw), a full sync takes 5–30 s rather than the 0.7 s of §6.7, "hook → toast" is 60–360 ms plus the line's reports, and the flood soak tops out at ≈2.8 lines/s idle. The writer should move to its own thread so reads and `focus{ack}` are not queued behind a title update; **done in week 2** (`device/writer.rs`: the `deckd-hid` thread hands one report to `deckd-hid-write` and never waits — `writes_in_flight_max` 1, `write_stalls` 0 on the deck), and the §6.7 table is still not revised.
- S4 — hidapi 2.6.7's exclusivity flag is process-global and reset by `hid_init()`; every `deckd` open goes through one `open_device` that clears it first (§6.6 holds).
- Q2 (SCO bring-up, A2DP resume) was left for week 4, as planned, and is **answered** there: 1031 ms median bring-up and 2307 ms median A2DP resume, n=16, 2026-09-21. Both thresholds blown, both defaults flipped (§16 Q2, §7.5).

**Spikes (week 3)**, measured 2026-09-20 against `claude 2.1.276`, `codex-cli 0.153.4`, Ghostty 1.3.1 and the week-2 daemon; each one is a fact the adapters were then built on, and all four are folded into §5 as C1–C27.

- **S1 — can a held http hook really stay open for 130 s? Yes.** A `POST /v1/rpc agent.wait{timeout_s:140}` stayed open **140.048 s** and returned `200` with the right body; no panics, no proxy interference on loopback. Nothing in deckd, hyper 1.11.1 or axum 0.8.9 cuts a 130 s request, so the held Claude hooks stay on HTTP as §5.1 designs them. Two riders the builders took: `Served::shutdown` abandons an in-flight request after 2 s (the store releases the hold either way, so `{}` is written promptly on `Released`), and a held request occupies a tokio task for its whole life — which is what the `[api] max_held_hooks` cap is for.
- **S2 — how do we mark our own entries in `settings.json`?** A top-level `_openwalkietalkie` key is accepted by `claude doctor`, leaves hooks firing, and **survives Claude's own rewrites** of the file, so no sidecar is needed. But `"_managed":"openwalkietalkie"` inside a hook object is **deleted** the next time Claude writes the file (a theme change stripped 3 of 3), so ownership is recognised by `url`/`command` and the marker is a hint. The finding that changed the installer: a key named `hooks`, or `PreToolUse`/`PermissionRequest`, **anywhere inside our block** is a fatal diagnostic that voids the *entire* settings file — `model` fell back to the built-in default and `Hooks: Found 0 total hooks in registry`, with nothing on screen or in the transcript. Two method facts came with it: `claude doctor` exits 0 even on a file it rejected (20/20 runs), so validation parses stdout for `Invalid settings`; and a bare temp `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` answers "Not logged in" and fires no hooks at all, so a throwaway config needs a dummy `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and a dead `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` to be useful.
- **S3 — does Ghostty's tab `name` keep the `deck:` stamp? No.** A tab's `name` is a read-only mirror of the live terminal title, so the stamp appears and is wiped by the very next OSC 2 — the shell prompt, before Claude's own rewrites even count. §5.1's plan of re-emitting it on `UserPromptSubmit`/`Stop` is dropped and the stamp becomes a one-shot beacon; the durable replacement, measured to work, is **resolve then pin**: match the nonce in the live titles, then `perform action "set_tab_title:deck:<id4>"`, which holds against every later OSC 2 (`set_surface_title` does not). `state.json.terminal_map` keys on the surface UUID, and AppleScript `-1712` means "wake and retry", not "no Ghostty".
- **S4 — what does Codex's hook trust actually require?** A freshly written user hook is `untrusted` and provably does not run; the `/hooks` review appends exactly `[hooks.state."<realpath>:<event>:<group>:<handler>"] trusted_hash = "sha256:…"` and nothing else. The trust key is **realpath-based** and **positional**, deckd **cannot compute the hash** (so `--trust` must ask app-server `hooks/list`, and there is no `codex hooks list` in 0.153.4), any path/timeout/matcher change re-breaks it as `modified`, and — confirmed twice — one array-form `command` makes Codex discard **every hook in the file** with only a `warnings` string to show for it.

**Built (week 5).** `deckd::desktop` and the GUI's testable half. **The Ghostty seam**: everything goes through `trait Scripting`, with `AppleScriptRunner` (NSAppleScript on the process's main thread, through week 4's `dispatch_async_f` hop) as the daemon's path and `OsaRunner` (a child on stdin) beside it, so every resolver and injector path is a counted `#[test]` against a scripted fake and needs no Ghostty at all. **The resolver**: resolve-then-pin, keyed on the surface UUID, with the two title fields kept apart — the beacon is found on `terminal.name`, an already-pinned session is re-found on `tab.name`, resolutions cache for 30 s, session end and shutdown unpin what they pinned. **The injector**: the three guards of §5.5 in order, then `input text … to:` and `send key "enter" to:`; `order.dispatched{text}` is published before a key is typed, and the later verdict comes back as `Command::OrderResult` so the reducer — not the injector — publishes `order.failed` with a rev the GUI's mirror accepts. **Guard 3** is `proc_pidinfo(PROC_PIDTBSDINFO)`, checking three things (alive, its process group *is* the tty's foreground group, and the tty it is on *is* the recorded one) with no fork. **`deck-gui-core`** carries the GUI's substance with no gpui dependency: the `async_channel` bus, a tokio bridge whose `Handle` is private with no accessor (so a UI thread cannot name a runtime to block on — the compiler enforces §8.1's rule rather than discipline), `ApiClient` over the UDS only, and the rev-checked mirror reducer with `HostState::detached()` behind a 42-test hermetic suite. **`openwalkietalkie-app`** is the gpui view layer, pinned `default-features = false, features = ["runtime_shaders", "macos-blade"]` because the default features need an Xcode Metal Toolchain that is not installed here. `/v1/info.capabilities` now names `inject.ghostty`, and `perm.status.automation.ghostty` answers from what the injector learned by trying — the only way an Apple Events grant can be read, since TCC offers no query for it. **Not built in week 5:** the one-time "which tab is *cohdl-api*?" prompt has a persisted answer path (`Resolver::adopt`) but no wire surface yet; `[desktop]` is not hot (a change needs a restart) and a `-1743` denial is never cleared once seen; guard 2's card half is evaluated when the job is built and not re-read by the worker, which leaves an open window a dialog can appear in (§5.5); `-1743`/`-1712` remain unit-only, since Automation is already granted to the responsible process on this machine and neither could be provoked; the ~118 ms window between guard 3's last check and the Return is measured and documented, not closed; and a pin that outlived a `kill -9` is cleared only late — `Desktop::restore` runs no scripts at start-up, so the tab keeps its old label until the next *clean* shutdown, and a session that never comes back cannot be cleared at all, because `agent.removed` names the id this run minted while the restored entry remembers the previous one. A returning session repairs its own entry on its first order, which is the common case. Blindly unpinning at start-up would be **wrong**: it destroys the only handle a session that *did* survive has. The fix is to carry the agent key on the release and reconcile restored pins against the roster after a grace window.

**Built (week 6 — the three methods the GUI calls).** `config.set`, `service.restart`/`service.stop` and `voice.engines`/`voice.test`; all five are rows of the method table now, and therefore of `/v1/schema`, which they may be only because they answer. **`config.set{patch}`** (§4.11, §9) writes managed keys of the operator's own `config.toml` through `toml_edit` — comments, order and formatting kept, nothing else touched, `null` to unset — and lets §3.7's notify + `ArcSwap` path apply them, because that is the one route a config has ever taken into the running daemon and a second one would mean two ideas of what is in force. What it may write is a **whitelist** (`core::dispatch::MANAGED`: `api.port`, `hooks.enabled`, the `[voice]` preferences by name): `config.toml` is where the trust boundary is declared, so `[trust]`, `[desktop] inject`, `[api] allowed_origins`/`uds_*_scope`, `[privacy] *` and `[voice.stt] allow_server` are refused `-32602` — a list of forbidden keys would fail open the next time `[trust]` grew a field. A patch is **all-or-nothing**: applied to one in-memory copy, validated whole, every key checked to have taken with nothing outside the patch moving, and only then published through a temp file plus a rename — so one bad key writes none of them and a refusal never reaches the disk even transiently. §4.1's "change port and re-install hooks" is one such patch, then `integrations.repair`, then `service.restart`. `hooks.enabled` joined `HOT_KEYS` with it: `Adapters::enabled()` already read the cell on every hook request, so it was live and reported "restart needed" — the mirror of B14's `[voice]`. **`service.restart` (75) / `service.stop` (0)** got the half that was missing: `service::Exit`, one cell the core writes and the supervision loop on `main` reads. It carries a deadline rather than a flag, because the reply is written by a door *after* `Core::call` returns and a supervisor acting the instant the core decided would race it — a client that never receives its reply cannot tell "restarted" from "crashed". `tests/service_exit.rs` proves that against the **real process**: the binary is spawned as launchd spawns it, the method is called over its own socket, the reply is read while the process is still alive, and the process then exits 75 (and 0). The plist was corrected with them — `KeepAlive: true` restarts on every exit and made both codes meaningless; it is `{SuccessfulExit: false}` now, which is what `EXIT_ALREADY_RUNNING`'s comment always described. **`voice.engines`/`voice.test`** answer from a cache, never from the run loop: reading an `EngineInfo` is a main-thread hop and the core thread carries every agent, hook and device line, so `audio::spawn` **posts** the read once when the engines come up and the answer arrives as a new `FromAudio::Engines` — the shape of week 5's `FromDesktop::Automation`, for the same reason. `voice.test{what:"speaker"}` queues §7.3's `ready` chime (110 ms, peak 0.2, the deck's own UID, the operator's own gain — never the system default device or volume) and reports the route it took; `mic`, `stt` and `tts` are reports and say so in `limits`. **Not built in week 6's method slice:** `voice.test` **does not exercise the recogniser or the synthesiser** — `stt`/`tts` report what the engine said about itself when it came up, and `mic` does not open a microphone, because a capture has an owner, a 60 s cap and a TCC grant behind it and driving an engine is the hop the core may not take; the `voice.*` pair is still `-32013 degraded{reason:"voice_unavailable", why}` under `--no-device` and in any build with no engine, which is what the `api` gate step runs with; `config.set` applies nothing itself, so a daemon whose watcher could not start (`WatchHandle::watching() == false`) needs a `config.reload` or a restart, exactly as a hand edit does; and the produce half of `FromAudio::Engines` — `audio::spawn`'s posted read — is exercised only by `tests/voice_mainloop.rs`, the `harness = false` target that *is* the main thread, because libtest owns no main queue.

**What the week-5 review found**, because the shape of these is worth more than the fixes. Three
adversarial passes ran against the finished code, each required to prove a finding by running it.
**The resolver could never have worked in production**: the Claude adapter writes the whole beacon
*name* (`deck:<id4>`) into the OSC 2 and into `Terminal::title_stamp`, and the resolver prepended
`deck:` again, so it searched for `deck:deck:<id4>`, every first resolution answered `Ambiguous(0)`,
no tab was ever pinned and every dictated order failed. Every test missed it because both sides
called the same `beacon_name` helper — and so did the hardware rows, which write their own beacon
the same way, so even a live run could not have caught it. The function whose doc promised "one
place that writes the beacon cannot disagree with the place that reads it" had **no production
caller at all**. **Guard 2's status list was missing `failed`** — the status that means the hook
response never reached the CLI, so Claude is asking in the terminal with Allow highlighted; it was
the one status that let an order through, and `text + Return` on a highlighted Allow is the worst
outcome this design has. (That list was incomplete in the build contract too, so the builder
implemented it faithfully.) **`voice.listen` was a second, unguarded door into the injector**:
`Store::dispatch_order` calls `op_order` directly rather than through `Request::OpOrder`, so the
trust guard never saw a transcript, and a caller refused `op.order` outright could inject through
the lesser `voice` scope. **A pin left behind by a killed daemon could capture the next run's
session**: `id4` is a function of the agent sequence alone, so the first agent of *every* run pins
`deck:0001`, and a `kill -9` leaves it there for a different session, in a different tab, on a
different tty to adopt — guard 3 cannot catch that, because the new session's pid and tty are both
genuinely its own. Three corrections were also found to be **decoration**: the `e_tdev` comparison
guard 3 exists for could be deleted with every test still green (the runner has no controlling tty,
so an earlier arm always answered first), the 30 s cache passed at 3600 s because every assertion
was written in terms of the constant rather than the number, and one documented `[desktop]` knob had
no code behind it at all and has been removed rather than left to imply otherwise.

**Week 8 (the soak buffer) — what it measured, and the number §15 got wrong.** §15's week-8 row
asks for "every v1 row of §12 exercised". That was audited row by row for the first time
(`docs/section-12-coverage.md`, 35 v1 rows, 18 mutation probes) and the honest count is **8
covered, 24 partial, 3 uncovered** — not 35. Worse than the arithmetic: **eleven of the partial
rows describe behaviour that was never built**, not behaviour that went untested — the 30 s lane
hold and its "release keys", the whole `err range` recovery (no correlation window, no re-send, no
`failed{device}`), firmware feature-flagging, both "not delivered to *name*" / "no route to *name*"
TTS lines, the duplicate-hook-config state, `not_foreground` as a `-32006` (it is an async
`order.failed`), the `unregister` action, the "(microphone not allowed)" transcript, the terminal
picker, and "utterance finalised first". Three more rows carry stale numbers (`ARMING_LIVENESS` is
2 500 ms where the row says 1.5 s; `metrics.get.outbox_overflows` is really `overflows`). §12 is
therefore a **design** document in those places and should be read as one until each row is either
built or struck.

Four mutations stayed green, i.e. four clauses the suite did not hold. Two are now pinned:
`eviction_candidate`'s card guard (the existing test gives its card-holder a `Q`, which flips the
agent to `Question`, so the `state == Idle` arm always answered first and the card clause was dead
— the case that matters is an **Idle** agent holding a *ready* card, and the new row is the only
test in the suite that fails when the clause is deleted), and the back-off length (asserted as
`pace_at(t0 + BACKOFF_FOR)`, which holds for a back-off of any length — `BACKOFF_FOR` could be cut
from 600 s to 60 s with everything green; it is now asserted at nine and eleven minutes). The other
two are recorded, not fixed: the overflow half-drain gate, and the hello's live local clock, whose
mechanism no test can see because every test pins `hm_override` and `clock_local: false`.

**The soak.** Not the 8 h / 24 h of §15 — a **24-minute** run against the real deck with `--demo`
traffic and the legacy icon-sync host attached as a second non-seizing HID client, sampling
`metrics.get` every 60 s. Over 1445 s: `link_drops` **0**, `decode_errors`/`encode_errors`/
`err_range`/`err_size`/`err_parse`/`err_unknown` **all 0**, `write_stalls` **0**, `overflows` **0**,
`ops_dropped` **0**, `outbox_depth` drained 30 → 0, `hello_misses_total` **1** (the row degrades at
3). `ch2_reports` grew **0 → 38**, which is the number open question 3's second half wanted: with
two clients attached our poll did **not** starve channel 2. The first half of Q3 — whether IOKit
delivers *every* report to both clients — needs a known-count comparison this run cannot make.
**Q5 remains open**: `perm.request` is still stubbed, so no TCC prompt was raised from a
launchd-spawned daemon, and answering it needs a person at the keyboard. The 8 h, 24 h and
sleep/wake soaks, and the 8-agent eviction soak, were **not run**.

**Open after week 3:** five of the seven week-3 proof rows have not been run (`docs/deck-device-checklist.md` §11, "Week 3 rows"): the title beacon, a real permission prompt answered from the deck, a 3-question group answered card by card with nothing drawn in the terminal, a snooze degrading under the same id, and a Codex session with its hooks trusted through `/hooks`. The other two ran off-device on 2026-09-21 and are recorded under the rows — a real `claude 2.1.278` session on the roster through its own hooks (the run that found the `pid_kind` symlink defect and the `[api] port = 0` defect), and `integrations install/uninstall` against throwaway `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`/`CODEX_HOME` dirs leaving all three installer-owned files byte-identical with `claude doctor` printing no `Invalid settings` line. Everything under the remaining five is proven in the daemon and against recorded payloads, not yet from an edge. The Usage face has its feeders since 2026-10-10 (`deckd::usage`, the way Zeron does it): the Claude account's windows are probed from `api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage` with the Claude Code login (the `Claude Code-credentials` Keychain item, which macOS asks about once; `~/.claude/.credentials.json` where that exists) every `[usage] poll_s`, the Codex app-server's `account/rateLimits/read` and `account/rateLimits/updated` come through the daemon client, and a `statusLine` chained by hand (`deckd hook claude-statusline`, which the plugin install no longer writes) is ingested for `session_name` titles and its `rate_limits`; ENTER on the face re-probes both. **Since the companion app (2026-11-11) the Claude bars are the owner's opt-in:** `[usage] providers` is `["codex"]` by default, a provider left out is neither probed nor drawn (the status line's Claude bars included), and the Faces & Keys page's Usage panel switches Claude on (§9). The Codex side of `integrations.status` asks `codex app-server`'s `hooks/list` whenever the call may run the CLIs and maps Codex's own verdict onto the row: `modified` → `modified`, a warning or no deckd rows → `hooks_not_firing`, `enabled: false` → `paused`. That is what reaches the two states nothing else can see, because deckd cannot compute a hook's hash (C14a iii): a `trusted_hash` that has gone stale in place — the table is still there, so the files alone still read `installed` while Codex skips the hook — and the `warnings` string that means Codex discarded the whole `hooks.json`. Reading `[hooks.state]` out of `config.toml` is the **fallback**, for a call that may not spawn (`detect:false`, every test) or a `codex` that will not answer; a row that fell back says so in `limits` and does not pass the guess off as a check. `--trust` asks the same `hooks/list`, and forges nothing. Desktop injection and the Ghostty pin of S3 are week 5. **Still open from week 2:** the ENTER row of `docs/deck-device-checklist.md` §11 needs a hand on the deck — `answer_to_clear_host_us` and `answer_to_clear_wire_us` are measured from a **device** `answer` input report and every answer in the week-2 run came from `deckd ctl` over the API, so both are still 0 (the host half of that budget is covered off-device by `core_delivery::answer_to_card_clear_under_100_ms`, 1–3 ms); until it is run, "device `answer` → `card clear` < 100 ms" of §15's week-2 row is proven in the daemon but not from an edge. The §6.7 latency budget is still written for the 8 ms pacer and has to be revised for the BLE ack cadence week 1's S3 measured. `deckd doctor` is still the minimal version (no launchctl exit-status analysis beyond the two labels). `[api] allowed_origins`, `config.set` and the GUI confirmation sheet have no client until the GUI lands: today `op.confirm_requested` goes on the bus and nothing offers the sheet, so a guarded allow can only be answered from the deck. Weeks 4 and 5 are built — see "Built (week 4)" and "Built (week 5)" above for what they do and do not claim. `op.confirm_requested` now has a client: the GUI's confirmation sheet. Next is week 6's remaining screens (Integrations, Voice, Service, Welcome, tray).

## Summary for the user

1. **What it is.** `deckd` is a background service that owns the deck's Bluetooth HID link, the roster of AI agents, the question/answer cards and the voice pipeline; `OpenWalkieTalkie.app` is a gpui GUI that is a pure client of it. Quit the GUI and nothing changes on the deck.
2. **Process model.** One signed bundle ships both binaries. `deckd run` is a LaunchAgent inside the app (SMAppService), so mic/speech/Automation grants land on one stable identity; `deckd hook|ctl|doctor|integrations|token` (and `deckd mcp` in v1.1) are thin clients of the same API.
3. Inside the daemon a single-threaded core actor owns one `Snapshot`; every input is a `Command`, every change an `Event{rev}` plus the `Effect`s the core then executes (device ops, deliveries, timers, hold releases). The HID thread owns the device's desired state (`DeviceView`) and writes it through the 8 ms pacer, lanes and the sync barrier; the API fan-out and the audio engine are projections of the same snapshot.
4. **The API in one paragraph.** JSON-RPC 2.0, newline-delimited over a Unix socket (peer credentials, `session.hello` first) and over `127.0.0.1:47201` HTTP/WS/SSE (bearer tokens, Origin/Host checks). *Since 2026-11-12 that TCP door is off by default. It is kept on only while an old install's Claude HTTP hooks still need it, an explicit `[api] tcp` always wins, and its removal, with its tokens, is planned for the next release (§4.1). The socket is the one door for connected apps.* Every connection is a **session**; an agent is owned by `{principal, session}`, and answers/orders are delivered to the owning session (or polled by an HTTP-only one). Methods: `agent.*` (register a virtual agent, set cards and replies, wait for answers/orders, focus your own terminal), `op.*` (answer, order, focus, snooze), `voice.*` (say, listen, stop), `device.*` (faces, settings, usage, macropad), and admin (`config`, `token`, `integrations`, `perm`, `service`). Every party sees the same snapshot and event stream, redacted per scope; the JSON Schema of everything is served from the one Rust source. The MCP server (`/mcp` + stdio shim) is designed here and ships in v1.1.
5. **Claude Code (v1)** gets http hooks: permission prompts and `AskUserQuestion` become deck cards held until the operator answers; snooze or expiry falls back to the terminal dialog; `Stop`/`Notification`/statusLine drive state, replies, announcements and the Usage face.
6. **Codex CLI (v1)** gets command hooks through the `deckd hook` helper with the same card mapping (permission holds). **Gemini CLI** is designed (§5.3) and ships in v1.1 — it is being replaced by Antigravity and its `BeforeTool` bypass is unverified.
7. **Claude Desktop and MCP clients (v1.1)**: one rmcp server (`deck_report`, `deck_ask`, `deck_say`, `deck_done`, `deck_wait`, `deck_list`, `deck_focus`) at `/mcp` and as a stdio shim; identity works for modern-era (session-less) clients; ChatGPT desktop stays out (remote MCP only).
8. **Third parties (v1)** get virtual agents with the same slot/lease policy as the CLIs, scoped tokens, `curl`/WS/SSE access and `agent.wait` long-polls; the `[scripts] on_event` hook is v1.1.
9. **Voice (v1)** lives in the daemon: Apple on-device STT for PTT, AVSpeech TTS with one voice per agent, an RT-safe lane mixer feeding the deck's custom BLE speaker (legacy Classic firmware can select CoreAudio/A2DP), barge-in on PTT, and an intent grammar (allow/deny/report/stop).
10. **Orders (dictated text)** reach a CLI through its owner (`agent.wait`, and MCP `deck_wait` in v1.1) or, for hook-only Claude sessions, by typing into the resolved Ghostty tab, guarded so it never confirms an open permission dialog or types into a bare shell.
11. **Trust boundary.** Agents run as the same uid as the daemon, so "same uid = trusted" is not enough: permission allows and terminal injection are accepted only from the deck and the code-signature-verified GUI by default; a voice answer is attributed to whoever started the capture, so an agent cannot say "yes" to its own card through `voice.listen`; hook tokens can only ingest hooks; no plaintext all-scope token is written to disk.
12. **Out of v1 (designed, v1.1):** MCP server + stdio shim + Claude Desktop, Gemini adapter, card paging (v1 truncates to 4 choices + PTT free text), `[scripts] on_event`, Macropad actions, the degraded Omarchy `ws`, usage feeders, `device.raw`, the port-hijack pause/rotate flows (v1 keeps the "port in use" banner + change port), `report_via = "command"`, the audit-token trust path (v1 uses `LOCAL_PEERPID`). **Out of v1 (v1.1/v2):** Codex app-server, Claude channels, Elicitation, tmux/CGEvent/AX injection, inbound focus sync, desk-presence hold shortening, cloud STT/TTS, LLM summaries, Linux (designed for, built in v2), broadcast PTT.
13. **Biggest risks:** whether a held Claude http hook really suppresses the terminal dialog (week-1 spike, fallback "first answer wins"); SCO bring-up and A2DP resume latency on macOS 27 (measured in week 4 and worse than the design assumed: 1031 ms and 2307 ms medians, both over their thresholds — §16 Q2); IOKit delivering reports to two non-seizing HID clients (Codex app + `deckd`).
14. Further risks: TCC prompts from a launchd-spawned daemon (GUI fallback designed in, identity equality asserted in CI); Ghostty terminal matching when several sessions share a cwd (title stamp, then a one-time GUI pick).
15. **Plan:** 8 weeks, one Rust engineer, macOS, at the cut scope of item 12 (the full design as written is a 13–15 week build); hardware gates every week; v1.1 (≈6–8 weeks) restores the cut items and adds Codex app-server, Claude channels and more injectors; v2 adds Linux/Omarchy.

---

Synthesis of the three judged proposals (`proposal-api-first.md` as the base; the core actor, device writer, audio engine and latency budget from `proposal-device-realtime.md`; the operator flow, packaging, TCC hedge, diagnostics and degrade paths from `proposal-product-ops.md`), with every weakness the judges named resolved in place, and revised after the design review (sessions as the ownership unit, voice attribution, NDJSON on the UDS, `DeviceView`, effects-as-data, the asymmetric channels, the audio lane hand-off, the modern-era MCP identity, the cut v1 scope). Dated 2026-09-18. Research is cited as `research/<file>.md:<line>` (the six reports under `docs/host-research/`), the wire contract as `docs/deck-protocol.md:<line>` (P) and the device grammar as `docs/agent-command-deck.md:<line>` (G). The firmware is done; nothing here changes the wire.

This is the document to implement from. Names in `code` are the names to use: files, sockets, ports, bundle ids, plist labels, config keys, API methods, event types, crate names.

## 0. Decisions at a glance

| # | Decision | Pick | Why (and what it replaces) |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Process shape | `deckd` (tokio daemon, LaunchAgent inside `OpenWalkieTalkie.app`, registered with SMAppService) + `OpenWalkieTalkie.app` (gpui GUI) as a **pure client** | User's binding decision; the only shape with a stable TCC identity for mic/speech/Automation (`host-research/voice-macos.md:15,102`, `host-research/critic.md:20`) that survives a GUI quit |
| D2 | Domain model | One `Snapshot` owned by a **single-threaded core actor**; every mutation is a `Command`, `apply(Command, now) -> (Vec<Event>, Vec<Effect>)` is pure (`deck-model`), and the core executes the effects (device ops, deliveries, timers, hold releases, audio); device writer, API and audio are projections | api-first's model + device-realtime's actor; a `RwLock` cannot guarantee the orderings the wire needs (`channel.seq`→`focus.ack`, PTT latch, answer→clear budget); effects as data keep the reducer testable with a simulated clock and give holds, oneshots and timers one owner |
| D3 | Identity on the wire | Agents are addressed by a **stable `agent` id** everywhere in the API; the device slot 1..8 is a read-only projection (`slot`, may be `null`) | Fixes device-realtime's slot-keyed API; slots are reassigned on eviction and never leak into third-party code |
| D4 | Ownership | Every agent has an owner **`{principal, session}`** (a session id is assigned at `session.hello`; a stateless HTTP client gets a logical session keyed by token + `client.name`), a lease, `agent.claim` as the only takeover path, and each card carries a `deliver` mode (`hold`/`push`/`poll`/`focus`/`none`) whose `card clear` semantics are honest; delivery goes to the owning session; `inject` is an *order* route, never a card route | api-first D5 with the reviewers' fix: two scripts (or the GUI and a script) under one uid are different sessions, so same-key registrations conflict instead of silently sharing an agent; hook adapters are just owners speaking the same primitives; `focus` (critique) makes the degraded and informational cards answerable on the device without a "not delivered" revert |
| D5 | Transport | Two doors, one `dispatch(Principal, Request) -> Response`: UDS `deckd.sock` speaks **raw newline-delimited JSON-RPC** (own tokio accept loop, peer creds read once per connection, `session.hello` as line 1); axum serves **TCP only** — `http://127.0.0.1:47201` HTTP/WS/SSE (bearer token, Host/Origin checks) and `/mcp` (v1.1); `deckd mcp --stdio` shim for Claude Desktop (v1.1). `curl --unix-socket` is unsupported (use `deckd ctl`). **2026-11-12:** the TCP door is off by default (`[api] tcp`, §4.1), kept on only while an old install's Claude HTTP hooks still need it, and an explicit `[api] tcp` always wins; its removal, tokens with it, is planned for the next release; the socket is the one door for connected apps | Claude `http` hooks and MCP clients need TCP (`host-research/critic.md:6`); the GUI/helpers prefer peer creds (`host-research/rust-service-stack.md:14`); HTTP-over-UDS (the research's `axum::serve(UnixListener)`) was dropped because the hello-first trust check, the stdio shim and `deckd hook` all want a line protocol, and the MCP spec itself says custom UDS transports SHOULD reuse newline-delimited JSON-RPC (`host-research/rust-service-stack.md:61`) |
| D6 | Envelope | JSON-RPC 2.0; server push as notifications `method:"event"` with `rev`; errors in the JSON-RPC server range `-32000..-32099`; REST GET + SSE mirror | api-first's envelope with the judges' fix (no custom positive codes) |
| D7 | Scopes and content | `observe` / `observe:content` / `observe:raw` / `agent` / `operate` / `voice` / `device` / `admin`; every model field is *state* or *content*; content is redacted per subscriber at fan-out; retention knobs in `[privacy]` | api-first §4.3 + product-ops' explicit `raw` opt-in |
| D8 | Port conflict | Fixed `47201`; if the bind fails the daemon **keeps serving the UDS and the device**, emits `service.degraded{reason:"port_in_use"}`, probes the squatter (`GET /v1/info`), raises a **tray alert** and offers one-click "change port and re-install hooks". Fail-open holds only while nothing accepts on 47201: a foreign listener makes every held Claude hook block for its installed timeout and receives hook payloads plus the `hooks` token, so the status is `hooks_hijack_risk`; in v1 the banner names the listener and offers change port + re-install, in v1.1 the tray alert, one-click temporary removal of the managed http hook entries and token rotation inside `integrations.repair` follow | product-ops; never exit (api-first) and never silently move (device-realtime) — the deck link must stay up; the hijack case is the critique's |
| D9 | Question round trip | Hold the synchronous Claude/Codex permission/question request; `hold_s = 120`; **snooze (CANCEL) releases the hook** with no decision and the card is re-pushed under the **same id** as a degraded `⌨ ` card (content update only, no new chime, keeps its drawer state, P:115); on expiry respond `{}` and degrade the same way; a degraded card delivers by `focus` (ENTER focuses the terminal and is cleared honestly); desk-presence shortening is v1.1 behind a flag after the spike | device-realtime's snooze + product-ops' degrade; api-first's adaptive probe deferred (unverified on macOS 27, and typing a label does not answer an arrow-driven dialog) |
| D10 | Orders (free text) | Owner push/poll (MCP `deck_wait`, virtual agents) → **Ghostty AppleScript `input text` + `send key`** (v1, Automation TCC) → `none` (kept on the GUI with Copy, TTS "no route to <name>"). Injection carries **only operator-originated text** (PTT transcript, GUI text box) from a **trusted principal** (D19), is refused while the agent has a permission/question card open or the CLI is not the foreground process of its tty, and never carries text derived from a card choice; Claude channels, Codex app-server, tmux, CGEvent are v1.1 | Keeps the spec's voice loop alive in v1 (fixes product-ops' "no input path") without the research-preview channel flag; the guards close the critique's unattended-approval and bare-shell cases |
| D11 | Device writer | On the HID thread: keyed, prioritised, coalescing outbox with lanes P0..P3, 1 s aging, device-side shadow, per-slot title token bucket, fragment-atomic chunking, 8 ms pacer with overflow-signature back-off; the **full sync is a sequenced barrier**, not lane traffic | device-realtime §5 with the judges' fix (`synced` was in the top lane and could purge the roster) |
| D12 | Answer ack | `card clear` is sent when the answer is **delivered** (hook response written, RPC response flushed, `agent.wait` result written, AppleScript returned); budget 4 s; on failure the store marks the card `failed` and the device reverts by itself — store and device agree | device-realtime D12; fixes api-first's `card.cleared{delivered:false}` divergence |
| D13 | Audio | Daemon owns capture (cpal by UID, PTT-scoped), STT (`SFSpeechRecognizer` on-device en-US), TTS (`AVSpeechSynthesizer` buffers) and an RT-safe lane mixer in the CoreAudio render callback; output pinned to the deck's A2DP UID and kept open while the route is A2DP; route state machine with `ScoExternal`/`ScoStuck` | device-realtime §9; fixes product-ops' lazy-open clipping and api-first's missing external-SCO state |
| D14 | Summarisation | Heuristic only (first sentence, fences stripped, ≤120 chars); `agent.reply{announce}` is the extension point for an external summariser client; Ready-card steps only from `deck_done`/`agent.card.set type:r` | Spec says no summarisation (`host-research/spec.md:39`); no key in the daemon |
| D15 | Icon sync | Removed with the Codex Micro emulation: no channel 3, no icon-sync code in `deckd`. `tools/install-host.sh` still boots out a leftover `com.conol.openmicrodial.icon-sync` agent | One writer per host (`host-research/critic.md:16`) |
| D16 | Identity names | Bundle id `ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie`; LaunchAgent label `ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie.deckd`; data dir `~/Library/Application Support/OpenWalkieTalkie/`; logs `~/Library/Logs/OpenWalkieTalkie/` | Continues the repo's existing `ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie.*` naming (`tools/install-host.sh`) |
| D17 | GUI | gpui `=0.2.2` + gpui-component `=0.5.1`, openmicrokbd's `events`/`HostState`/`HostEffect`/`detached()` pattern with the state as a mirror fed by API events; tray, Sparkle, `package-macos.sh` copied; login item via `SMAppService.mainApp` (not `auto-launch`, whose AppleScript path triggers an Automation prompt and a second Login Items row); the GUI talks **UDS only** (no WS fallback, no token on disk); the tokio bridge is a std thread running a 2-worker runtime whose `Handle` is a gpui `Global` (daemon→GUI via `events::post`, GUI→daemon via an mpsc + oneshot awaited in `cx.spawn`, never `block_on` on the gpui thread) | User's binding decision (`host-research/openmicrokbd.md:18-27`); the two deviations are the critique's; Zed's `gpui_tokio` is a Zed-workspace crate that does not build against `gpui =0.2.2`, so the bridge is spelled out in §8.1 |
| D18 | v1 scope | 8 weeks, one engineer, at the **cut** scope: Claude + Codex adapters, virtual agents, voice, Ghostty injection, a 4-screen GUI, packaging. Designed here but v1.1: MCP server/stdio shim/Claude Desktop, Gemini, card paging, `[scripts]`, Macropad actions, degraded Omarchy `ws`, usage feeders, `device.raw`, hijack pause/rotate, `report_via="command"`, audit-token trust; app-server, Elicitation, tmux, AX/CGEvent, Claude channels, inbound tab focus sync, cloud engines, Linux are v1.1/v2 | Judges: api-first's v1 was not credible; product-ops' was too thin on voice; the reviewers priced the full design at 13–15 weeks, so v1 keeps the three real risks (Codex-only regression, held-hook spike, TCC gate) and defers the rest |
| D19 | Trust model | The parties this daemon supervises run as the **same uid**, so uid is not the boundary. A **trusted principal** is `device` (a line that arrived on the HID link) or the GUI verified over the UDS by code signature (§4.2). By default (`[trust] require_trusted_for_allow = true`) `op.answer{allow}` on permission cards, `op.order` with `via:inject` and `voice.listen{ctx:"answer"}` on a permission card are accepted from trusted principals only (`-32002` otherwise); a voice answer is attributed to the caller of `voice.listen`, never to `device`; the `hooks` token gets `hooks:ingest` only; UDS peers default to `observe`; peers whose ancestry contains an agent CLI are capped at `observe,agent,voice,hooks:ingest`; no plaintext all-scope token is written to disk; card choices carry no hidden `value` | Critique blocker: with the old model any agent could approve its own permission prompt or type into another agent's terminal via loopback; the reviewers' second blocker (self-approval through `voice.say` + `voice.listen` with the `mcp` token) is closed by attributing the answer to the caller |

## 1. Goals and non-goals

**Goals (v1, macOS).**
1. The deck works with terminals the operator launched themselves: Claude Code and Codex CLI sessions (Gemini CLI in v1.1) appear on the roster within 100 ms of their hook, questions become cards, answers from the deck reach the CLI, replies are announced, PTT dictation lands in the agent's prompt.
2. One open API that every party uses: the GUI, the hook adapters, the MCP server, the voice pipeline, `curl`, a Python script, a future phone client. Anything the GUI can do, `curl` can do; there is no privileged internal path.
3. Third parties are first-class: a script can register a *virtual agent*, get a slot under the same policy as the CLIs, receive answers and orders, and die cleanly.
4. The wire contract is honoured exactly: 8 ms pacing, the 64-entry firmware queue, ≤4 `agent` updates/s/agent, 1024-byte lines, `card clear` within 5 s, `focus{ack}` echo, `up` handling, `boot` adopt/push (P:37-104).
5. Hook payloads (prompts, tool inputs, replies, and the titles derived from them) are treated as the most sensitive data on the box: scoped, redacted per subscriber, not persisted by default; and the agents themselves, which run as the operator's uid, cannot use the API to approve their own permission prompts or type into each other's terminals.
6. The deck is its own device (OpenWalkieTalkie, VID 1209 PID 5754): `deckd` owns channel 5, the firmware answers its system RPC on channel 2 by itself, and nothing else needs a host.
7. Boringly operable: Welcome sheet, SMAppService, one TCC story, `deckd doctor`, redacted diagnostics, uninstall that restores backups, Sparkle updates that restart the daemon.

**Non-goals (v1).** Rendering the agents' work (the terminal is the work surface, `host-research/spec.md:27`); LLM summarisation; ChatGPT desktop (remote-MCP only, `host-research/desktop-apps.md:56`); remote (non-loopback) access; broadcast PTT (`host-research/spec.md:136`); Linux (designed for, §13); daemon-hosted sessions (`claude -p --input-format stream-json`, ACP); in-process plugins. Designed in this document but built in v1.1 (D18): the MCP server and Claude Desktop shim, Gemini, card paging, `[scripts] on_event`, Macropad actions, the Omarchy face on macOS, usage feeders, `device.raw`, the port-hijack pause/rotate flows and `report_via = "command"`.

## 2. Architecture

### 2.1 Processes

```
 ┌──────────────────────────────── OpenWalkieTalkie.app (one signed bundle, Developer ID) ────────────────────────────────┐
 │ Contents/MacOS/OpenWalkieTalkie   gpui GUI: pure API client, tray, Welcome/TCC sheets, Sparkle, SMAppService toggle    │
 │ Contents/MacOS/deckd           the service + CLI: run | ctl | hook | token | integrations | doctor | mcp (v1.1)     │
 │ Contents/Library/LaunchAgents/ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie.deckd.plist   (BundleProgram → Contents/MacOS/deckd run)      │
 └──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────┘
                        │ UDS deckd.sock (NDJSON JSON-RPC, peer creds, hello first)                 │ launchd (SMAppService)
                        ▼                                                                          ▼
 ┌──────────────────────────────────────────── deckd (LaunchAgent, tokio) ─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │  api: UDS accept loop (NDJSON) + axum on 127.0.0.1:47201 (HTTP/WS/SSE; /mcp in v1.1)                                │
 │       auth → principal{scopes} + session → dispatch(Principal, Request) → Command                                   │
 │       adapters: claude (http hooks) · codex (deckd hook helper over UDS) · virtual (API) · gemini, mcp (v1.1)       │
 │                              │                                                                                      │
 │                              ▼                                                                                      │
 │      core actor (owns Snapshot{rev,…}; Command in, Event{rev} + Effect out; executes effects: timers, slots,        │
 │                  card ids, holds: HashMap<(AgentId, CardId), HoldHandle>, waiters: HashMap<AgentId, Vec<oneshot>>)             │
 │               │ broadcast<Event>            │ DeviceOp (bounded 512) → hid thread     │ audio commands              │
 │               ▼                              ▼                                        ▼                             │
 │   WS/SSE/UDS subscribers          hid thread: DeviceView (desired state,      audio control loop + CoreAudio render │
 │   (GUI, scripts, adapters,        one entry per key) + outbox (lanes,         callback (lane mixer), cpal capture,  │
 │    owner sessions, mailboxes)     shadow, barrier = ordered DeviceView        deckd-tts (AVSpeech synth, resample),  │
 │                                   dump, 8 ms pacer), framer,                  SFSpeech STT, route SM                │
 │                                   demux ch5 → core (unbounded)                                                      │
 │   desktop: Ghostty AppleScript (focus, input text), NSWorkspace activation, TCC prompts (main thread)               │
 └──────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                                ▼  hidapi, non-exclusive open, VID 1209 PID 5754 usage page ff00
                                      OpenWalkieTalkie (USB/BLE HID ch5 deck; ch2 the device's system RPC; BLE GATT voice)

 Parties on the API:  Claude Code http hooks → POST /v1/hooks/claude      deckd hook codex|gemini|claude → UDS hooks.* (scope hooks:ingest)
                      scripts → curl /v1/rpc, SSE /v1/events, WS /v1/ws (token)     GUI → UDS only (code-signature-verified peer = trusted principal)
                      v1.1: Claude Desktop → deckd mcp --stdio → UDS;  Claude Code/Codex/Gemini as MCP clients → POST /mcp
```

### 2.2 Data flow

Every inbound fact becomes a `Command` handled by the core actor in arrival order; `deck-model`'s pure reducer `apply(Command, now) -> (Vec<Event>, Vec<Effect>)` produces every observable change as an `Event { rev, ts, type, principal, data }` and every side effect as data (`Effect::{Device(DeviceOp), Deliver{agent, card, answer, deliver}, Timer{key, at}, ReleaseHold{card, reason}, Audio(..), Desktop(..)}`), which the core executes after the apply; the device writer, the API fan-out and the audio policy are projections of the same `Snapshot`. `rev` increases only when the snapshot actually changed, so hook redelivery is harmless. Time enters the reducer only through `now` and `Command::Tick(now)`, so every timer is testable with a simulated clock.

Write path, a Claude Code permission prompt: `PermissionRequest` → `POST /v1/hooks/claude` (principal `token:hooks`, scope `hooks:ingest`; the adapter, not the token, owns the agent) → adapter `claude` normalises it to `SetCardHeld{agent.card.set{type:q, deliver:"hold", origin:{kind:"permission"}}}` → the handler does `core.request(SetCardHeld{cmd, hold_tx}) -> CardId` and parks the HTTP response on `hold_rx`, behind a **drop guard** that sends `Command::HoldGone{agent, card}` if the parked future is dropped (Claude's own timeout, Ctrl-C, connection reset) → core stores `holds[(agent, card)] = HoldHandle`, bumps `rev`, emits `card.set` and `Effect::Device(Put(Card(slot,id), line))` → the hid thread updates its `DeviceView` and the outbox sends `card` (P1 lane; chime + toast on the deck, G:184-191) → audio policy plays the question chime → GUI shows the card with the hold countdown → operator presses ENTER on the deck → device `answer{agent,card,choice}` (principal `device`) → core sets the card `answering`, emits `Effect::Timer{DeliveryTimeout(card), now+4 s}` and `Effect::Deliver{deliver:hold}` → core resolves `holds[(agent, card)]` with the answer → the hook response `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PermissionRequest","decision":{"behavior":"allow"}}}` is written → the handler sends `HoldWritten{agent, card}` (the adapter's `agent.card.clear{delivered:true}`) → `Effect::Device(Remove(Card))` + `CardClear` (P0) well inside the 5 s budget (P:135) → `card.cleared` event. If `HoldGone` arrives first, the hold is released with no decision and the card is degraded at once (§4.8) instead of lingering for `hold_s`.

Read path: every party gets the same `Snapshot` at `session.hello` (redacted to its scopes) and the same `event` stream after it; a lagged subscriber (`RecvError::Lagged`) receives `{type:"resync", rev}` and calls `state.get`, or reconnects with `since:rev` and gets the ring replayed (`host-research/rust-service-stack.md:24,115`).

### 2.3 Thread model inside `deckd`

```
deckd
├── main thread          CFRunLoop: TCC prompts, SFSpeechRecognizer/AVSpeechSynthesizer callbacks, NSAppleScript
├── deckd-hid            std thread: hidapi read (zero-timeout, ≤2 ms poll) + DeviceView + outbox + 8 ms pacer + framer (ch5); opens the USB instance of the deck when one is listed, else the Bluetooth one (Rev A serves both)
├── CoreAudio render     real-time thread: lane mixer, atomics only, no allocation, no locks; owns the lane Consumers while the stream lives
├── cpal capture         per-PTT input callback → rtrb SPSC ring → STT feeder
├── deckd-audio          std thread: pure control loop (`crossbeam::select` over route/capture commands + tick): route SM, stream
│                        open/close (built, kept and dropped here — by the deckd-hid-probe precedent, not by a
│                        type bound: V10 measured cpal 0.18.2's `Stream` as `Send + Sync`, §7.2), capture
│                        start/stop — never synthesises
├── deckd-tts            std thread: PCM out of the main thread's AVSpeech callbacks (V13: they are delivered
│                        on main and nowhere else) → rubato resample → lane ring Producers; chime PCM
├── deckd-audio-watch    std thread: the CoreAudio property listeners that run while no stream is open (nominal
│                        rate, DeviceIsAlive, DeviceIsRunningSomewhere) → RouteEvents; it re-registers, because
│                        the deck's AudioDeviceIDs move on every connection (V8)
└── tokio runtime (2 workers)
    ├── core actor       Snapshot owner; `apply` + effect execution; `holds`, `waiters`; timers (holds, 4 s clear, 60 s PTT, leases, TTLs)
    │                    mailboxes: device lines (from the hid thread, unbounded, read first) and API/adapter commands (mpsc 1024)
    ├── api              UDS accept loop (NDJSON, peer creds) + axum TCP listener → one `dispatch`; WS/SSE writers; held hook handlers
    ├── adapters         claude / codex / virtual (v1); gemini / mcp (v1.1)
    ├── desktop          Ghostty resolver + injector behind a `Scripting` seam, NSWorkspace focus,
    │                    the sysctl foreground guard (§5.5); main-thread hops for Apple Events
    └── config / state / usage / liveness pollers (`claude agents --json`, kill(pid,0))
```

Rules: the render callback and the hid thread never take a lock shared with tokio; they exchange data through `rtrb` rings and `crossbeam` channels. Anything ObjC that must run on the main thread is posted with `dispatch_async(main_queue)` and completes back into core through a oneshot. The two channels between core and the hid thread are asymmetric on purpose:

- **core → hid**: a bounded `crossbeam::channel<DeviceOp>` (cap 512), `try_send` only — the core must never block on the pacer. On overflow the core drops the op, sets `needs_resync`, and once the channel is below half full re-emits the whole projection of the snapshot (`project(&Snapshot) -> Vec<DeviceOp>`, the same function that seeds the hid thread at daemon start) followed by `DeviceOp::Sync{reason:"overflow"}`, which makes the hid thread write a barrier (§6.5). The hid thread applies every `DeviceOp` to its `DeviceView` before touching the outbox, so an overflow can never leave the view and the device disagreeing for longer than one barrier.
- **hid → core**: an **unbounded** `crossbeam::channel<DeviceLine>`. Device edges are wire-limited (one report per edge, ≥4 ms apart, P:47-49), so the channel is bounded by physics; a `blocking_send` would stall the 8 ms pacer behind a slow core (a hook storm), and a `try_send` would drop an `answer` or a `ptt act:0` — a stuck card or a stuck PTT session. A small tokio task forwards it into the actor, and the actor's loop is `select! { biased; device line, api command }`, so device lines are never queued behind a hook storm.

## 3. Components and crates

One Cargo workspace at `host/` (superseding the old icon-sync crate), five crates:

| Crate | Kind | Responsibility | Key deps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `deck-proto` | lib, no I/O | The channel-5 vocabulary and framing shared with the firmware by `#[path = "../../src/deck/proto.rs"]`/`msg.rs`; host-side line encoder (byte caps on char boundaries, `…` on cut), report chunker (61-byte payloads), line assembler for device lines | serde_json |
| `deck-wrap` | lib, no I/O | The firmware's **own** text wrapping, mounted for the host: `#[path]` on `src/ui/{font,font_data,theme,layout}.rs`, `src/display/{framebuffer,orientation}.rs` and `src/deck/{msg,text}.rs`, under the module names those files name absolutely. `wrap_question`/`wrap_reply` break a body exactly where the deck would, at the size the operator chose. Not a reimplementation — two wrap implementations that can disagree is the defect §6.8 exists to avoid | `deck-proto`, `embedded-graphics` |
| `deck-model` | lib, no I/O | Domain types (`Agent`, `Card`, `Reply`, `Order`, `Principal`, `Session`, `Scope`, `DeviceState`, `VoiceState`, `Settings`, `Faces`, `Usage`), the `Store` reducer `apply(&mut self, Command, now: Instant) -> (Vec<Event>, Vec<Effect>)` (below), slot policy, content redaction, card/delivery/hold/lease state machines driven by `Command::Tick(now)`, the device projection `project(&Snapshot) -> Vec<DeviceOp>`, JSON Schemas via `schemars` | serde, schemars 1.2 |
| `deck-client` | lib, published | Typed async client (UDS newline-delimited JSON-RPC, or WS), `session.hello` (keeps the returned `session` id), auto-reconnect with `since:rev` resync, subscription filters; used by the GUI, `deckd ctl/hook/events/notify/statusline` (and `deckd mcp` in v1.1) | tokio, tokio-tungstenite 0.30 |
| `deckd` | bin | The service and CLI.
