Modules: `api` (`uds` accept loop + `dispatch`, axum 0.8.9 + tower-http on TCP, rmcp 3.4 at `/mcp` in v1.1), `trust` (UDS peer verification: `LOCAL_PEERPID` → `SecCode` → designated requirement + path in v1, audit token in v1.1; ancestry walk), `core` (actor: applies commands, executes effects, owns `holds`/`waiters`/timers), `bus` (broadcast 4096 + ring 4096), `hid` (hidapi 2.6.7), `device::{view,outbox,session,framer}`, `iconsync`, `audio::{engine,capture,route,tts_thread}`, `voice::{stt,tts,ptt,policy}`, `adapters::{claude,codex,virtual_}` (+ `gemini`, `mcp` in v1.1), `desktop::{ghostty,focus,inject,perm}`, `config` (toml 1.1 + toml_edit 0.25 for `config.set`'s in-place, comment-preserving write + notify 8.2 + arc-swap), `state`, `integrations` (port of openmicrokbd `agent_integrations.rs`), `diag` (`doctor`, bundle), `service` (flock, exit codes, launchd log fd setup, panic hook) | see cells |
| `deck-gui-core` | lib | **No gpui.** The client half of the app, so it is testable without a GPU: `deck-client` over UDS, `events.rs` bus, the tokio bridge, `ApiClient`, `HostState` mirror reducer + `HostEffect`, reconnect/resync, `HostState::detached()` and the hermetic reducer suite (§8.1) | async-channel, tokio, deck-client, deck-model |
| `openwalkietalkie-app` | bin (gpui) | The view layer only: the pages and first run (§8.2), the status item (`tray-icon`), Sparkle bridge, SMAppService registration (agent + `mainApp` login item), TCC sheets. Everything it renders comes from `deck-gui-core`'s mirror and the replies its pages keep; the confirmation sheet for untrusted allows went with the console (2026-11-11) | gpui =0.2.2 (`default-features = false`, `runtime_shaders`, `macos-blade` — §8.1), gpui-component =0.5.1, objc2-app-kit, deck-gui-core, tray-icon =0.25.1 (§8.1), objc2-service-management =0.3.2 |

The reducer's contract, which every state machine in this document is written against:

```rust
pub enum Command {                      // every input, from every door
    Api { principal: Principal, session: SessionId, req: Request },   // agent.*, op.*, voice.*, device.*, …
    SetCardHeld { cmd: CardSet, hold: HoldId },                       // a held hook: the core allocates the card id
    HoldWritten { agent: AgentId, card: CardId },                     // the parked response was written
    HoldGone { agent: AgentId, card: CardId },                        // … or dropped; holds are keyed (agent, card): ids are per slot
    Device(DeviceLine), Link(LinkEvent),                              // from the hid thread
    Delivered { agent: AgentId, card: CardId, ok: bool },             // result of an Effect::Deliver
    SessionClosed(SessionId), Tick(Instant), …
}
pub enum Effect {                       // every side effect, executed by the core after apply
    Device(DeviceOp),                                                 // Put / Remove / Sync / Control (§6.5)
    Deliver { agent: AgentId, card: CardId, answer: Answer, deliver: Deliver },  // hold → holds[(agent, card)]; push → session; poll → waiters[agent]
    Timer { key: TimerKey, at: Instant },                             // DeliveryTimeout(card), HoldExpiry(card), Lease(agent), ReadyTtl(card), PttCap, …
    ReleaseHold { agent: AgentId, card: CardId, reason: Release },    // snooze, expiry, agent gone, shutdown → holds[(agent, card)] resolves with `{}`
    Audio(AudioCmd), Desktop(DesktopCmd), Persist(StatePatch),
}
```

The core keeps what the reducer cannot own: `holds: HashMap<(AgentId, CardId), HoldHandle>` (a oneshot the held hook handler awaits), `waiters: HashMap<AgentId, Vec<oneshot::Sender<WaitResult>>>` (parked `agent.wait`s), per-session outboxes, and the timer wheel that turns `Effect::Timer` into a later `Command::Tick`. Adapters never hold any of these.

Responsibilities that are *not* where one might expect: the GUI never opens HID or audio and never writes third-party config files (the daemon's `integrations.*` does, because its `current_exe()` is the stable path hook files embed); the MCP stdio shim (v1.1) never touches TCC-gated APIs (its TCC identity would be Claude's, `host-research/desktop-apps.md:30`); the hook helper `deckd hook` asks for scope `hooks:ingest` only and announces itself as `client:{name:"deckd-hook"}`; `deckd ctl` shares the bundle signature but is **not** a trusted principal (its executable path is not the GUI's, §4.2), so `deckd ctl op.answer` on a permission card is refused by default; the core, not the hid thread, decides *what* the device should show, and the hid thread, not the core, decides *when* and *in what order* it is written.

Subcommands of `deckd`: `run [--foreground]`, `status [--json]`, `ctl <method> [json]`, `events [--kinds a,b] [--since rev]`, `hook <claude|codex|gemini|codex-notify|claude-statusline -- <cmd>>`, `mcp --stdio` (v1.1), `token <create --scopes a,b [--expires]|list|revoke|rotate>` (`create` prints the plaintext once and never stores it), `integrations <status|install|uninstall|repair> <target>`, `doctor [--json]`, `diagnostics [--out path]`, `install [--dev]`, `uninstall [--purge]`, `device <flood N|raw '<line>'>` (debug; `raw` is v1.1).
## 4. The open API

### 4.1 Transports and discovery

| Door | Address | Who | Auth |
|---|---|---|---|
| UDS | `~/Library/Application Support/OpenWalkieTalkie/deckd.sock` (dir 0700, socket 0600; 67 bytes, under macOS's 104-byte `sun_path`, `host-research/rust-service-stack.md:45`). Linux: `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/openwalkietalkie/deckd.sock`, else `${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-~/.cache}/openwalkietalkie/deckd.sock` (`config::Paths::detect`). On either, `$DECKD_DATA_DIR/deckd.sock` when `DECKD_DATA_DIR` is set, as the Omarchy unit sets it (§13) | GUI, `deckd ctl/hook/events` (and `deckd mcp` in v1.1), local scripts written against `deck-client` | **Raw newline-delimited JSON-RPC, not HTTP.** `UnixStream::peer_cred()` uid must equal the daemon's uid (`host-research/rust-service-stack.md:53`); pid recorded for audit; default scope for a UDS peer is **`observe`** (`[api] uds_default_scope`); a peer may ask for more in `session.hello` up to `[api] uds_max_scope` (default `admin`) **unless its process ancestry contains `claude`/`codex`/`gemini`/`node`** (macOS; then it is capped at `observe,agent,voice,hooks:ingest`; asking for `operate`/`admin` → `-32002 {need, reason:"agent_ancestry"}`); the GUI is recognised as the **trusted principal** by code signature (§4.2; macOS), never by pid or path alone. On Linux there is neither the cap nor a trusted principal (§13) |
| HTTP/WS/SSE (**off by default** since 2026-11-12, below) | `http://127.0.0.1:47201` (`[api] port`), never `0.0.0.0`; served by axum, the only thing axum serves | Claude Code `http` hooks, MCP streamable-HTTP clients (v1.1), browsers, non-Rust tools, `curl`, a future phone relay behind Tailscale `serve` | `Authorization: Bearer <token>` or `token` in `session.hello`; `Host` must be `127.0.0.1:47201` or `localhost:47201`; any `Origin` header not in `[api] allowed_origins` → 403 (`host-research/rust-service-stack.md:57,61`); body cap 1 MiB; 64 concurrent held hooks |
| MCP (v1.1) | `POST /mcp` (rmcp 3.4 `StreamableHttpService`, dual-era 2026-07-28 / 2025-11-25, `host-research/rust-service-stack.md:16,66`) and `deckd mcp --stdio` (proxies to the UDS) | Claude Code / Codex / Gemini as MCP clients; Claude Desktop (stdio only, registered in `claude_desktop_config.json` by bundle path, §4.16) | HTTP: bearer token with scope `agent`; stdio: inherits the UDS peer cred and asks for `agent`+`voice` (its ancestry is Claude, so it can never get `operate`; `deck_focus` therefore uses `agent.focus` on its own agent, §4.6) |

**The TCP door is off by default (2026-11-12, the owner's answer to decision 12).** Nothing shipped with the deck talks through it — the app, `deckd ctl`, the hook helpers, the channel helper, the connected apps such as Conol and the Omarchy bridge all use the UDS — and nobody outside this repository and Conol uses the port or a token. `deckd run` binds it only when `[api] tcp = true`, or, with the key left out of `config.toml`, while the hooks the pre-plugin installer gave Claude may still post to it (`config::Api::tcp_door`, `integrations::legacy_claude_hooks`): closing it under them would hand every Claude permission ask back to the terminal. Either of two things keeps it open. One: Claude's settings file (the one the installer reads) still carries one of our `type:"http"` hooks; only those count, since the other leftovers (the inventory, the allowlist pattern, the chained `statusLine`, the exec-form `SessionStart` hook) post nothing to the door, though each still makes the Claude row ask for Repair. The Integrations page's Repair migrates them, the app's Local API switch offers that Repair before turning the door off, and the next start finds the door closed. Two: deckd's own record of the Claude target in `integrations.json` is still a pre-plugin one, which is how hooks written under a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` the service does not see keep their door. The Claude row cannot show those (`claude::status` reads the settings file alone); `deckd integrations install claude` run under that `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` migrates them, and any Claude install, repair or removal replaces the record and so ends the exception. An explicit `[api] tcp`, `true` or `false`, always wins. `deckd run` logs the rule it applied, and `config.get` publishes the result as `api.tcp` and the rule as `api.tcp_rule` (`explicit`, `default` or `legacy_hooks`), which is not a key of the file: `config.set` refuses it. Off, nothing is bound: no `port_in_use` degrade, an empty `port` file and `service.info.port: null`. The switch is read at start, like the port, and is the Service page's Local API switch. **Removing the door is planned for the next release**, with the bearer tokens, `op.*`, the public `voice.*` methods, `focus.report` and the socket's `agent.wait`, none of which Conol or the Omarchy bridge calls. What this document says about the HTTP, WS and SSE doors, `curl` and the tokens holds only while the door is on.

Framing. The UDS door is a plain tokio `UnixListener` accept loop: per connection it reads the peer credentials once (`peer_cred()`, `LOCAL_PEERPID`; the audit token in v1.1), requires `session.hello` as **line 1** (any other first line → `-32001` and close), assigns the session id, and then feeds every further line — one JSON value per `\n`, max 256 KiB — to the same `dispatch(Principal, Session, Request) -> Response` that the axum handlers call for `POST /v1/rpc` and WS frames; notifications (`event`) are written back as lines. There is no HTTP parser on the socket, so `curl --unix-socket` is **unsupported** by design — the shell path is `deckd ctl` / `deckd events` (which are `deck-client` over the UDS), and `curl` uses the TCP door with a token. On WS one JSON value per text frame (`GET /v1/ws`). `GET /v1/info` is the only unauthenticated route:

```json
{"api":"1","daemon":"0.1.0","platform":"macos","capabilities":["device","voice.stt.apple","voice.tts.apple","mcp","hooks.claude","hooks.codex","hooks.gemini","inject.ghostty","virtual_agents"],
 "uds":"/Users/tony/Library/Application Support/OpenWalkieTalkie/deckd.sock","port":47201,"mcp":"/mcp","degraded":null}
```

Versioning: `/v1` prefix; fields are additive-only (the deck's own rule, P:55); unknown fields are ignored by every client; `GET /v1/schema` serves the schemars JSON Schema of every method, event and type from the one Rust source in `deck-model`, so `deck-client`, the GUI and third parties cannot drift — and [`docs/deckd-api.md`](deckd-api.md) is that schema rendered as a reference (`deckd api-doc`, never hand-written; a `deckd` test fails when the checked-in file is not what the schema produces, and names the one command that fixes it). A breaking change bumps `/v2` and keeps `/v1` alive for one minor release.

Port-in-use (D8), while the TCP door is on: bind failure → the daemon runs on the UDS only, `info.degraded = "port_in_use"`, event `service.degraded{reason:"port_in_use", port:47201, listener:{pid,command}}` (from `lsof -nP -iTCP:47201 -sTCP:LISTEN`). The daemon then probes `GET http://127.0.0.1:47201/v1/info` every 30 s: if the reply is not ours the status is **`hooks_hijack_risk`** — the installed Claude http hooks keep POSTing prompts, tool inputs, `last_assistant_message` and the `hooks` bearer token to whoever holds the port (any uid can bind it before login), and each held `PermissionRequest`/`AskUserQuestion` blocks Claude for the installed `timeout` (130 s), report-only hooks for theirs. Fail-open holds **only** while nothing accepts on the port. Response in v1: the banner and tray dot, and the Integrations card offers "Change port and re-install hooks" (`config.set{api.port}` → `integrations.repair claude`). v1.1 adds the one-time tray alert, "Pause Claude hooks now" (one-click temporary removal of the managed http entries, backups kept, re-added by `repair`) and `token.rotate hooks` inside `repair`.

### 4.2 Principals and scopes

A **principal** is `device` (the deck itself, internal: a line that arrived on the HID link), `uid:<n>` (UDS), `token:<name>` (HTTP), or the internal adapters (`adapter:claude|codex|gemini|mcp`, which own the hook-registered agents — a token never owns them). Each connection asks for scopes in `session.hello`; the daemon grants the intersection with the principal's maximum. A principal is additionally either **trusted** or not (below); trust is not a scope and cannot be requested.

**Sessions.** A principal is *who*; a **session** is *which connection*, and ownership and delivery are per session, because on the UDS every local process is the same `uid:<n>` — two scripts, or the GUI and a script, must not silently share an agent. Every WS/UDS connection gets a session id (`sess_…`) in the `session.hello` reply, and the session ends with the connection. A stateless HTTP client (`POST /v1/rpc` + SSE) gets a **logical session** keyed by `(token, client.name)` — `client.name` from `session.hello` or from `params.client` on any call, `"default"` when absent — that lives for `[api] mailbox_ttl_s = 600` past its last call. An agent's owner is `{principal, session}`; `agent.register` with a key owned by another **live** session → `-32004 conflict{owner:{principal,session}}`, even when the principal is the same; `agent.claim` (after lease expiry) is the **only** takeover path. `push` delivery, `agent.answer`/`agent.order` events and the implicit heartbeat all need a live WS/UDS session: a logical HTTP session registering `caps.answer:"push"` or `caps.order:"push"` gets `-32009 invalid{field:"caps.answer", why:"push needs a WS/UDS session"}` and must use `poll` (`agent.wait`, which parks on the logical session's mailbox) and explicit `agent.heartbeat`. Conflict and ownership checks compare session ids, never uids.

| Scope | Grants | Default holders |
|---|---|---|
| `observe` | Snapshot and events with **content fields redacted** (§4.3); `state.get`, `agent.list`, `device.get`, `voice.get` | every principal |
| `observe:content` | Content fields of all agents (card text/choices, reply text, transcripts, order text, `meta`, prompt/tool-derived titles) | GUI |
| `observe:raw` | Raw hook payloads (`hook.raw` events) and `log.tail` with content | `admin` only unless ticked explicitly |
| `hooks:ingest` | `POST /v1/hooks/*` / `hooks.*` only; no `agent.*`, no events. The adapter behind the route owns the agents | `hooks` token, `deckd hook` helper (`client.name:"deckd-hook"`) |
| `agent` | `agent.register/heartbeat/update/release/claim`, `agent.card.*`, `agent.reply`, `agent.wait`, `agent.focus` on agents this **session owns**; receives answers/orders for own agents with full content; `voice.say` for its own agent | `mcp` token, scripts acting as agents |
| `operate` | `op.answer/order/focus/snooze/ack` on any agent (implies `observe:content`). **Permission allows and `inject` orders additionally require a trusted principal** (or the GUI confirmation path, below) unless `[trust] require_trusted_for_allow = false` | GUI; the internal PTT dispatch acting for `device` (only for a real deck `ptt` edge) |
| `voice` | `voice.say/stop/listen/cancel/engines/test`. The answer or order produced by `voice.listen` is attributed to the **caller of `voice.listen`**, never to `device`, so `voice.listen{ctx:"answer"}` on a permission/elicitation card is a guarded operation (below; `-32002` before capture starts) | GUI |
| `device` | `device.set`, `device.faces.set`, `device.usage.set`, `device.macro.set`, `device.ws.set`, `focus.report`, opt-in `device.key/macro/ws` events | GUI, macropad scripts |
| `admin` | `config.*`, `token.*`, `integrations.*`, `perm.*`, `service.*`, `log.tail`, `metrics.get`, `device.raw` | GUI (UDS), `deckd ctl` (UDS, when asked for and not agent-descended) |

**Trust boundary.** The processes this daemon supervises (Claude Code, Codex, Gemini, in "full autopilot" per `host-research/spec.md:21`) run as the operator's uid; a sandboxed or tool-limited agent that can merely read a file and hit loopback must not be able to approve its own permission prompt or type into another agent's terminal. So:

- **Trusted principals** are exactly (1) `device` — an `answer`/`ptt` line that arrived on the HID link, and nothing else: a `voice.listen` started over the API runs the same PTT pipeline, but its transcript is dispatched under the **caller's** principal; (2) the GUI, verified on the UDS at `session.hello`. v1 path: `getsockopt(SOL_LOCAL, LOCAL_PEERPID)` → `SecCodeCopyGuestWithAttributes(kSecGuestAttributePid)` → `SecCodeCheckValidity` against `anchor apple generic and identifier "ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie" and certificate leaf[subject.OU] = <TeamID>` → `proc_pidpath` must end in `/Contents/MacOS/OpenWalkieTalkie`, re-read immediately after the validity check; the pid-reuse window between the two calls is documented as a known gap (Q26). v1.1 replaces the pid with the race-free audit token (`LOCAL_PEERTOKEN` → `kSecGuestAttributeAudit`) once the macOS 27 SDK path is verified. `deckd ctl`, which shares the signature, is not trusted (path). Dev builds: `[trust] dev_requirement` accepts an ad-hoc signature with a specific `cdhash`, logged loudly.
- **Guarded operations** (`[trust] require_trusted_for_allow = true`, default): `op.answer` with an allow decision (`choice` mapping to Allow/Allow always, or a PTT allow intent) on a card with `origin.kind ∈ {permission, elicitation}`; `op.order` that would resolve to `via:"inject"`; and `voice.listen{ctx:"answer", card}` on such a card — checked **before capture starts**, because the transcript it produces would be an allow or deny attributed to the caller. From an untrusted principal they return `-32002 forbidden {need:"trusted", how:"gui_confirm"}`, and the daemon emits `op.confirm_requested{agent, card|order, principal}` so the GUI can show a confirmation sheet (optionally behind `LAContext` Touch ID, `[trust] confirm = "sheet"|"touch_id"`); the operator's click on the sheet re-issues the call from the trusted GUI. Deny and snooze are never guarded (they withhold power, they do not grant it). `voice.say` is not guarded: once the answer is attributed to whoever started the capture, an agent that plays "yes" through the deck speaker (or the Mac speakers under `fallback_output = "system_default"`) gains nothing.
- **Ancestry defence in depth** (the accidental case: an agent's shell script connecting to the socket): a UDS peer whose ppid chain (`proc_pidinfo`/`sysctl kinfo_proc`) contains a process named `claude`, `codex`, `gemini` or `node` is capped at **`observe,agent,voice,hooks:ingest`** regardless of what it asks for. `hooks:ingest` is in the cap set on purpose: `deckd hook codex|gemini|claude` is by construction a descendant of the CLI (the parent walk is how it finds `cli_pid`), and the scope grants nothing beyond posting hook payloads to the adapter that owns the agent; the helper's hello carries `client:{name:"deckd-hook"}` so the grant is logged as such.
- Tokens are for browsers, sandboxed tools and a future phone relay. A token created in the GUI defaults to `observe,agent,voice`; `operate`, `observe:content`, `device`, `observe:raw` must be ticked deliberately, with the GUI explaining that hook payloads carry prompts and tool inputs (`host-research/critic.md:25`); a token is never trusted.

Built-in tokens at first run (stored blake3-hashed in `tokens.toml`, plaintext shown once): `hooks` (`hooks:ingest`, embedded in `~/.claude/settings.json`) and, in v1.1, `mcp` (`agent,voice`, embedded in `~/.codex/config.toml`/`~/.gemini/settings.json` MCP entries). There is **no** `operator` token and no plaintext token file on disk; a `curl` user runs `deckd token create --scopes observe,voice` once and keeps the printed value themselves.

### 4.3 Content classes, redaction, retention

State fields: ids, `slot`, `kind`, `name`, `state`, `since`, `title_source`, card `id/type/status/deliver/origin.kind/choices_n`, device link, voice state, settings, faces, usage. Content fields: `card.text`, `card.choices[].label/detail`, `reply.text`, `transcript.*`, `order.text`, `agent.meta`, `session.cwd`, `session.pid`, `session.terminal`, `hook.raw`, and **`title` whenever `title_source ∈ {prompt, tool}`** (the first 64 B of the operator's prompt or a tool input such as `Bash: psql … staging` is content, not state; only `session_name`- and `cwd`-derived titles are state). The deck still shows every title on its roster — that is the physical room, which the operator controls — but the API does not hand prompt heads to every `observe` principal (e.g. every MCP-connected model via `deck_list`).

For `observe`-only principals content strings are `null` and the object carries `redacted:true`; counts still work (`choices_n`); a prompt/tool title is `null` with `title_source` kept so the GUI-less client can still say "working on a prompt". The event ring is stored once unredacted and redacted per subscriber at fan-out. Retention (`[privacy]`): `keep_tool_input = "pending"` (tool inputs live only while the card is pending, then are dropped from the store and the ring), `keep_prompts = false` (prompt text derives the title, then is dropped), `keep_replies = "last"`, `transcript_log = false`, `log_content = false`, `event_ring = 4096`. Raw hook payloads are never written to disk.

### 4.4 Envelope and errors

```jsonc
// request
{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":7,"method":"agent.update","params":{"agent":"ag_7hq2v3","state":"working","title":"Compiling openmicro"}}
// response
{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":7,"result":{"rev":1201}}
// error
{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":7,"error":{"code":-32002,"message":"forbidden","data":{"need":"operate"}}}
// event (never has an id)
{"jsonrpc":"2.0","method":"event","params":{"rev":1202,"ts":"2026-09-18T19:42:03.120Z","type":"card.set","principal":"token:hooks","data":{…}}}
```

Error codes: standard `-32700`, `-32600..-32603`, plus the server range: `-32001 unauthorized`, `-32002 forbidden {need}`, `-32003 not_found`, `-32004 conflict {owner:{principal,session}}` (agent owned by another live session, or a stale card), `-32005 bad_state {status, reason?}`, `-32006 undeliverable {reason}`, `-32007 device_offline`, `-32008 rate_limited {retry_ms}`, `-32009 invalid {field, why}`, `-32010 timeout`, `-32011 lease_expired`, `-32012 too_large`, `-32013 degraded {reason}`. Batches are accepted on every door. Every mutating method returns `{rev}`.

### 4.5 Session methods

| Method | Params | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `session.hello` | `{client:{name,version}, token?, scopes:["agent"], subscribe:["agent.*","card.*","device.link"], snapshot:true, since?:rev}` | `{api:"1", daemon, principal:{id,scopes,trusted}, session:"sess_…", rev, snapshot?, replayed?:n}`; must be the first line on the UDS and the first message on WS; over `POST /v1/rpc` it is optional and binds the caller to the logical session `(token, client.name)` (§4.2) — a request without `hello` and without `params.client` runs in `(token, "default")`, may call methods (curl-grade) but gets no events; token only here or in `Authorization`, never in a URL |
| `session.subscribe` / `session.unsubscribe` | `{types:[…]}` (globs) | `{subscribed:[…]}`; high-volume types (`transcript.partial`, `voice.level`, `device.key`, `device.macro`, `device.raw`, `hook.raw`) are never matched by `*` and must be named (obs-websocket's rule, `host-research/rust-service-stack.md:14`) |
| `session.ping` | `{}` | `{ts}` |
| `state.get` | `{}` | `{rev, snapshot}` redacted per scope |
| `state.events` | `{since:rev, limit?, types?}` | `{events:[…], gap:bool}`; `gap:true` means the ring no longer holds `since` → call `state.get` |

### 4.6 Agent methods — the virtual-agent surface

Any principal with `agent` may register an agent. Hook adapters, the MCP server and scripts all use exactly these.

| Method | Params | Result / rules |
|---|---|---|
| `agent.register` | `{kind:"claude"\|"codex"\|"gemini"\|"other", name, key?:"claude:<session_id>", cwd?, pid?, pid_kind?:"cli"|"shell"|"unknown", terminal?:{program,tty,tmux_pane,iterm_session,term_session,title_stamp}, lease_ttl_s:30, caps:{answer:"push"\|"poll"\|"none", order:"push"\|"poll"\|"none", focus:bool}, slot_hint?:1..8, voice?, meta?}` | `{agent:"ag_…", slot:3\|null, lease:{expires}, owner:{principal,session}}`. `key` makes registration idempotent: same key + same **session** → same agent (no `rev` bump if nothing changed); same key from any other live session (same uid or not) → `-32004 conflict{owner:{principal,session}}`; after lease expiry the agent is orphaned and `agent.claim` transfers it. `caps.answer:"hold"` and `caps.order:"inject"` are **adapter-only values** (`-32009` from the API, exactly like `deliver:"hold"`); a virtual agent's `terminal`/`pid` are informational (shown in the GUI, used for nothing else — the injector only ever types into terminals the hook helper resolved, §5.5). `push` caps need a WS/UDS session (§4.2). `pid_kind` is what the hook helper's parent walk resolved for `pid`; §5's `kill(pid,0)` liveness poll runs **only** for `"cli"`, so an omitted value is never polled and a later re-register under the same `key` is how an `"unknown"` is corrected. Rate limit 30/min/principal (external principals only, §11) |
| `agent.heartbeat` | `{agent}` or `{agents:[…]}` | `{expires}`; a WS/UDS session that stays open is heartbeated implicitly every 10 s for every agent it owns; a logical HTTP session must call this (or any other method on the agent, which counts) |
| `agent.update` | `{agent, state?, title?, since?, name?, meta?}` | `{rev}`; external principals: ≤10/s per agent, `-32008` above; adapter-originated updates are never rate-limited — core coalesces them keep-latest per field before the outbox, which sends ≤4/s per slot (P:44) |
| `agent.release` | `{agent, reason?}` | Agent removed (`agent gone` on the device), cards dropped, slot freed |
| `agent.claim` | `{key}` | Take over an orphaned agent (lease expired) into the calling session; returns the same `agent` id. The **only** ownership transfer in the API |
| `agent.focus` | `{agent}` | Focus the terminal/window of an agent **this session owns** (the same `desktop::focus` path as `op.focus`, then `focus` to the device without `ack`); under `agent`, so an MCP session or a script can bring its own terminal forward without `operate` |
| `agent.card.set` | `{agent, id?, type:"q"\|"r", text, choices?:[{label,detail?}], rec?, free_text?:true, deliver:"push"\|"poll"\|"focus"\|"none", timeout_s?, origin?:{kind:"custom"\|"ready", tool?, request_id?}}` | `{card:{id}}`; one pending card per agent (the device model, P:115); a new card replaces a pending one; the device raises a notice only for a new `(agent,id)`; >4 choices: v1 truncates to the first 4 (`card.truncated{shown:4,total:n}`, PTT free text stays available, the GUI shows all), v1.1 pages (§5.6). **Choices carry no hidden `value`**: the answer payload is `{choice:n, label}` with exactly the text the operator saw on the deck (≤32 B) — a third party that needs a longer identifier maps `n` itself. `origin.kind` `permission`/`question`/`elicitation` are **reserved for the built-in adapters**; a card from any token or script is `custom` and is prefixed `» ` on the deck and badged "custom" in the GUI so it cannot impersonate a permission prompt. `deliver:"hold"` is likewise adapter-only (`-32009` from the API) |
| `agent.card.clear` | `{agent, card, delivered:bool, reason?}` | Sends `card clear`; **must follow an answer within 4 s** or the card is marked `failed` (§4.8) |
| `agent.reply` | `{agent, text, speak?:bool, announce?:string, lane?:"ready"\|"chatter"}` | Sets the last reply (device gets ≤200 B, P:116); `announce` overrides the heuristic one-liner (the external-summariser extension point); `speak` queues TTS in the agent's voice |
| `agent.wait` | `{agent, timeout_s:600}` | Long-poll from the owning session; resolves with the next `{kind:"answer"\|"order"\|"snooze"\|"timeout", …}` for that agent from the session's mailbox; shell scripts (and the MCP `deck_ask`/`deck_wait` tools in v1.1) use this |
| `agent.list` / `agent.get` | `{}` / `{agent}` | Redacted per scope; `agent.list` includes unslotted agents |

Question groups (Claude `AskUserQuestion` with 2–4 questions, §5.1): the adapter issues the cards **sequentially**, never concurrently. Card *k* is set with `origin.group:{index:k,count:n}`; when its `answer` arrives the daemon immediately sends `card clear` for *k* (P0; the answer is delivered into the group accumulator, which is honest — nothing can lose it) and only then enqueues card *k+1* (the device model would otherwise replace card *k* unanswered, firmware `src/deck/model.rs:604-608`); only the **last** card's clear waits for the hook response. A snooze on any card releases the whole group (the remaining cards are never issued; the snoozed one is re-pushed degraded). The group is **one held request**, so `hold_s` bounds the whole group, not each card: when the budget runs out the remaining cards are skipped, the hook is released with `{}` and the current card is degraded like any expiry. A `deck-model` test asserts that every intermediate group answer is cleared or replaced within 4 s.

### 4.7 Operator methods — the "hands"

| Method | Params | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `op.answer` | `{agent, card, choice?:n, text?}` | What the deck's `answer` and a PTT `ctx:answer` become internally; returns `{delivered:bool, via:"hold"\|"push"\|"poll"\|"focus"\|"none", reason?}` after delivery or 4 s. An **allow** on a `permission`/`elicitation` card is a guarded operation (§4.2): trusted principal or GUI confirmation. On a type `r` card `choice` is a next step → dispatched as `op.order{text:label}` to the owner by `push`/`poll` **only**, then `card clear` |
| `op.order` | `{agent, text, ctx:"talk", source:"ptt"\|"gui"\|"api"}` | Route: owner `push`/`poll` → injector chain (trusted principals only, and only for operator-originated text; refused with `-32006 undeliverable{reason:"dialog_open"}` while the agent has a permission/question card `pending`/`answering`/`snoozed`/`degraded`/`failed` or `claude agents --json` reports `waitingFor`) → `-32006`; returns `{via}`; the exact string that will be typed is written to the order history (`order.dispatched{text}`) **before** it is typed |
| `op.focus` | `{agent}` | Focus any agent's window (`desktop`), then `focus` to the device (with `ack` only when triggered by a device `channel`); an owner focusing its **own** agent uses `agent.focus` under `agent` (§4.6) |
| `op.snooze` / `op.ack` | `{agent, card}` | `op.snooze` mirrors the device `snooze` (releases a held hook); `op.ack` is **GUI-only**: marks a Ready card acknowledged in the store and sends `card clear`. ENTER-with-no-step on the deck is device-local (`src/ui/state/agents.rs:352-355` sets `acknowledged` and sends nothing), so no `ack` ever originates from the device |
| `focus.report` | `{agent\|null, source}` | A PC-side tracker (v1.1 Ghostty poller, Linux Hyprland) reports the focused agent → device `focus` without `ack` |

### 4.8 Delivery contract (`deliver` on every card)

| `deliver` | Mechanism | `card clear` sent when | If nothing can deliver |
|---|---|---|---|
| `hold` | The adapter's HTTP/UDS hook request is parked on a `HoldHandle` in `core.holds[(agent, card)]`; the answer becomes the response | the parked response has been written (`HoldWritten`, ≈ms); for a group, intermediate cards are cleared on answer receipt (§4.6) | hold expiry, snooze, or the hook client going away (`HoldGone` from the drop guard: Claude's own timeout, Ctrl-C) → the hook is released with no decision and the **same card id is re-pushed** as a `focus` card (text prefixed `⌨ `, choices `["Focus terminal"]`); a same-id re-push updates content only — no new chime, drawer/snoozed state kept (firmware `model.rs:614-622`) |
| `push` | `event agent.answer` on the owning **session's** WS/UDS connection + that session's mailbox | owner calls `agent.card.clear` (≤4 s) | owning session closed → `focus` if the agent has an adapter-resolved terminal, else `none` |
| `poll` | The next `agent.wait` from the owning session returns it (session mailbox, `mailbox_ttl_s = 600`) | when the `agent.wait` response is written | no waiter within 4 s → `failed` (no clear; the device reverts and the operator can answer again). The answer **stays in the mailbox**, and on a late pickup the daemon sends `card clear` and moves the card `failed → delivered` (the same 4 s rule, counted from the pickup), so store and device agree again. A mailbox entry is dropped when its card is answered again, cleared, replaced or expired, and delivery is deduplicated by `(card, choice|text)` — a late waiter and a second device answer can never both fire for one decision |
| `focus` | ENTER on the card runs `op.focus` (activate the terminal, §5.5); used by degraded holds and by informational cards that point at a terminal dialog (Gemini `ToolPermission`, Claude `permission_prompt`) | when the activation returns (well inside 4 s; on failure still cleared — the operator can retry from the channel view) | never fails to clear; after the clear the card is **retired on the device** (not re-sent under that id: after a clear the firmware treats the same `(agent,id)` as new and chimes again, `model.rs:593-611`) while the store keeps it `degraded{focused_at}` for the GUI until the hook flow ends |
| `none` | Informational, **no choices** — the device cannot send `answer` for a card without choices (`agents.rs:213` rejects `choice >= choice_count`) | n/a | — |

`inject` is not a card delivery mode: it is one route for `op.order` (§5.5), reserved for operator-originated text from trusted principals; a card choice is never injected.

Card status machine: `pending → answering{choice|text} → delivered → cleared`, or `answering → failed{reason}` (no `clear`; the device reverts by itself after 5 s, so both sides show the card pending again), `pending → snoozed → degraded` (device CANCEL / `op.snooze` releases the held hook and re-pushes the same id as a `focus` card, so a later ENTER from the drawer focuses the terminal instead of failing), `pending → degraded` (hold expiry or `HoldGone`), `degraded → degraded{focused_at}` (ENTER → `op.focus` → `card clear`, card retired on the device), and any of these `→ cleared` when the owner moves on (`PostToolUse`/`Stop`/`SessionEnd`) or, for a Ready card, on `UserPromptSubmit`, the next state change, or `[cards] ready_ttl_s = 900`. Out of `failed`: `failed → answering` (GUI **Retry**, or the device answering again) only for `push`/`poll` cards whose owning session is live; `failed → delivered` (late `poll` pickup, above); `failed → degraded` (GUI **Release to terminal**: the `⌨` card re-pushed under the same id); for a `hold` card the parked response is gone after a failure (Claude timed out or closed), so Retry is refused with `-32005 bad_state{status:"failed", reason:"hold_gone"}` and only Release is offered.

### 4.9 Voice methods

| Method | Params | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `voice.say` | `{text, agent?, lane:"question"\|"ready"\|"chatter"\|"chime", voice?, interrupt?:bool, ttl_s?:30}` | `{utterance}`; `chatter` is dropped if not started within `ttl_s`; `interrupt` cannot pre-empt a higher lane |
| `voice.stop` | `{utterance?\|lane?\|all:true}` | Barge-in path used by `ptt act:1` |
| `voice.listen` | `{agent, ctx:"talk"\|"answer", card?, max_s:60, input:"deck"\|"mac"}` | GUI/API-initiated PTT: the same pipeline as the deck's edges (`input:"mac"` uses the Mac's default mic), but the resulting `op.answer`/`op.order` carries the **caller's** principal and session, never `device`. `ctx:"answer"` on a card with `origin.kind ∈ {permission, elicitation}` is therefore a guarded operation (§4.2): an untrusted caller gets `-32002 {need:"trusted"}` **before capture starts** and `op.confirm_requested` fires; only a real deck `ptt` edge is principal `device`. This closes the loop in which an agent holding the `mcp` token plays "yes" with `voice.say` and listens to its own permission card. A capture that is **already open** belongs to the session that started it: `-32004 conflict {owner:{principal, session}}` rather than taking the microphone off it. The deck's own press has no session at all, so it can never be listened over from the API — pressing again is how the operator restarts one |
| `voice.cancel` | `{}` | Equivalent of `ptt abort:1`, **for the session that started the capture and no other**: ownership is per session and never per principal (§4.2), so any other caller gets `-32004 conflict {owner:{principal, session}}` and a deck press — which has no session — can never be cancelled from the API. It also ends a session that has been released and is still waiting for its words, with `voice.state{state:"aborted", reason:"cancel"}`. Idempotent when nothing is listening |
| `voice.engines` / `voice.get` | `{}` | `{stt:[{id,locales,on_device}], tts:[{id,locales,on_device}], selected:{stt,tts,locale}}` / `VoiceState`. **Built week 6.** Answered from a cache, never from the run loop: reading either engine is a main-thread hop (`SFSpeechRecognizer.supportedLocales`, `AVSpeechSynthesisVoice.speechVoices`) and the core thread carries every agent, hook and device line, so `audio::spawn` **posts** the read once when the engines come up and the answer arrives as a `FromAudio::Engines` like any other report. The TTS half reports `locales` and not the `voices` first published here — that is what an `EngineInfo` carries; the per-agent voice **pool** is `[voice.voices] default_pool` in `config.get` and the assignments are `state.json.voices`, which is where the picker already reads them. `-32013 degraded{reason:"voice_unavailable", why}` with no engine (`--no-device`, a build without one, or before the first report lands), and `why` says which |
| `voice.test` | `{what:"speaker"\|"mic"\|"stt"\|"tts"}` (default `speaker`) | Setup helpers, and **only `speaker` makes a sound**. It queues §7.3's `ready` chime — 110 ms, peak 0.2, the ordinary lane, the deck's own output UID, the operator's own `set speaker` gain — and reports `{played, route, output_uid, input_uid, listening, speaking, permissions}`: `played` is "the audio thread took it", because a chime carries no utterance id and so raises no `voice.utterance` to wait for; the `voice.route` events that follow are the route it took. `mic`, `stt` and `tts` are **reports, not exercises**, and say so in `limits` — a capture has an owner, a 60 s cap and a TCC grant behind it (§7.4) and a diagnostic may not open one, and driving an engine is the main-thread hop the core may not take. Never touches the system default device or the system volume |

### 4.10 Device methods

| Method | Params | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `device.get` | `{}` | `DeviceState` (§4.13) |
| `device.set` | `{k:"speaker"\|"mic"\|"announce"\|"chime"\|"autojump"\|"brightness"\|"idlehome"\|"demo", v}` | Host-side keys persisted in `state.json` and applied by the daemon (gain, announce policy); every key forwarded as `set` (P:124), never echoed |
| `device.faces.set` | `{list:[{f,t,n}]}` | Sent as `faces`; result is `{sent:true}` only — the device silently ignores it while the manager/gallery/naming overlay is open (P:122, G:181-182) and the host cannot know; the next device `faces` event is the truth |
| `device.usage.set` | `{period, items:[{n,p,r,c?}]}` | Any `device` principal may feed the Usage face; the daemon fills it itself from its feeders (`deckd::usage`, 2026-10-10): the Claude account's `/api/oauth/usage` windows, probed with the Claude Code login every `[usage] poll_s`; the status line's `rate_limits` when one is chained (`host-research/critic.md:23`); and the Codex app-server's `account/rateLimits/*`. Only the providers `[usage] providers` names are probed and drawn: `["codex"]` by default since the companion app, because the Claude probe reads the Claude Code login (from the Keychain on a Mac, which asks) and sends it to `api.anthropic.com`, so Claude is the owner's to switch on in the Usage panel. One bar per window (`Claude session`, `Claude week`, `Codex session`, …) with the countdown to its reset, re-rendered once a minute; `period` is `[device] usage_period`. ENTER on the face (`usage.refresh_requested`) re-probes the providers it names |
| `device.macro.set` | `{f, layout, keys?:[{l,b?,action?}]}` | Binds face id `f` (a Macropad instance in the current strip) to the layout named `layout` (`[macropad."<name>"]` in `config.toml`, §9) and sends `macro`; the binding `f → layout` is persisted in `state.json.macropad_bindings`. Up to three instances get device-chosen ids (G:178-179) and user-spoken names, so a layout is first matched by name against `faces[].n`, then by this binding; `action` stays host-side |
| `device.ws.set` | `{active,count,sel,windows:[{c?,t}]}` | Omarchy face content from a tracker/third party (Linux Hyprland adapter, or a script); the daemon enforces `active <= count` (the firmware rejects it with `range`, `model.rs:643-650`) |
| `device.name` | `{text}` | Answer to `ptt ctx:name` (≤16 B, P:123); the voice pipeline calls this internally |
| `device.raw` | `{line}` | v1.1. `admin`, debug: bypasses coalescing, still paced and byte-checked; logged **redacted** (`m`, key, byte length, blake3 prefix) unless `[privacy] log_content` |

### 4.11 Hook, admin and service methods

`hooks.claude` / `hooks.codex` / `hooks.gemini` / `hooks.codex_notify` / `hooks.claude_statusline` — `{payload, env?, tty?, cli_pid?, pid_kind?}` in (`pid_kind` is what the helper's parent walk resolved — `"cli"`, `"shell"` or `"unknown"` — and only a `cli` pid is ever polled, §5.1), the CLI's expected hook output JSON out (held for the question events; for every report-only event the handler **writes `{}` (2xx) first and then enqueues the Command**, so the CLI never waits on the core actor); over HTTP the same handlers are `POST /v1/hooks/{claude|codex|gemini}` with the raw hook JSON as body (so a `curl` command hook works without the helper). These are the one method family the tokio side answers itself, never as a call into the core actor: a held hook parked in the reducer would stop every agent, every device line and every other request for the length of the hold, and `[api] max_held_hooks` (64) is what bounds the parked ones — past it a hook is refused `-32008` and fails open. `config.get` / `config.set {patch}` (validated whole-file, written with `toml_edit` preserving comments) / `config.reload`. **Built week 6.** `patch` is `{"<dotted key>": <value>}` in the names `config.get` publishes, and a `null` value removes its key so the documented default applies again. **The keys it will write are managed keys, and that is a whitelist** (`core::dispatch::MANAGED`): `api.port`, `hooks.enabled` and the `[voice]` preferences, spelled out one by one, and **nothing else** — `config.toml` is where the trust boundary is declared, and a caller that could write `[trust] require_trusted_for_allow`, `[trust] gui_requirement`/`dev_requirement`, `[desktop] inject`, `[api] allowed_origins`/`uds_*_scope`, `[privacy] *` or `[voice.stt] allow_server` would be granting itself trust (or moving the operator's speech off the machine) rather than changing a setting. A list of *forbidden* keys would fail open the next time `[trust]` grew a field, so the direction is fixed: unlisted is refused. **A patch is all-or-nothing**: every key is applied to one in-memory copy, the **whole resulting file** is validated as a `Config` — keys that are each plausible can still be invalid together — every key is checked to have taken with nothing outside the patch moving, and only then is the file published, through a temp file plus a rename. So one bad key writes none of them, the bytes on disk never move on a refusal, not even transiently, and §3.7's watcher can never read half a config. `toml_edit` changes only the named keys and hands back every other key, the order, the blank lines and the operator's comments byte for byte. It **does not apply the change itself** — that is §3.7's notify + `ArcSwap` path, the one route a config has ever taken into the running daemon, so the reply describes the write (`{keys, changed, applied, restart_needed}`) and `config.changed{keys}` follows one debounce later exactly as it does for an editor's save; `token.create {name,scopes,expires?}` (plaintext returned once, never stored) / `token.list` / `token.revoke {name}` / `token.rotate {name}` (rewrites the managed blocks that embed it); `integrations.status` → `[{target, detected:{path,version}, status:"installed"|"outdated"|"absent"|"conflict"|"hooks_conflict"|"path_drift"|"token_mismatch"|"hooks_not_firing"|"paused"|"untrusted"|"modified", files, backups, last_event_at, detail?, limits}]` — the last two are Codex's trust gate (§5.2): a hook that is written but not reviewed **does not run**, and neither does one whose command changed after it was (measured 2026-09-20) — `integrations.install {target, trust?, dry_run?}` / `uninstall/repair/pause {target:"claude"|"codex"|"gemini"|"claude-desktop"|"icon-sync"}` (`trust` is the Codex-only opt-in of §5.2, `dry_run` prints "What this writes" and changes nothing); `perm.status` → `{microphone, speech, bluetooth, automation:{ghostty}, accessibility}`, `perm.request {which}` (the daemon triggers the prompt so the grant lands on the bundle; see §10.4 for the GUI fallback); `service.info` / `service.restart` (exit 75) / `service.stop` (exit 0), both `admin`, **built week 6**: `main.rs` was already `std::process::exit(deckd::cli::main())` and what was missing was a way for the core to name the code, which is `service::Exit` — one cell the core writes and the supervision loop on `main` reads. It carries a **deadline**, not a flag: the reply is written by a door after `Core::call` returns, so a supervisor acting the instant the core decided would race the one answer the call exists to produce (`Served::shutdown` breaks each connection loop on its stop watch, `biased`, before a pending line is flushed) — and a client that never receives its reply cannot tell "restarted" from "crashed". So the code is readable at once, for the reply (`{action, exit, pid, in_ms}`), and the supervisor may not see it for `Exit::GRACE` (250 ms). The first request wins and the reply says which. The plist `tools/install-host.sh` writes is `KeepAlive = {SuccessfulExit: false}` for exactly these two numbers — it was `true`, which restarts on **every** exit and made both codes meaningless; `log.tail {lines, level, follow}`; `metrics.get` → pacer stats, outbox depth per lane, err counters, hook latencies, SCO/A2DP timings; `diag.bundle {}` → `{path}`.

### 4.12 Events

| Type | Data | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `agent.added` / `agent.changed` / `agent.removed` | `{agent:{…}}` / `{agent, fields:{…}}` / `{agent, reason}` | `changed` carries only changed fields |
| `agent.slot` | `{agent, slot}` | Assignment / eviction |
| `card.set` / `card.answered` / `card.delivered` / `card.failed` / `card.snoozed` / `card.degraded` / `card.focused` / `card.acked` / `card.expired` / `card.cleared` / `card.paged` | `{agent, card:{…}}` / `{agent, card, choice?, label?, text?, via, principal}` / … | Owners see `card.answered` for their agents regardless of scope; `card.acked` comes only from the GUI's `op.ack`; `card.expired` is the Ready-card TTL / next-state expiry (§4.8) |
| `agent.answer` / `agent.order` | `{agent, card?, choice?, label?, text?, source, principal, session?, utterance?}` | Delivered **only** to the owning session — its WS/UDS connection, or the logical HTTP session's mailbox for `agent.wait` (plus `operate` observers as `card.answered` / `order.dispatched`); `principal`/`session` name who produced the decision (`device` for a deck edge, the caller of `voice.listen`/`op.answer` otherwise) |
| `order.dispatched` / `order.failed` | `{agent, via, text}` / `{agent, reason, text_kept:true}` | `text` is the exact string typed or pushed (content; the GUI order history shows it before an `inject` is executed) |
| `op.confirm_requested` | `{agent, card?, order?, principal, need:"trusted"}` | A guarded operation from an untrusted principal (§4.2); the GUI shows the confirmation sheet |
| `reply.set` | `{agent, text}` | Content |
| `transcript.partial` / `transcript.final` | `{agent, ctx, text, utterance, intent?}` | Opt-in; `intent` ∈ `allow`/`deny`/`choice:n`/`report`/`stop` when the grammar matched |
| `voice.state` / `voice.route` / `voice.utterance` / `voice.level` | `{state, agent?, ctx?, card?, principal, session?, utterance?, empty?, reason?, error?, message?}` / `{route, output_uid?, input_uid?, stuck?, transport?}` / `{utterance, lane, phase:"queued"|"started"|"ended"|"cut"|"dropped"}` / RMS | `level` opt-in. `state` ∈ `arming` / `listening` / `finalizing` / `aborted` / `ended`; **`{state:"ended", empty:bool}` is the end of a PTT session** — the edge §7.4 calls the session's end, carried here rather than as a `ptt.ended` kind of its own, because a second kind for the same edge would have to be subscribed separately and every client watching `voice.*` would miss it. `empty` says whether anything was said; an abort ends on `aborted` and never reaches `ended` |
| `device.link` / `device.session` / `device.face` / `device.faces` / `device.set` | `{link, sid, fw, boot, host_online}` / `{session}` / `{f}` / `{list}` / `{k,v}` | Mirrors of device events after persistence |
| `device.key` / `device.macro` / `device.ws` / `device.refresh` / `device.raw` | per-edge | Opt-in; how a script binds a Macropad face or an Omarchy remote |
| `focus.changed` | `{agent, source:"device"\|"desktop"\|"api"}` | |
| `usage.refresh_requested` / `usage.updated` | `{}` / `{usage}` | From device `refresh` (P:139); feeders re-push |
| `config.changed` / `integration.changed` / `perm.changed` / `service.degraded` / `service.shutdown` | diffs | |
| `hook.raw` | `{cli, event, payload_summary}` | `observe:raw` only |
| `error` | `{code, message, context}` | e.g. device `err why:range` with the offending line's `m` |

### 4.13 JSON shapes

```jsonc
// Agent (observe:content)
{"id":"ag_7hq2v3","slot":1,"kind":"claude","name":"cohdl-api","state":"question","title":"Run migrations on staging?","title_source":"prompt",
 "since":"2026-09-18T19:40:03Z","source":"hook","owner":{"principal":"adapter:claude","session":"sess_adapter_claude","lease_expires":"2026-09-18T19:43:03Z"},
 "session":{"key":"claude:1f3c…","cwd":"/Users/tony/projects/cohdl","pid":48213,"pid_kind":"cli",
            "terminal":{"program":"ghostty","tty":"/dev/ttys004","title_stamp":"deck:1f3c"}},
 "caps":{"answer":"hold","order":"inject","focus":true},          // hold/inject: adapter-only values (§4.6)
 "card":{"id":17,"type":"q","status":"pending","deliver":"hold","origin":{"kind":"permission","tool":"Bash","request_id":"toolu_9","group":null},
         "text":"Bash: psql -f migrate.sql staging","choices":[{"label":"Allow"},{"label":"Allow always"},{"label":"Deny"}],
         "rec":0,"free_text":true,"page":{"index":0,"count":1},"created":"…","expires":"2026-09-18T19:42:03Z"},
 "reply":{"text":"Tests fail on main. Run pending migrations on staging first?","at":"…","announce":null},
 "voice":"com.apple.voice.enhanced.en-US.Samantha","pinned":false,"meta":{}}

// Same agent for an observe-only principal (prompt-derived title is content → null)

 "source":"hook","card":{"id":17,"type":"q","status":"pending","deliver":"hold","origin":{"kind":"permission"},"choices_n":3,"redacted":true},
 "reply":{"redacted":true,"at":"…"},"redacted":true}

// Snapshot
{"rev":1202,"agents":[…],"focus":{"agent":"ag_7hq2v3","seq":41,"source":"device"},
 "device":{"link":"connected","session":"live","sid":314159,"fw":"0.1.0","boot":0,"face":1,
           "faces":[{"f":1,"t":"agents","n":"Agents"},{"f":6,"t":"settings","n":"Settings"}],"host_online":true,

          "speaking":{"utterance":"ut_77","lane":"ready","agent":"ag_9k"},"stt":{"engine":"apple","locale":"en-US","on_device":true},
          "tts":{"engine":"apple"},"permissions":{"microphone":"granted","speech":"granted"}},
 "usage":{"period":"5h / 7d","items":[{"n":"Claude 5h","p":42,"r":"resets 21:00"}],"at":"…"},
```

### 4.14 REST mirror

`GET /v1/info` (no auth) · `GET /v1/state` · `GET /v1/agents` · `GET /v1/agents/{id}` · `GET /v1/cards` · `GET /v1/device` · `GET /v1/voice` · `GET /v1/events?since=<rev>&types=card.*` (SSE, `KeepAlive` 15 s, `id:` = rev) · `GET /v1/schema` · `POST /v1/rpc` (one JSON-RPC request or batch) · `POST /v1/hooks/{claude|codex|gemini}` · `POST /mcp` (v1.1) · `GET /v1/ws`. All of it on TCP only; the UDS has no REST mirror (§4.1).

One-liner for a script, after a one-time `export DECKD_TOKEN=$(deckd token create --name build-bot --scopes observe,voice)` kept by the user (nothing is written to disk by the daemon): `curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $DECKD_TOKEN" -d '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"voice.say","params":{"text":"build finished","lane":"chatter"}}' http://127.0.0.1:47201/v1/rpc`. A `curl`-only virtual agent passes `"client":{"name":"build-bot"}` in every call so all its requests land in one logical session (§4.2) and uses `caps:{answer:"poll"}`. Shell-first tooling over the UDS needs no token: `deckd events --kinds card.*` tails events as JSON lines (`observe`); `deckd ctl --scopes operate op.snooze '{"agent":"ag_7hq2v3","card":17}'` works, while `deckd ctl op.answer` with an allow on a permission card is refused (`-32002 need:"trusted"`) and hands off to the GUI sheet — by design (§4.2).

### 4.15 Virtual-agent contract (what a third party must do)

1. `session.hello{client:{name:"myapp"}, scopes:["agent"]}` (the reply carries your `session`) then `agent.register{kind:"other", name, key:"myapp:<stable id>", caps:{answer:"poll", order:"poll"}, lease_ttl_s:30}` → `{agent, slot, owner}`. Over WS/UDS keep the connection open (implicit heartbeat) and you may use `caps:"push"`; over plain HTTP (`POST /v1/rpc`) pass the same `client.name` on every call so they share one logical session, use `poll` (`push` → `-32009`) and call `agent.heartbeat` every < 30 s.
2. Report state with `agent.update{state,title}` (≤10/s), replies with `agent.reply{text, speak?}`, questions with `agent.card.set{type:"q", text, choices, deliver:"poll"}` and Ready cards with `agent.card.set{type:"r", text, choices:[next steps]}`; bring your own terminal forward with `agent.focus`.
3. Receive decisions with `agent.wait{agent, timeout_s}` (long-poll from the owning session) or, on a WS/UDS connection, as `agent.answer`/`agent.order` events (`caps:"push"`). After an answer, call `agent.card.clear{delivered:true}` within 4 s (for `poll` the daemon clears when the wait result is written; for `push` you must clear). If nobody was waiting the card shows `failed` on the deck until your next `agent.wait` picks the answer up, which clears it (§4.8). A snooze arrives as `{kind:"snooze"}`; a chosen Ready step arrives as `{kind:"order", text:<label>, choice:n}` — exactly the label the operator saw, only ever by `push`/`poll` (never typed into a terminal).
4. Orders (PTT `talk`, GUI text) for your agent arrive the same way; answer with nothing (the daemon already sent `order.dispatched{via:"poll"}` when your wait returned).
5. Release with `agent.release` when done; if your session closes or you stop heartbeating, the agent is orphaned for `grace_s = 300` (drawn `idle` "(disconnected)"), then removed. `agent.claim{key}` from a new session takes it back — the only takeover path; a second session registering the same key while the lease is live gets `-32004 conflict{owner}`, whoever it runs as.

Virtual-agent lifecycle: `(none) —register(key)→ LIVE{owner:{principal,session}} —lease expires / session closes→ ORPHANED —claim(key)→ LIVE` / `—grace_s or slot pressure→ removed` / `LIVE —release / SessionEnd→ removed`. Rules live in `deck-model` and are unit-tested with a simulated clock.

Slot policy (projection, never identity): `slot = [agents] pin > state.json memory by key > memory by (kind, cwd) (a re-opened repo keeps its channel number) > lowest free`. When all 8 are taken, unslot the longest-idle agent in state `idle` (never one with a pending card, never `question`/`working`/`ready`); it stays in the API and GUI as `slot:null` ("not on deck"); when a slot frees, the most recently active unslotted agent takes it. Slots 1–4 are the Hyprland binds (`host-research/spec.md:104`) and pins default there.

### 4.16 MCP tool surface (`deckd::adapters::mcp`, v1.1)

Designed now so that the v1 API needs no change when it lands; built in v1.1 (D18). One rmcp server, two transports: in-process `POST /mcp` (streamable HTTP) and `deckd mcp --stdio` for Claude Desktop, registered by **one mechanism only**: the `claude_desktop_config.json` entry `openwalkietalkie-deck` with `command` = the absolute bundle path `/Applications/OpenWalkieTalkie.app/Contents/MacOS/deckd` and `args ["mcp","--stdio"]`, tracked by `integrations.status` (`path_drift`) exactly like the CLI hooks. No `.mcpb` of `server.type:"binary"`: it would extract a second, full `deckd` into Claude's Extensions directory, outside the notarised bundle, not updated by Sparkle and invisible to `path_drift` (`host-research/rust-service-stack.md:82` describes the format; we do not ship one). The server is itself an API client with `agent`+`voice`, one logical session per identity below. Tool results are plain text so every client renders them.

**Identity.** Modern-era clients (MCP 2026-07-28, what rmcp 3.4 serves by default) have no `initialize`, no `clientInfo` and no `Mcp-Session-Id` — every POST is independent (`host-research/rust-service-stack.md:59,63`) — so the virtual agent cannot be keyed on a session the client never establishes. The key is `mcp:<token name>:<id>` where `id` = `_meta.session_id` when the client supplies one, else the legacy `Mcp-Session-Id` for a 2025-11-25 client that ran `initialize`, else `"default"`. A modern client that supplies nothing therefore gets **one virtual agent per token** whose lease is refreshed by every tool call, and `deck_wait` has a stable identity across calls; a modern client that wants one row per session passes `_meta.session_id` (documented in the `deck-operator` prompt). `kind` comes from `clientInfo.name` on legacy sessions (`claude-code`→claude, `codex`→codex, `gemini`→gemini, `Claude`→claude with `meta.self_reported:true`) and otherwise from the token's `--client` label set at creation (`deckd token create --name codex-mcp --client codex`), else `other`. A Claude Code session that has both hooks and this MCP server is deduplicated by `session_id` when the client passes it in `_meta`, else the MCP agent is a separate row badged `mcp`.

| Tool | Input | Behaviour | Returns |
|---|---|---|---|
| `deck_report` | `{state:"working"\|"ready"\|"error"\|"idle", title?, name?}` | `agent.update` | `"ok"` |
| `deck_ask` | `{question, choices?:[string] (any count; paged on the deck once paging ships, truncated to 4 before), recommended?:n, allow_free_text?:true, timeout_s?:540}` | `agent.card.set type:q deliver:"poll"` then `agent.wait`; rmcp progress notifications every 15 s keep the client alive | `"choice: <label>"`, `"text: <transcript>"`, `"snoozed"` or `"timeout"` |
| `deck_say` | `{text, lane?:"ready"\|"chatter", interrupt?:false}` | `voice.say` in the agent's voice + `agent.reply` (Channel view shows it) | `"ok"` / `"muted"` |
| `deck_done` | `{summary, next_steps?:[string] (≤4, each ≤32 B — the label *is* the step)}` | `agent.update state:ready`, `agent.reply summary`, `agent.card.set type:r choices:next_steps`, announce per policy; a step chosen on the deck comes back as an order (`text` = the label) on the next `deck_wait`, never through terminal injection | `"ok"` |
| `deck_wait` | `{timeout_s:540}` | `agent.wait`: how a self-reporting agent receives an unsolicited PTT order or a chosen next step | the order text, or `"none"` |
| `deck_list` | `{}` | `agent.list` reduced to **name/kind/state/slot only** (no titles, no card text: every MCP-connected model would otherwise see other sessions' prompt heads) | text table |
| `deck_focus` | `{}` | `agent.focus` on its own agent (under `agent`; the shim is ancestry-capped and the `mcp` token never holds `operate`, so `op.focus` would always be `-32002`) | `"ok"` |

Resource `deck://agents` (JSON, redacted). Prompt `deck-operator`: a pasteable snippet ("call `deck_report` when you start a task, `deck_ask` instead of asking in chat when the operator may be away, `deck_done` when you finish, `deck_wait` when idle") for Claude Desktop Projects (`host-research/desktop-apps.md:54`). Claude Desktop Chat/Cowork rows show `self-reported`; a PTT `talk` to such an agent with no `deck_wait` pending opens `claude://claude.ai/new?q=<transcript>` (prefill only, `host-research/desktop-apps.md:9`) and reports `via:"deeplink"`. Claude channels (`claude/channel`, permission relay) are added to this same server in v1.1 behind the `deckd claude` wrapper.
## 5. Agent adapters

All adapters live in `deckd::adapters` and speak only the §4.6 primitives as owners (the adapter is the owner — principal `adapter:<cli>`, one internal session each — whatever door the hook came through: `token:hooks` for HTTP, the helper's `uid` on the UDS). They are **pure normalisers**; every effect (holds, timers, deliveries) is owned by the core (§3):

```rust
trait Adapter {
    /// Normalise one hook payload. Report-only events return the requests the handler enqueues after
    /// writing `{}`; a question event returns `Held` and the handler parks on the hold (below).
    fn ingest(&self, payload: HookPayload, ctx: HookCtx) -> Ingest;
    /// Pure: the hook output for an answer (or `HookOutput::Empty` for a release), formatted per CLI.
    fn answer_to_output(&self, card: &Card, ans: Option<&Answer>) -> HookOutput;
    fn deliver_order(&self, agent: &Agent, text: &str) -> BoxFuture<Result<Via>>;   // injector chain, §5.5
}
enum Ingest {
    Report(Vec<Request>),                            // `Command` is the reducer's own input enum; an adapter
                                                     // is an owner speaking §4.6, so it produces `Request`s
    Held { cmd: CardSet, group: Option<GroupPlan> }, // one card, or the first of a question group
    Nothing,                                         // dedupe hit, unknown event, suppressed Notification
}
```

The held-hook handler: `let (hold_tx, hold_rx) = oneshot(); let card = core.request(SetCardHeld{cmd, hold: hold_tx}).await?;` — the core allocates the card id, stores `holds[(agent, card)] = HoldHandle(hold_tx)` and answers with the id; the handler then awaits `hold_rx` inside a **drop guard** whose `Drop` sends `Command::HoldGone{agent, card}`. When the answer arrives the core executes `Effect::Deliver{deliver:hold}` by resolving `holds[(agent, card)]`; the handler writes `answer_to_output(..)` as the response and sends `HoldWritten{agent, card}`, which is the adapter's `agent.card.clear{delivered:true}`. `Effect::ReleaseHold{card, reason}` resolves the handle with `Empty` (`{}`). A hold is released **with no decision** by: device `snooze` / `op.snooze`, the hold timeout (`[hooks.claude|codex] hold_s`, default 120, always < the installed hook timeout; it bounds a whole question group), daemon shutdown, the agent going `gone`, and `HoldGone` — the hook client itself went away (Claude's own timeout, Ctrl-C, connection reset), which releases the hold and degrades the card **immediately** instead of letting both linger for `hold_s`. Releasing lets the CLI's own dialog appear (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:22`: "if no hook decides, normal terminal prompt continues"). In every release case the adapter **re-pushes the same card id** with the degraded content (`⌨ ` prefix, choices `["Focus terminal"]`, `deliver:"focus"`): a same-id re-push updates content only and keeps the snoozed/drawer state without a new chime (firmware `src/deck/model.rs:614-622`), so the Allow/Deny rows that would otherwise sit in the drawer (G:192-196) with no `HoldHandle` behind them are replaced by a choice that still works — ENTER from the drawer focuses the terminal where the real dialog now is. The card is `degraded` in the store until the next `PostToolUse`/`Stop`/`Notification` hook clears it. While such a card exists, `inject` orders to that agent are refused (§5.5).

`deckd hook <cli>` (Codex/notify/statusline and the Claude `SessionStart` command hook in v1; Gemini in v1.1): reads stdin (cap 16 MiB as openmicrokbd, `host-research/openmicrokbd.md:63`), captures the `env` subset (`TERM_PROGRAM`, `TMUX_PANE`, `ITERM_SESSION_ID`, `TERM_SESSION_ID`, `GHOSTTY_RESOURCES_DIR`, `GEMINI_SESSION_ID`, `GEMINI_CWD` — `ITERM_SESSION_ID`/`TERM_SESSION_ID` are worth capturing for iTerm and Apple Terminal, but a **Ghostty** surface's environment carries no session identifier of any kind (measured 2026-09-20), so on this Mac the parent walk plus the §5.5 beacon are the only bridge between the process and the window), then **walks its own parent chain** (`sysctl kinfo_proc` per pid, ≤8 hops) to the first process whose executable basename is `claude`, `codex`, `gemini` or `node` and reports that as `cli_pid` with `pid_kind:"cli"` and its tty (`ps -o tty= -p <cli_pid>`); if no such ancestor exists (an unexpected spawn shape) it reports `pid_kind:"unknown"` and the daemon does **no** liveness polling for that agent. The naive `$PPID` is wrong whenever a CLI runs a hook in shell form: the parent is then `sh -c`, which exits as soon as the hook returns, so a `kill(pid,0)` poll would fail within 2 s and the roster would flap (`cc_hooks.md:466-472`). It connects to the UDS with a 500 ms connect timeout, sends `session.hello{client:{name:"deckd-hook",version}, scopes:["hooks:ingest"]}` as line 1 — the helper is by construction agent-descended, and `hooks:ingest` is inside the ancestry cap for exactly this reason (§4.2), so the grant is logged as `deckd-hook` — calls `hooks.<cli>`, prints the returned JSON (`{}` when the CLI expects that), exits 0. Daemon down → prints `{}` and exits 0 (hooks are fail-open; the operator loses the deck for that prompt, never the terminal). Spawn forms per CLI: Claude `command` + `args` = exec form (no shell; the installer always writes `args`), Claude `command` alone = `sh -c`; **Codex `command` is a shell-quoted string, never an array** (§5.2's caution), so every Codex hook is shell form and the parent walk — not `$PPID` — is what finds `codex` (measured 2026-09-20; the design's earlier "the parent is normally `codex` itself" was wrong); Gemini `command` string = shell — the walk handles all of them and a fixture test covers both forms. `codex-notify` is the one variant whose payload is not stdin: Codex appends the JSON to the configured command, so it arrives as the **last argv entry**.

### 5.1 Claude Code (a plugin, registered at user scope; `settings.json` is never written)

**As built (2026-10-10), the Claude integration is a Claude Code plugin.** `integrations.install claude` writes a one-plugin marketplace under deckd's own data directory — `<data>/claude-marketplace/.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, `plugins/openwalkietalkie/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and `plugins/openwalkietalkie/hooks/hooks.json` — and registers it with Claude Code itself: `claude plugin marketplace add <dir> --scope user`, then `claude plugin install openwalkietalkie@openwalkietalkie --scope user` (both idempotent, measured against 2.1.296: a second `add` and a second `install` answer `ok` and change nothing). Claude records the marketplace (`plugins/known_marketplaces.json`, `extraKnownMarketplaces`) and the `enabledPlugins` flag in its own files; deckd puts nothing into `settings.json`. A local-path marketplace loads **in place**, so a daemon upgrade rewrites the plugin directory and the next session start (or `/reload-plugins`) picks the new hooks up. The same user-scope plugin is read by the terminal, the Desktop app's Code tab and the VS Code extension.

Every hook in the plugin is a **command hook** in exec form (`{"type":"command","command":"<deckd>","args":["hook","claude"],"timeout":<s>}`): `SessionStart` (5 s), `PermissionRequest` and `PreToolUse{AskUserQuestion}` (130 s, held), `UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop`, `StopFailure`, `Notification`, `PostToolUse`, `PostToolUseFailure`, `PermissionDenied`, `SubagentStart`, `SubagentStop` (2 s) and `SessionEnd` (1 s); `PostToolUse` sets a tool title only with `[hooks.claude] tool_titles`.

**Only an ask nobody else decided reaches the deck (2026-11-06).** Claude runs the `PermissionRequest` hook beside whatever else can decide the call — the terminal dialog, auto mode's classifier, a host app's approver — and the first decision wins, so a card raised on the hook put every approval on the deck (50 cards on 2026-11-06, none answered there). The door now parks a permission ask unseen for `[hooks.claude] settle_s` (default 6 s, when Claude itself sends `permission_prompt` for an unanswered dialog; `adapters::settle`): `Notification{permission_prompt}` sends it to the deck at once, and that call's own `PostToolUse` / `PostToolUseFailure` / `PermissionDenied` — matched by tool name and input digest, because the payload has no `tool_use_id` and tools run in parallel — or the session's `Stop`, `StopFailure`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `SessionStart`, `SessionEnd` or `idle_prompt` answers the hook `{}` with no card. A card on the deck goes the same way: a tool event takes off only its own call's card, and the session-wide events take off whatever is left (an interrupt sends no `Stop`, so the next prompt or the idle prompt is what clears it). A Bash ask also settles the moment its command starts: Claude runs every allowed Bash call as a fresh child shell of its own process (`zsh -c … eval '<command>'`), so a child of the hook's CLI pid whose arguments carry the asked command (`deckd::procs`, read-only `proc_listchildpids` + `KERN_PROCARGS2`) proves the call was allowed long before a long command's `PostToolUse`. Measured live under a Zeron-hosted session (`permission_mode` `default`, no `permission_prompt` ever sent): 5 of 5 asks settled with nothing on the deck, 2 of them by their command starting. A question group never waits. The Codex app-server client marks a thread working again on `item/autoApprovalReview/*` and on a tool item's `item/started` unless an approval of its own is on the deck. Because every event runs the helper, every event carries the helper's environment — the terminal identity of §5.5 **and the session's message inbox** (`CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_SOCKET`, with the per-session `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_TOKEN`) — so a session that started before the daemon gets both on its *next* hook rather than never. No `type:"http"` hook exists any more: no bearer in a settings file, no `allowedHttpHookUrls` switch, no chained `statusLine`, no `_openwalkietalkie` inventory, and C24a's fatal-settings predicate guards only the **migration**: an install that finds the pre-plugin entries in `settings.json` takes exactly those back out (`integrations::claude_legacy`, under the old ownership-by-fingerprint rules) and re-reads the result with Claude's own predicate. `integrations.status` reads Claude's own records and never runs the CLI; `claude plugin list --json` is the authority on what a session loads.

**Orders reach a Claude session through its inbox socket** (`deckd::inbox`), not through the terminal: two newline-terminated JSON lines, `{"type":"auth","token":…}` then `{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":…}}`, on a fresh connection to the socket the hook reported. Claude reads the message between tool calls during a turn and starts a new turn with it when idle; with the token the post counts as the session's own child and is delivered even under `bypassPermissions`. A peer message can never answer a permission prompt, and one arriving while a dialog is open has been reported to cancel it (anthropics/claude-code#93720), so guard 2 of §5.5 — no open dialog — runs in front of this route as a correctness rule. The token lives in `adapters::rt::Adapters` beside the agent and reaches no snapshot, event or log line. The Ghostty injector below is the fallback for a session with no inbox (an older CLI, bare mode).

The inbox is terminal-independent but Claude labels its messages as coming from another session, even with the hook token and `type:"user"`. That banner is a transport limitation, not something to hide by requiring Ghostty. A separate user-enabled integration such as [Claude Channels](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/channels) is needed for external user messages without peer attribution.

**The Claude Channels bridge is built, and it is the owner's opt-in (2026-11-11).** The switch is `[sources] claude_channels`, the Integrations page's Claude Channels row (`deckd::claude_channel`). On, the plugin carries a `.mcp.json` that declares the channel helper (`deckd claude-channel`, a stdio MCP server Claude starts per session), and the daemon serves the helpers on `<data>/claude-channel.sock`, a receive-only door that accepts only a helper running the daemon's own executable. A session must also opt in itself: custom channels are a Claude preview, so it is started as `claude --dangerously-load-development-channels plugin:openwalkietalkie@openwalkietalkie` (the page's How to Connect). An order goes through the channel only after a notification round trip has proved that session opted in, and otherwise through the inbox as above; a channel write whose outcome is uncertain is never repeated through the inbox. Left out of `config.toml`, the switch follows the installed plugin: one installed before the switch existed keeps its helper, and a new install has none. The door follows a change within a second, the plugin at the next `integrations.repair claude`, and a session at its next start.

*The paragraphs that follow describe the pre-plugin http-hook install, kept as the record of what was measured; the per-event table still applies, with every `Type` now `command`.* Installed by the pre-plugin `integrations.install claude`: `type:"http"` hooks to `http://127.0.0.1:47201/v1/hooks/claude` with `headers.Authorization: Bearer <hooks token>` written literally (the file already holds the user's private Claude config; `$VAR` interpolation was rejected because every shell that launches Claude would have to export it); `allowedHttpHookUrls` is **appended** to an existing list, and when the key is absent it is added only together with a visible line in "What this writes" — the key restricts *every* http hook at every settings level to the merged allowlist (`cc_hooks.md:282`), so adding it blindly would silently disable a user's or plugin's other http hooks (`hooks_conflict` is reported when one exists, and a list that is already there is appended to, never replaced — measured 2026-09-20); `timeout` is in **seconds**, not milliseconds — the binary multiplies by 1000 (measured 2026-09-20) — so `timeout: 130` on the held events (hold_s + 10; the installer refuses `hold_s > timeout - 10`); **`timeout: 2` and no `async`** on every report-only event — `async` exists only on `type:"command"` hooks (`cc_hooks.md:3665`, `host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:19`), so http hooks are synchronous and the daemon keeps them cheap by answering `{}` before doing any work (§4.11); operators who want true fire-and-forget can switch `[hooks.claude] report_via = "command"`, which installs exec-form `deckd hook claude --async` command hooks with `async: true` for those events instead; one `command` hook (`SessionStart`) in **exec form** (`command` + `args`, `cc_hooks.md:466-472`) because only command hooks inherit the env we need for terminal identity (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:10,119`) — and because it is not a preference: 2.1.276 **silently drops** every `type:"http"` handler on `SessionStart` and `Setup`, logging `Skipping HTTP hook … not supported` to the debug log and nothing else (measured 2026-09-20), so the installer refuses to write an http `SessionStart` at all; a chained `statusLine` `deckd hook claude-statusline -- <user's previous command>` that preserves the user's own line (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:31`) — `statusLine` has **no `args`** and runs in a shell (measured 2026-09-20), so it is the one entry that needs the user's previous command shell-quoted into a single passthrough word (§5.7, `shell_word`).

Three properties of the file itself shape what `integrations.status` may claim (all measured 2026-09-20). `~/.claude/settings.json` is the **lowest**-precedence settings file (`userSettings` < project < local < flags < policy): hook arrays merge, so ours run beside a project's, but `statusLine` is a scalar a project can override — status says so rather than claiming the chain is installed. Every response we write must carry `hookSpecificOutput.hookEventName` **equal to the event that fired**, or Claude throws the whole response away; every adapter response is fixture-tested for it. And an http hook is **not sent at all** when the process's proxy/TLS/resolver environment differs from the person's own — a silent "no hook" that looks exactly like a daemon that is down, which `integrations.status` cannot distinguish and therefore says so.

| Hook event (matcher) | Type | → primitives (state / question+choices / reply / identity) | Answer path |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SessionStart` (startup/resume/clear/compact) | command, exec form: `{"command":"/Applications/OpenWalkieTalkie.app/Contents/MacOS/deckd","args":["hook","claude"]}` | `agent.register{kind:claude, key:"claude:"+session_id, cwd, pid:<cli_pid from the parent walk>, terminal:{program, tty, …}}`; name = `session_name` if the statusLine has reported one else basename(cwd) (`title_source:"cwd"`); state `idle`; the hook output stamps the tab title with a one-shot `deck:<nonce>` beacon (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:30`) that deckd matches against Ghostty's live terminal titles and then **pins** (§5.5) | `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"SessionStart"},"terminalSequence":"\u001b]2;deck:1f3c\u0007"}` — `terminalSequence` is a **top-level** hook output field, not a `hookSpecificOutput` member, and must be a complete OSC 2 sequence `ESC ] 2 ; … BEL` (`cc_hooks.md:955,993-996`); anything else is silently dropped. The stamp is a **beacon, not durable state**: a Ghostty tab's `name` mirrors the live title and the next OSC 2 — the shell's prompt, Claude's own rewrite — wipes it (S3, measured 2026-09-20), so it is **not** re-emitted on `UserPromptSubmit`/`Stop`; deckd resolves the tab from the beacon within ~0.2 s and pins it instead (§5.5) |
