| `UserPromptSubmit` | http, `timeout: 2` | `agent.update{state:working, title: first 64 B of prompt unless session_name, title_source:"prompt"}`; prompt dropped afterwards (`keep_prompts=false`); expires a pending Ready card (`card.expired`, `card clear`) | `{}` — the beacon is never re-emitted (C25: the tab title is not ours to hold) |
| `PermissionRequest` | http **held**, `timeout: 130` | `card q`: text = `tool_name + ": " + summary(tool_input)` (Bash → command; Edit/Write → path; MCP → server/tool) ≤128 B; choices `Allow` / `Allow always` (only when `permission_suggestions` is non-empty, `host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:22`) / `Deny`; `rec:0`; `deliver:"hold"`; `origin.kind:"permission"`; state `question`. The event carries **no `tool_use_id`** (measured 2026-09-20), so the dedupe key is `(session_id, tool_name, blake3(tool_input))` inside a 5 s window. **Answered `{}` at once, no card,** when `tool_name == "AskUserQuestion"`, or when a question group for this `session_id` is in flight or was released in the last 30 s: `AskUserQuestion` also fires `PermissionRequest` (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:71`), so after a group is released with `{}` (snooze/expiry) this event would otherwise raise a second, permission-origin card on top of the degraded group card; both fixtures in §14. Holding this hook does **not** suppress the terminal dialog (S1), so the card is "first answer wins" and is cleared by the `PostToolUse`/`Stop` that says the operator answered in the terminal | `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PermissionRequest","decision":{"behavior":"allow"}}}`; Allow always → `allow` + `updatedPermissions: permission_suggestions`; Deny → `{"behavior":"deny","message":"Denied from the deck"}`; PTT free text → grammar (§5.6); released → `{}` and the same card id re-pushed as a `focus` card |
| `PreToolUse` (`AskUserQuestion`) | http **held**, `timeout: 130` | one `card q` per `questions[]` entry, issued **sequentially** (§4.6): text = `header + ": " + question`, `origin.group:{index,count}` (GUI shows `#17.1/2`; the GUI shows all questions at once); `questions` is schema-limited to 1–4 and `options` to **2–4** (measured 2026-09-20), so a Claude question card never needs the §5.6 truncation and `header`'s 12 characters are model guidance, not validation; `multiSelect` → single pick on the deck (comma-joined multi only from the GUI); `free_text:true`. Card *k*'s `answer` → immediate `card clear` for *k* → card *k+1* enqueued. `hold_s` (120 s) bounds the **whole group** (it is one held request): when it runs out the remaining cards are skipped and the group is released. Unlike the permission hook, holding this one **does** suppress the terminal dialog (S2), so the group owns the terminal for the whole hold and nothing is drawn there meanwhile | after **all** cards of the call are answered: `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecision":"allow","updatedInput":{"questions":<echo>,"answers":{"<question>":"<label or transcript>"}}}}` (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:23`) — `answers` is `Record<string,string>`, so a multi-select answer is a **comma-joined string**, never an array (measured 2026-09-20); only the last card's clear is tied to this response; a snooze on any card (or the group budget) releases the group with `{}` (remaining cards never issued, the snoozed one re-pushed degraded); the `PermissionRequest{tool_name:"AskUserQuestion"}` that follows a release is answered `{}` (row above) |
| `Elicitation` | — | v1.1 (`card q` with `message`; single-string schemas answered by voice) | `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"action":"accept","content":{…}}}` |
| `Stop` | http, `timeout: 2` | `agent.update{state:ready}`, `agent.reply{text:last_assistant_message (sanitised)}`, `card r` (no steps: no CLI provides them, `host-research/critic.md:9`); clears any card of this session still pending/snoozed/degraded (the turn ended, and a permission card is "first answer wins": this is how an answer given in the terminal takes it off the deck) | `{}` — the beacon is never re-emitted (C25) |
| `StopFailure` | http, `timeout: 2` | `state:error`, title = `error` enum (state-classed), reply = message | — |
| `Notification` (`idle_prompt`) | http, `timeout: 2` | `state:idle` | — |
| `Notification` (`permission_prompt`) | http, `timeout: 2` | Ignored if a permission card is pending or degraded (it fires ~6 s late, `host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:21`); else (sandbox network prompt, Desktop-hosted session) `card q deliver:"focus"` "needs permission (see terminal)", choices `["Focus terminal"]` | — |
| `Notification` (`agent_needs_input` / `agent_completed`) | http, `timeout: 2` | `question` (`deliver:"focus"`) / `ready` for Desktop Code-mode sessions (`host-research/desktop-apps.md:8`) | — |
| `PostToolUse` | command, `timeout: 2`, always | settles a waiting ask and clears that call's card; with `[hooks.claude] tool_titles = true` also `state:working`, title = tool summary (`title_source:"tool"`, content-classed). A tool storm produces many of these per second: adapter updates are never rate-limited (§11); core coalesces keep-latest, the outbox bucket sends ≤4/s per slot | — |
| `SessionEnd` | http, `timeout: 1` (Claude's own 1.5 s budget) | `agent.release` | — |
| `statusLine` (chained by hand, not by the plugin) | command | `agent.register` (a session that started before the daemon gets onto the roster from its first line), `agent.update{title:session_name, title_source:"session_name"}` once per name, and `rate_limits.five_hour/seven_day` into the Usage face (`deckd::usage`, `host-research/critic.md:71`); the Claude bars do not depend on it — the daemon probes the account's `/api/oauth/usage` itself — and neither source feeds the face unless `[usage] providers` names `claude`, the owner's opt-in since the companion app (§4.10) | — |

The three `Notification` rows above are the ones the adapter acts on; 2.1.276 carries **17 notification types where the docs list 12** (measured 2026-09-20), so anything else is **default-ignored** — an unknown type is never a panic, an `unwrap` or a card.

Not installed: `MessageDisplay` (streaming TTS is v2), `SubagentStop` (subagents are not channels). Liveness: `claude agents --json` every 5 s (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:12,143`) is the primary source — it reconciles pid/status, fills `name`, seeds sessions that started before the daemon, and removes sessions whose pid is gone. An **interactive** row carries `status` (`busy|waiting|idle`) and **no `state`/`waitingFor`** (measured 2026-09-20: `state` belongs to background rows), so the reconciliation keys off `status`, and `waitingFor` is read only when `status == "waiting"`. The call is a process spawn — 0.12–0.17 s with six sessions — so the 5 s poll stands but runs on `spawn_blocking`, never on a tokio worker. `kill(pid,0)` every 2 s runs **only** for agents whose `pid_kind` is `cli` (a pid the helper resolved to a `claude`/`codex`/`gemini`/`node` ancestor), never for `unknown` (no `SessionEnd` on SIGKILL, `host-research/critic.md:24`). Orders: v1 Ghostty injection (§5.5); v1.1 the channel relay via `deckd claude` (`--dangerously-load-development-channels server:deck`, `host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:34`). *As built, orders go to the session's inbox, or through the opt-in Claude Channels bridge above, with Ghostty injection as the last fallback.*

### 5.2 Codex CLI (the shared app-server daemon first; `~/.codex/hooks.json` as the fallback)

**As built (2026-10-10), the primary Codex integration is a client of the shared local app-server daemon** (`deckd::codexd`). From codex-cli 0.161 the TUI runs through one app-server daemon per machine (`codex --no-daemon` opts out; `codex agents` browses it), reachable on `$CODEX_HOME/app-server-control/app-server-control.sock` — a WebSocket speaking the app-server's JSON-RPC, which a plain client may join (`initialize{clientInfo, capabilities{experimentalApi}}`, measured 2026-10-10 against 0.161/0.162). deckd attaches on start and reconnects with backoff: `thread/loaded/list` + `thread/resume` are the roster — a thread per agent, keyed `codex:<thread id>`, the same key the hooks adapter uses, so a thread the client drives is one the hooks leave alone (`{}`); `turn/started`/`turn/completed`/`item/*` are the state, the title (the `userMessage` that opened the turn) and the last reply (`agentMessage`); the server's own requests — `item/commandExecution/requestApproval`, `item/fileChange/requestApproval` (`Allow` / `Allow for session` / `Deny` → `accept` / `acceptForSession` / `decline`), `item/permissions/requestApproval` (`grant`/`deny`) and `item/tool/requestUserInput` (one card per question, answered `{answers:{<id>:{answers:[…]}}}`) — are the cards; `turn/steer{expectedTurnId}` while a turn runs and `turn/start` when idle are the orders, a rejected steer falling back to the next `turn/start`. The agents are poll-capability agents owned by the Codex adapter's internal session (§4.6, §4.15), driven through `agent.wait` exactly as a third party's script would be — the one core change was letting an internal session's parked waiter receive a polled delivery. `serverRequest/resolved` (the TUI answered first) and a finished turn clear the card without answering. This covers sessions that started before deckd did, which the hooks never could, and needs nothing written into the user's files. `[codex] daemon = false` turns it off; `daemon_socket` overrides the path.

The hooks remain for `--no-daemon` sessions and older CLIs. `integrations.install codex` writes the `hooks.json` entries with `command: "'/Applications/OpenWalkieTalkie.app/Contents/MacOS/deckd' hook codex"` — a single shell-quoted **string**, see the caution below. **It no longer writes anything into `config.toml`** (2026-10-10): `tui.terminal_title` was a terminal-identity fallback the hook environment made redundant, and `notify` duplicates what the `Stop` hook already carries (`last_assistant_message`, `turn_id`, C17); an install removes both when an older deckd wrote them (recognised by the receipt and, for `notify`, by the binary it names) and otherwise leaves the file unwritten. It does **not** write `features.hooks`: the feature is `stable true` by default (measured 2026-09-20). The helper prints `{}` where Codex expects it (`host-research/openmicrokbd.md:14`).

> **Caution — a Codex `command` is a string, and an array voids the whole file.** One array-form `command` anywhere in `hooks.json` makes codex-cli 0.153.4 discard **every hook in the file, on every event** (measured 2026-09-20). It surfaces nowhere a user would look: `hooks/list` answers `"hooks": []` with the parse failure as a `warnings` string and `errors: []`, and the session says nothing at all. The installer writes one shell-quoted word built with `shell_word`, and the test that guards it asserts `hooks/list` comes back with a **non-empty** `hooks` array and `warnings: []` — asserting that the file merely parses as JSON would pass while every Codex hook on the machine is dead.

**The trust gate** (measured 2026-09-20, and the reason `integrations install codex` is not finished when the file is written). A freshly written user hook is `trustStatus:"untrusted"` and **does not run** until the user reviews it in `/hooks`, or its hash is in `[hooks.state]` of `config.toml`. The review appends exactly `[hooks.state."<REALPATH of hooks.json>:<event_snake>:<groupIdx>:<handlerIdx>"]` with a single `trusted_hash = "sha256:<64 hex>"` key — no `enabled`, which is the user's own pause switch. Three consequences for the installer: it is keyed by the **realpath**, so keying off `$CODEX_HOME` verbatim writes a hash that never matches under a temp dir or a symlinked home; it is **positional**, so deckd appends its group at the **end** of each event's array and never reorders one (inserting ahead of a user's group silently re-attributes their trust to our handler and theirs reads `untrusted`); and deckd **cannot compute the hash itself**, so `deckd integrations install codex --trust` first asks app-server `hooks/list` for the `currentHash` of its own key and writes that back — which makes `hooks/list` a hard dependency of `--trust`, not just of `status` (there is no `codex hooks list` subcommand in 0.153.4 and no RPC that writes trust). **By default the installer writes no trust at all**: it prints the exact `/hooks` review step, because forging the hash bypasses the review `/hooks` exists to provide. Any path, `timeout`, `matcher`, `statusMessage` or `additionalContextLimit` change re-breaks it as `modified` and the hooks stop firing silently — a deckd upgrade that moves the binary is a Codex-hooks outage until it is re-trusted, which `integrations.status` must surface — while the file-level `description` and an explicit `async: false` are hash-neutral, so the file can be re-emitted idempotently. On a fresh machine two gates stand before our hooks ever fire: the directory-trust prompt for the cwd, then the hooks-review modal.

| Event | → primitives | Answer path |
|---|---|---|
| `SessionStart` / `SessionEnd` | `agent.register{kind:codex, key:"codex:"+session_id, cwd, pid:<cli_pid from the helper's parent walk — the shell-form `command` means `$PPID` is `sh -c`, so the walk is what resolves `codex`>, terminal?}` / `agent.release` (SessionEnd only after 30 min idle → liveness relies on pid polling of the resolved `codex` pid). `SessionStart` fires on the session's **first turn**, not when the session opens (measured 2026-09-20), so a roster entry may lag the window; `session_id` is a **UUID**, not `thr_…`, and is never prefix-matched | — |
| `UserPromptSubmit` | `state:working`, title from prompt head | — |
| `PreToolUse` | title = tool + command head (bucketed) | — |
| `PermissionRequest` | `card q` (`tool_input.command`/`.description`), choices `Allow`/`Deny` only (hooks accept allow/deny, nothing else, `host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:163`), `deliver:"hold"` (the command hook blocks Codex the same way; hook `timeout` 130 **seconds**) | stdout `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PermissionRequest","decision":{"behavior":"allow"}}}` / `deny`+`message`; released → `{}`. 0.153.4 also accepts **exit 2 with a reason on stderr** as a deny (undocumented, read out of the binary, measured 2026-09-20); the JSON `decision` stays the primary path and a non-zero exit is never our failure mode — the helper always exits 0 |
| `Stop` / notify `agent-turn-complete` | `state:ready`, `reply last_assistant_message`; whichever arrives first wins, the other is a no-op (dedupe by `turn_id`) | — |
| `Interrupt` | `state:idle` | — |
| Choice questions | none via hooks (`requestUserInput` is app-server only) → v1.1 | — |
| Error | none via hooks → v1.1 (`turn/completed status=failed`) | — |
| v1.1 app-server | daemon-owned `codex app-server --listen unix://…/codex-app.sock`, `deckd codex` wrapper runs `codex --remote unix://…`; `requestApproval` → card with `Allow`/`Allow session`/`Deny`, `requestUserInput` → card with options (`isOther` → PTT), `turn/steer`/`turn/start` → orders, `item/agentMessage/delta` → streaming reply, `account/rateLimits` → usage (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:62`); gated on the two-subscriber routing spike (§16) | app-server responses |

The Codex Desktop 0.129 hooks regression (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:175`) is **stale** and nothing is built around it: this machine runs cli 0.153.4 and Desktop 26.915 (measured 2026-09-20). Nor can `hooks_not_firing` be decided by the absence of a `SessionStart`, since that event only arrives on the session's first turn (C18): `trustStatus`, `enabled` and `warnings` from app-server `hooks/list` are the one place all four trust states and the whole-file-discard case of the caution above are visible, and it needs no session at all. **As built, `integrations.status` asks it** whenever the call may run the CLIs, and maps the answer on: a deckd row codex calls `modified` is `modified`, a `warnings` entry or a `hooks/list` that names none of deckd's rows is `hooks_not_firing`, and `enabled: false` is `paused`. Reading the `[hooks.state]` table out of `config.toml` is what is left when codex cannot be asked — a call with `detect:false`, every test, a `codex` that is absent or will not answer. That fallback separates `untrusted` from trusted with no CLI in the loop but cannot see a `trusted_hash` that has gone stale (the hooks have stopped firing and the table still says `installed`) or the `warnings` string that means the whole `hooks.json` was discarded, so a row that fell back to it says so in its own `limits` rather than passing the guess off as a check. `install codex --trust` asks the same `hooks/list`, and cannot forge the hash.

### 5.3 Gemini CLI (`~/.gemini/settings.json`, command hooks; v1.1)

Designed here, built in v1.1 (D18): the CLI is being replaced by Antigravity and the `BeforeTool` bypass is unverified, so it is not worth a v1 week. The `deckd hook gemini` subcommand and the `hooks.gemini` route exist from v1 (they are the same code path as Codex) but the installer target is hidden until the adapter ships.

| Event | → primitives | Answer path |
|---|---|---|
| `SessionStart` / `SessionEnd` | register/release (`kind:gemini`, `key:"gemini:"+session_id`, env `GEMINI_SESSION_ID`/`GEMINI_CWD`) | — |
| `BeforeAgent` | `state:working`, title from `prompt` | — |
| `Notification` (`ToolPermission`) | `card q deliver:"focus"` (informational, `host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:84`): text = `message`, choices `["Focus terminal"]`; ENTER = `op.focus` then an honest `card clear` (§4.8); the card is retired on the device after that and cleared in the store by `AfterAgent` | terminal |
| `BeforeTool` (matcher `run_shell_command\|write_file\|replace`) | only with `[hooks.gemini] permission = "beforetool"` (default `"notify"`): `card q deliver:"hold"`, hook `timeout` 130000 ms | stdout `{"decision":"allow"}` / `{"decision":"deny","reason":…}` — interactive bypass **unverified** (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:8`); the spike runs with the v1.1 adapter and decides the default (Q6) — week 3 did not run it, since the adapter is not built |
| `AfterAgent` | `state:ready`, `reply prompt_response` | — |
| Title glyphs ◇/✋/✦ | v1.1 cross-check only (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:85`) | — |

Kept deliberately thin (~150 lines): Gemini CLI was replaced by Antigravity CLI for unpaid tiers on 2026-06-18 (`host-research/critic.md:23`); the same shape serves Antigravity if it keeps the hook contract.

### 5.4 Desktop apps

| App | v1 | Later |
|---|---|---|
| Claude Desktop, Code mode | Claude Code hooks fire identically (`host-research/desktop-apps.md:8`) — nothing to build | — |
| Claude Desktop, Chat/Cowork | v1.1: stdio shim (`deckd mcp --stdio` registered by bundle path in `claude_desktop_config.json`), tools of §4.16, `self-reported` badge; PTT with no `deck_wait`/`deck_ask` pending → `claude://claude.ai/new?q=` | AX injection into the current chat (v2, opt-in, Accessibility) |
| ChatGPT / Codex desktop | Out (`host-research/desktop-apps.md:56`); Codex threads inside it are not attachable | Secure MCP Tunnel wrapper around `deckd mcp --stdio` for Business/Enterprise users (v2) |

### 5.5 Terminal identity, focus and injection (macOS, `deckd::desktop`)

Identity is captured at `SessionStart` from the hook environment (never from window titles): `TERM_PROGRAM`, the resolved CLI pid from the helper's parent walk (§5) → tty, `TMUX_PANE`, `ITERM_SESSION_ID`, `TERM_SESSION_ID`, plus the title beacon `deck:<nonce>` written through `terminalSequence` (Claude) or the `thread-id` title item (Codex). A Ghostty surface's environment carries **no session identifier at all** (measured 2026-09-20), so `ITERM_SESSION_ID`/`TERM_SESSION_ID` are for iTerm and Apple Terminal and nothing here may depend on them. Ghostty is the only terminal on this Mac and exposes AppleScript since 1.3: `terminal` has `id`, a read-only `name` and `working directory`, and **no tty and no pid** (dictionary read 2026-09-22) — which is why identity must come from the hook environment. `window` and `tab` each also carry a stable `id` (`tab-group-<hex>` and `tab-<hex>`; a terminal's is a **UUID**), and the terminal UUID is what everything below targets. `application` has a direct `terminal` element, so `terminals of application "Ghostty"` enumerates flat — cheaper than `terminals of tabs of windows`.

**The three title strings** (measured 2026-09-22, and the correction that matters most here). `window.name`, `tab.name` and `terminal.name` are **not** one shared string. The OSC 2 beacon lands in `terminal.name`. `perform action "set_tab_title:…"` writes `tab.name`, which Ghostty documents as an **override** — and an override is precisely why it survives every later OSC 2. `window.name` mirrors whichever tab is focused. Proven by pinning a tab to `deck:AAAA` and then pushing `ESC ]2;HIJACKED_BY_OSC2 BEL` at its tty: `tab.name` stayed `deck:AAAA`, `terminal.name` became `HIJACKED_BY_OSC2`. Consequently the resolver reads **`terminal.name` to find a beacon** and **`tab.name` to re-find an already-pinned session**; a resolver that reads only one of them fails half the time, and nothing may ever resolve on `window.name`. Before any override exists the three coincide, which is what made them look like one string.

Resolver — **resolve then pin** (S3 2026-09-20; mechanism re-measured end to end 2026-09-22). It replaces the title-matching resolver, which cannot work because a tab's live title is a mirror of the terminal's and the next OSC 2 wipes an unpinned stamp. (1) `SessionStart` answers with `terminalSequence = ESC ]2; deck:<nonce> BEL`, where the payload of that OSC 2 — the whole string `deck:<nonce>`, prefix included — is exactly what lands in `terminal.name` and exactly what is recorded as `Terminal::title_stamp`. The prefix is written **once**, by the adapter; a reader that prepends it again searches for `deck:deck:<nonce>` and finds nothing, which is a defect this ambiguity actually produced. (2) Within ~0.2 s deckd enumerates the terminals and takes the one whose **`terminal.name`** carries the nonce — `working directory` is the *ambiguous* key here (every tab of this Mac is `/Users/tony`), the nonce is the discriminating one. (3) deckd immediately **pins** the tab with `perform action "set_tab_title:deck:<id4>" on <terminal>`, which holds against every later OSC 2; `set_surface_title` is **not** a substitute — it carries no override semantics. (4) Uninstall and session end pin the **empty** value back (`set_tab_title:`), which clears the override and restores the live title (measured). `state.json.terminal_map` keys on the **surface UUID** (`terminal.id`), never on a tab name: names are user-retitleable. Still ambiguous (the beacon lost a race with Claude's own title write) → a retry on the beacon, then `None` and the one-time GUI prompt "which tab is cohdl-api?", whose answer is persisted under the same `terminal.id`. Resolutions are cached for 30 s, and the cache is load-bearing: one `osascript` round trip costs **~118 ms** (5 runs: 129/118/119/118/118 ms), so a resolve plus a pin is ~240 ms. AppleScript `-1712` is **not** "no Ghostty": a long-idle instance refused 4/4 element queries with its main thread provably idle, and activating it fixed the session permanently, so `-1712` means "wake and retry" (activate, one retry) and is reported distinctly from the `-1743` Automation denial below. `new tab` / `new window with configuration` answer **`-1708` "Can't continue" yet still create the surface**, so nothing may depend on their return value — re-enumerate to learn the new `terminal.id`.

- **Focus out** (device `channel` → `op.focus`): `activate` the app, then `perform action "goto_tab:<index>" on <terminal>`, verified through `id of (selected tab of <window>)`; failures are silent on the device (the `focus{ack}` was already sent, §6.4) and visible in the GUI. Fallback: `NSWorkspace` activation by bundle id (`com.mitchellh.ghostty`), which can only raise the app. Three measured details (2026-09-22). `activate window <n>` is **not** the call — it answers `-1700 Can't make 1 into type specifier` on a bare index; plain `activate` raises the app. `select tab` **does** exist and works, but only with a parenthesised specifier (`select tab (tab 1 of window 1)`; a bare `select tab 1 of window 1` is a syntax error, `-1723`) — and it is still the wrong primitive here, because a `terminal` carries **no back-reference to its tab** (only `id`, `name`, `working directory`), so selecting by tab specifier from a resolved surface UUID would need the very enumeration `goto_tab:` avoids. And `selected of <tab>` is **per window**: two tabs in two windows both read `true` at once, so it can never answer "is this surface frontmost".
- **One instance only.** `tell application "Ghostty"` reaches exactly **one** process, and `open -na Ghostty.app` starts a new one per launch (measured 2026-09-22: with three running, the enumeration saw one instance's surfaces and answered `-1728` for a `terminal.id` belonging to another). A beacon in a second instance is therefore **invisible** to the resolver, which reports `ambiguous` rather than guessing — it must never type into a surface it cannot confirm.
- **Inject** (`op.order` resolving to `via:"inject"`): `input text "<text>" to <terminal>` then `send key "enter" to <terminal>`. Two measured corrections (2026-09-22): the parameter is **`to:`** (`perform action` takes `on:`), and the key is named **`enter`** — `send key "return"` is answered `-1700 Unknown key name: return` and would have failed every injection. Both `input text … to:` and `perform action … on:` **genuinely target the named surface**: with one tab focused, text aimed at another landed in the aimed tab and the focused one received nothing, so **injection never steals focus** and "never the wrong session" is mechanically achievable rather than merely intended. Text is sent literally, never through a shell; control characters are rejected (`-32009`); the exact string is written to the agent's GUI order history (`order.dispatched{text}`) **before** it is typed. Ambiguous → `order.failed{reason:"ambiguous"}`, never the wrong session. Three guards, all mandatory:
  1. **Provenance.** Only operator-originated text from a trusted principal (§4.2): a PTT transcript from a deck edge (`source:"ptt"`, principal `device`), a transcript from a `voice.listen` the trusted GUI started, or the GUI text box. Text that came from a card choice, an `agent.*` call or a token is never injected (card-step orders go `push`/`poll` only, §5.6); a script with `operate` that calls `op.order` on an `inject`-only agent gets `-32002 {need:"trusted"}` and the GUI confirmation path — **and so does a `voice.listen` aimed at one, in every `ctx`**, because a capture's transcript is dispatched by `Store::dispatch_order` straight into `op_order` and never passes back through `Request::OpOrder`: without that second arm an untrusted caller holding only `voice` could aim the operator's dictation at a terminal of its choosing while being refused the `operate` call that would do the same thing. The injector only ever targets a terminal the hook helper resolved for an adapter-owned agent — a virtual agent's self-supplied `terminal`/`pid` is informational and never used here (§4.6), so a script cannot aim dictation at a Ghostty tab of its choosing.
  2. **No open dialog.** Refused with `-32006 undeliverable{reason:"dialog_open"}` (order kept on the GUI with Copy) while the agent has any `permission`/`question`/`elicitation`-origin card in `pending`/`answering`/`snoozed`/`degraded`/**`failed`**, or while `claude agents --json` reports `waitingFor` for it. The statuses are stated as "every one that does not mean *the answer reached the CLI*", and `failed` is the one an earlier reading of this list left out: it is written by `hold_gone`, `undeliverable`, `timeout` and `link_down`, every one of which means the hook response was **not** written, so the CLI has fallen back to asking in the terminal — a `failed` permission card is the *strongest* evidence a dialog is up, not the weakest. A held `q` card carries no TTL, so the state persists until its owner clears or replaces the card. Only `delivered` and `cleared` do not block. The predicate is one function, `deck_model::Card::is_open_dialog`, called by the reducer and again by the core when it builds the injector job. While a hook is held the TUI queues typed keys and the dialog consumes them the moment the hook returns; after a release the Claude/Codex dialog is on screen with Allow highlighted, so `text + Return` would confirm it — an unattended approval triggered by a voice order.
  3. **Foreground check.** Immediately before `input text` **and** again immediately before `send key`, verify three things about the agent's `cli_pid`: it is alive, its process group **is** the foreground process group of its recorded tty, and the tty it is attached to **is** that recorded tty. On any mismatch send nothing, not even the text, and fail with `reason:"not_foreground"` — otherwise an order lands on a bare shell prompt after the CLI exits and Return executes it as a command. The mechanism is `proc_pidinfo(pid, PROC_PIDTBSDINFO)` → `libc::proc_bsdinfo`, comparing `pbi_pgid` with `e_tpgid` and `e_tdev` with the recorded tty's `st_rdev`; it needs no fork and reads the same three kernel fields `ps` prints its `+` flag from. `sysctl(KERN_PROC_PID)` would have been the obvious route but `libc 0.2.189` declares no `kinfo_proc`/`eproc`/`extern_proc` for any Apple target, so it would mean hand-rolling a union-bearing `#[repr(C)]` struct that goes silently wrong if one padding byte moves. One caveat: `proc_pidinfo` answers 0 bytes for a root-owned pid, which therefore reads as dead rather than as having no tty — it still refuses, but anything reusing this as a liveness probe must know. **`tcgetpgrp` is not usable here**: opening another session's `/dev/ttysNNN` from the daemon and calling it returns `ENOTTY` (measured 2026-09-22), so the design's earlier suggestion of it is withdrawn. The third part — comparing the tty — is not redundant: a recorded pid that *is* the foreground group of a *different* terminal passes a pgid-only test, and was refused only once the tty was compared. All four cases (foreground → allow; alive but not foreground → refuse; dead → refuse; foreground elsewhere → refuse) are proven against live ttys in the week-5 gate.
  Guards 2 and 3 are exercised in the week-5 hardware gate (§15). Two honest gaps. Guard 3 is re-checked immediately before the Return, but an `osascript` round trip costs ~118 ms, so a **~118 ms TOCTOU window** remains between the last check and the keystroke; it is documented, not closed, and shrinking it means in-process AppleScript instead of spawning `osascript`. **Guard 2 has a wider window of the same shape and it is not closed either**: the card half is a pure function of the snapshot, so it is evaluated on the core thread when the job is built, and the job then spends a resolve, a pin and two foreground checks (~240 ms and up) on `deckd-desktop`, which holds no snapshot. A `PreToolUse` permission card raised in that interval — which is the *ordinary* thing to happen while an order is in flight — is not seen: the store refuses the *next* order `dialog_open` while the one already queued is typed. Closing it needs the worker to re-read the card state immediately before `input text`, beside guard 3, which means handing it a shared snapshot handle. Until then, the job carries the card half as the core last saw it (`DesktopJob::Inject{dialog_open}`, re-checked by the worker) rather than a hardcoded `false`, so the field at least has a producer.
- **Focus in** (PC → device): v1.1 — `NSWorkspace.didActivateApplication` + a 500 ms `selected tab` poll while Ghostty is frontmost → `focus.report`. Linux gets it free from Hyprland.
- Needs Automation TCC on the daemon (`NSAppleEventsUsageDescription` + `com.apple.security.automation.apple-events`); denied (`-1743`) → the injector chain skips Ghostty and `integrations.status` shows it. On this machine Automation was **already granted** to the responsible process (a benign `version & (count of windows)` returned with no prompt, 2026-09-22), so neither `-1743` nor `-1712` could be reproduced live; both paths are implemented and unit-tested against injected errors, and their live behaviour is **not** claimed by week 5.
- Omarchy face on macOS (v1.1): a degraded `ws` (`active:0, count:0` — the firmware rejects `active > count` with `range`, `model.rs:643-650`; `windows` = up to 6 agent terminals `{c: state colour, t: "kind · name — title"}`, `sel` = active channel row); `ws win` focuses that agent; `ws go` and `key` are API events only (no public Spaces API, `host-research/critic.md:22`). Mission Control keystrokes (CGEvent, Accessibility) are a later opt-in. In v1 the daemon sends nothing for the face (the operator removes it in the face manager, Q13) and `ws`/`key` lines are API events only.
- Macropad on macOS (v1.1): `action = {shell|url|say|api}` per key; `keys` (synthesised keystrokes) later (Accessibility). In v1 `macro` edges are API events only (`device.macro`).

### 5.6 Card mapping rules (shared by every adapter and by virtual agents)

- `text` ≤128 B on a character boundary with `…`; sanitiser strips ANSI, collapses whitespace, replaces a code fence by `code: <first line>`; the GUI always shows the full text.
- Choices ≤4 on the device, labels ≤32 B (colliding cut labels get `#n`); the answer payload carries `{choice:n, label}` — the label as shown, nothing hidden; the full untruncated label is content the owner can look up in the store by `n`. The text rules and the label rules are one place, `deck_model::sanitize` (pure: `strip_ansi`, `fold_code_fences`, `collapse`, `cut`, `text`, `label`, `labels`), shared by every adapter and by any virtual agent that wants to look like one.
- **>4 options, v1: truncate.** Claude cannot reach this case — `AskUserQuestion` schema-enforces `questions` 1–4 and `options` **2–4** (measured 2026-09-20) — so truncation is for virtual agents today and for the Codex app-server in v1.1. The deck shows the first 4 options (the recommended one is always among them: it is moved to position 0, displacing the option that was there), the card records the shown→original mapping as `choice_map` so the owner can map an answer back to the option it sent (the answer's `choice` indexes the **shown** list, and two long options can cut to the same label), the GUI shows all, PTT free text answers any of them by name, and the event `card.truncated{shown:4, total:n}` plus `metrics.get.cards_truncated` measure how often it happens. **v1.1: paging** (`[cards] paging = true`): pages are built so that no page exceeds the firmware's 4-choice limit (`model.rs:589-592` rejects >4 with `range`): first page = 3 options + `More…`; middle pages = 2 options + `Back` + `More…`; last page = ≤3 options + `Back`. Choosing `More…`/`Back` is a navigation delivered to the daemon (so `card clear` is sent honestly), then the same logical card is re-issued with a new device card id for the next page, event `card.paged{page}`; the GUI shows everything at once. Cost per page: the device chimes again (a new `(agent,id)` raises a notice, P:115) **and**, because a cleared card drops the view to the channel (G:88-89,95-96), the next page arrives as a new notice that needs a toast tap or ENTER — two extra interactions per page, counted in `metrics.get.cards_paged{pages, extra_presses}`; free text via PTT is always available on a paged card. `[cards] paging_back = false` drops `Back` (3 options per middle page, LEFT/RIGHT cannot page).
- Permission cards: `Allow` (rec) / `Allow always` (only when the source supports it: Claude `permission_suggestions`, Codex app-server `acceptForSession` in v1.1) / `Deny`. Only the built-in adapters may set `origin.kind:"permission"|"question"|"elicitation"`; any other principal's card is `custom`, prefixed `» ` on the deck and badged in the GUI.
- **PTT intent grammar** on a permission card (`ctx:answer`, en-US only, `[voice] commands = true`): `yes|allow|approve|go ahead|do it|ok(ay)` → allow — a guarded operation, accepted because the PTT session's principal is `device` (a real deck `ptt` edge) or the trusted GUI that called `voice.listen`; any other caller was already refused `-32002` before capture started (§4.9), so the grammar never runs for an untrusted principal on such a card; `no|deny|reject|stop|don't` → deny; anything else → deny with `message = transcript` (the agent reads the operator's instruction and re-plans). On a question card free text is the answer verbatim. On `ctx:talk`: exactly `report|status` → speak "`name` is `state` since `age`. `first sentence of reply`" on `chatter`; `stop|quiet` → `voice.stop all`; everything else is an order.
- Ready cards from hooks have no steps; steps come only from `deck_done` / `agent.card.set type:r`. A step chosen on the deck (`answer` on a Ready card, G:90-91) is `op.order{text:label}` delivered **only** by the owner's `push`/`poll` (never `inject`, whatever `caps.order` says), followed by `card clear`. ENTER with no step is device-local (`agents.rs:352-355`) — nothing reaches the host; the store's Ready card is expired by `UserPromptSubmit`, the next state change, or `[cards] ready_ttl_s = 900` (`card.expired` + `card clear`). A PTT release with `ctx:answer` on a Ready card (the device latches the Ready id, `agents.rs:165-169`) is `op.order{source:"ptt"}` followed by `card clear`.
- Hold expiry and snooze release (`degraded`): the **same card id** re-pushed with text prefixed `⌨ `, choices `["Focus terminal"]`, `deliver:"focus"`; the state stays `question` (red) until `PostToolUse`/`Stop`/`SessionEnd` clears it; ENTER focuses the terminal and the card is cleared honestly and retired on the device (§4.8).

### 5.7 The hook installer (`deckd::integrations`, port of openmicrokbd `agent_integrations.rs`)

- Targets (as built, 2026-10-10): **Claude** — a plugin marketplace under `<data>/claude-marketplace/` registered through `claude plugin marketplace add` / `claude plugin install --scope user` (§5.1); `~/.claude/settings.json` is only *read* (`enabledPlugins`, and the migration that removes the pre-plugin entries), never written to install. **Codex** — `~/.codex/hooks.json` (the Codex group always **appended last** per event, §5.2's trust gate); `~/.codex/config.toml` only for `--trust`'s `[hooks.state]` hashes and the removal of an older install's `notify`/`tui.terminal_title`. The plan carries "What this runs" beside "What this writes": the `claude plugin …` commands, run after the writes on install and before them on uninstall (Claude reads the manifest from the folder about to go), tolerant of "already installed"/"not installed". `Env::run_clis` is what the tests turn off. *The rest of this bullet records the pre-plugin design:* `~/.claude/settings.json` (JSON, managed entries under a top-level `_openwalkietalkie` key — measured 2026-09-20 to be accepted by `claude doctor`, to leave hooks firing and to survive Claude's own rewrites of the file, so no sidecar is needed; the `"_managed":"openwalkietalkie"` hint may be written on a hook object but is **never load-bearing**, because Claude deletes it the next time it writes the file (a theme change stripped 3 of 3), so **ownership is recognised by `url`/`command`** and `_openwalkietalkie` is a cache the installer can rebuild from the file; command hooks always in exec form with `args`; `allowedHttpHookUrls` merged — appended when present, added with a visible note when absent, never replaced, and on uninstall only the one exact string the inventory records appending is removed), `~/.codex/hooks.json` + `~/.codex/config.toml` (TOML edited as text, line by line, so comments and key order survive; `# openwalkietalkie:begin/end` comment markers; the Codex group is always **appended last** per event, §5.2's trust gate), `~/.gemini/settings.json`, `~/Library/Application Support/Claude/claude_desktop_config.json` (the **only** Claude Desktop registration: stdio entry `openwalkietalkie-deck` with the absolute bundle path, §4.16), and the legacy `~/Library/LaunchAgents/ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie.icon-sync.plist` (target `icon-sync`: bootout + delete plist and binary, keep logs; `deckd run` also boots it out itself before opening HID, §10.2).

> **Caution — the managed inventory must contain no key named `hooks`, and no `PreToolUse`/`PermissionRequest` key, at any depth (measured 2026-09-20).** Such a key inside our own `_openwalkietalkie` block is a *fatal* settings diagnostic that **silently voids the entire settings.json**: measured, `"model"` fell back to the built-in default, `Hooks: Found 0 total hooks in registry`, and **nothing appeared on screen or in the transcript**. On the user's own file that would void `permissions`, their security profile, `model` and `enabledPlugins` at once, with no notice of any kind. The inventory therefore records flat strings (`entries: ["PermissionRequest/0/0", …]`) or an event map whose values are hashes (`events: {PostToolUse: […]}`) and never a copy of a matcher group or a handler object. A unit test asserts the rendered block trips none of the three traps, and the installer re-reads its own file with the same predicate before it publishes it; a file that trips it is restored from the backup rather than reported as success.

- Behaviour copied from openmicrokbd (`host-research/openmicrokbd.md:15`): `env::current_exe()` as the embedded path, staged atomic writes, managed markers, conflict detection (a foreign hook on the same event is reported, never overwritten), never editing keys it does not own, **never changing the mode of the user's files** (device-realtime's `chmod 0600` is dropped). Two places where the port is deliberately not a copy (measured 2026-09-20): openmicrokbd refuses only **translocated** paths (an `/AppTranslocation/` substring) and has no `/Applications` check, and a hard `/Applications` requirement would reject this machine's own `~/Applications/…` install — so translocation is a refusal and "outside `/Applications`" is a warning. And openmicrokbd's backups are unpruned hidden sibling directories, so `backups/<file>.<ts>.bak` with a 10-per-file prune is new work here; it keeps openmicrokbd's **rename, not copy** property, which is what makes a backup inode- and mode-exact. `statusLine` is the one entry that needs shell quoting (it has no `args` and runs in a shell, C23): `shell_word`/`decode_shell_word` are ported for it and reused for the Codex command string.
- Validating what was written: `claude doctor` **exits 0 even on a file it has rejected** (20/20 runs, measured 2026-09-20), so every test and gate row that validates settings parses stdout for the literal `Invalid settings` plus the file path and never looks at the exit code. A throwaway config that actually fires hooks needs more than `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`: a bare temp dir answers `Not logged in · Please run /login` and fires nothing, so the recipe is `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<tmp> ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=<dummy> ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:<local 401 stub>`, which fires every hook before the model call and then fails locally with nothing leaving the machine; `--debug-file <path>` is the reliable hook trace (`--debug hooks` on stderr is lost when the process is killed).
- Status vocabulary (`integrations.status`, nine words as built): `installed`, `outdated` (what is on disk differs from this build; for Claude also "the plugin files are written but Claude has not registered them" and "settings.json still carries the pre-plugin hooks"), `absent`, `conflict` (a file deckd must read cannot be parsed, or holds a shape the CLI would reject), `path_drift` (embedded path ≠ `current_exe`), `hooks_not_firing` (Codex: what `hooks/list` says, C18 — never the absence of a `SessionStart`), `paused` (Codex: `hooks/list` reports the hook trusted but `enabled: false`, switched off in `/hooks`), and the two Codex trust states of §5.2, `untrusted` (written, never reviewed, **does not run**) and `modified` (reviewed once, then the path, timeout or matcher changed — it stops running silently). "What this writes" shows the diff before install; Restore lists the backups. Two limits the status has to admit rather than paper over (measured 2026-09-20): `~/.claude/settings.json` is the lowest-precedence level, so "installed" means our entries are in the user file, not that they win (hook arrays merge, `statusLine` does not); and an http hook is simply never sent when Claude's proxy/TLS/resolver environment differs from the person's own, which is indistinguishable from a daemon that is down. Two words this section once listed are **not** in the wire vocabulary until something can produce one, and `listener?:{pid,command}` goes with them: `hooks_unreachable` and `hooks_hijack_risk` are the §4.1 port-degrade states, which `/v1/info.degraded`, `service.degraded{listener}` and the tray do carry but `integrations.status` cannot see — it answers on a blocking pool from its own `Paths`/`Config` with no handle on the daemon's service state. A client given a word no answer can carry writes a branch that is dead, so each lands with its path. `hooks_conflict` and `token_mismatch` left with the http hooks (2026-10-10): nothing produces them, so they are not in the vocabulary. `hooks_not_firing` and `paused` were out with them and came back with theirs: `codex::status` asks `hooks/list` whenever the call may run the CLIs, which is also the only thing that can tell a stale `trusted_hash` from a current one (C14a(iii), C18). Note that `paused` here is Codex's own `enabled: false`, not `integrations.pause` — that verb is still a v1.1 flow with nothing in this daemon behind it.
- `integrations.repair` rewrites the Claude plugin files for the current binary path and re-runs the two `claude plugin` commands (idempotent), and for Codex reinstalls with the current path and re-reads `hooks/list` rather than assuming the group indices it wrote last time. There is no embedded token to rotate any more: the plugin's hooks carry none. `install codex --trust` is the explicit opt-in that records the hook hashes; without it the install ends by printing the `/hooks` review step the user has to take (§5.2).

## 6. The device link (`deckd::hid`, `deckd::device`)

### 6.1 Constraints the writer is built around

| Constraint | Source | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| Report `[6, 5, len, payload≤61]`; hidapi buffers are 64 bytes with byte 0 = report id 6 (`host-research/critic.md:11`); JSON line ≤1024 B incl. LF; fragments of one ch-5 line never interleave with another ch-5 line, may interleave with other channels at report granularity | P:18-35 | One framer; whole lines serialised; ch-3 reports slotted only between whole ch-5 lines |
| ≤1 output report / 8 ms; firmware queue of 64 shared by reports, input and diagnostics; overflow drops the link | P:39-43 | Single writer with a monotonic 8 ms deadline on the hid thread; overflow-signature back-off |
| ≤~4 `agent` updates/s/agent; `ws` only while an Omarchy face is visible | P:44-46 | Per-slot title token bucket; `ws` gated by `face` |
| Device per-edge events one report each, ≥4 ms apart | P:47-49 | Zero-timeout reads polled at ≤2 ms |
| Whole-line validation; unknown fields ignored; strings cut on the device too; `err` ≤1/s | P:51-68 | Host truncates first; `range` for a card of an unknown agent must be prevented by ordering |
| hello/sid/sync/synced/offline/boot/first-line/no-echo rules | P:70-104 | Session state machine §6.3 |
| `answer` needs `card clear` ≤5 s | P:135, G:84-85 | 4 s delivery-confirmed clear |
| `channel{seq}` → `focus{ack:seq}`; `focus` without `ack` always applies | P:118,133 | Core keeps `last_channel_seq` |

### 6.2 HID thread loop

```
loop {
  while let Ok(op) = ops.try_recv() { view.apply(&op); outbox.enqueue(&view, op) }   // DeviceOp from core (bounded 512); view first, outbox second
  Absent  => enumerate every 800 ms for VID 1209 / PID 5754 / usage page ff00; open with set_open_exclusive(false) → Linked
  Linked  => while let Some(report) = dev.read_timeout(0)? { demux: ch5 → framer → to_core.send(DeviceLine) /*unbounded*/; else drop }
             if now >= next_write_at { if let Some(report) = outbox.next_report() { dev.write(report)?; next_write_at = now + pace } }
             if hello_due() { outbox.control(Hello) }
             sleep_until(min(next_write_at, now + 2 ms))
  on read/write error, removal callback, or Ok(0) streak (2 s presence re-enumeration, host-research/openmicrokbd.md:32) → Absent; to_core.send(LinkDown)
}
```

`view` is the `DeviceView` (§6.5): the hid thread's copy of what the device *should* show, kept whether or not a device is linked, so a barrier can be built from it at any time. `to_core` is the unbounded device→core channel of §2.3; `ops` the bounded core→hid one.

`pace` = `[device] pace_ms` (default 8, floor 8). Overflow signature: two link drops within 5 min each following a burst of ≥40 lines → `pace` 10 ms for 10 min, event `error{context:"pace_backoff"}`, visible in `metrics.get`. Channel 4 (diagnostics) input reports are dropped, and the daemon never writes on it. Channel 2, the deck's own system RPC, is no longer left alone (§6.6): the hid thread asks it for the deck's charge and board, and reads back its own replies.

### 6.3 Session state machine (core side)

```
             open ok                    hello reply, sid == mine && != 0
  Absent ──────────▶ Linked ──hello──▶ AwaitHello ───────────────────────────▶ Live ◀──────────────────┐
    ▲                                  │  ▲ │ 2 s no reply → resend hello,        │ hello every 5 s; reply → stay │
    │ link down (any state)            │  └─┘ miss++ (miss == 3 → Degraded)       │ 2 s no reply → miss++         │
    │                                  │ reply sid != mine (0 or foreign)         │ miss == 3 → Degraded          │
    │                                  ▼                                          ▼                               │
    ├──────────────────────────── Syncing ──barrier written, `synced` sent──▶ Live                                 │
    │                                  ▲                                                                           │
    │                                  │ reply sid != mine                                                         │
    └──── I/O error, or miss == 6 ── Degraded (handle kept, hello every 5 s) ──reply sid == mine──────────────────┘
```

| State | Entry actions | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `Absent` | `device.link{link:"offline"}`; release synthesised keys (Macropad `keys` actions, later); abort any PTT (`voice.state{state:"aborted", reason:"link"}` — there is no `ptt.aborted` kind, §7.4); cards in `answering` → `failed{reason:"link"}`; audio lanes **held** (not flushed) for 30 s | Device greys the roster after 15 s without hello (P:90) |
| `Linked` | send `hello{v:1, sid, hm}` at once; `sid` random nonzero u32 chosen once per daemon start | `hm` = local `HH:MM` (`[device] clock`) |
| `AwaitHello` | 2 s timer: on expiry resend `hello`, `miss++`; `miss == 3` → `Degraded` | Store `fw`, `sid`, `boot`, `rx` (assert 1024), `faces` from the reply |
| `Syncing` | `boot:1` → persisted `faces` + **every** persisted `set` key (up to 8 lines: speaker, mic, announce, chime, autojump, brightness, idlehome, demo — the device persists nothing, G:277-279, and `boot:1` asks for all of them, P:95-97) are part of the barrier; `boot:0` → adopt the device's `faces` into `state.json`; build and write the **sync barrier** (§6.5) from the `DeviceView` | The device raises no toasts during the sync (P:88) |
| `Live` | hello every 5 s; `up` handling; `face` gates `ws`/`macro`; normal lane traffic | A reply whose `sid` ≠ mine at any time → `Syncing` (device rebooted / link came back) |
| `Degraded` | `device.link{link:"degraded"}`; the handle is **kept** and `hello` keeps going every 5 s (so a late reply is still received and, with `sid == mine`, returns to `Live`; with a foreign `sid` → `Syncing`); the handle is dropped and the device re-enumerated only on an I/O error or after 3 further misses (`miss == 6`, 30 s), because BLE HoG may deliver nothing without an error | 3 misses = 15 s, the device's own offline threshold, so both sides agree |

`up` (P:132): release every synthesised key, cancel any live PTT session, clear held Macropad state. After a hello gap with the link up, the reply to the next hello is followed by `up` (P:93-94) — handled identically.

### 6.4 Inbound device lines → core commands

| Line | Core handling | Outbound |
|---|---|---|
| `hello` | session SM; `hello.faces` seeds the strip; `visible_face` is **unknown until the first `face`** — the device never reports it in `hello`, and `face` is emitted only on changes (`src/ui/state/global.rs:537-541`). While unknown, and the strip contains an Omarchy face, one `ws` goes into the barrier and the `Ws` key passes at ≤1 per 2 s, so a deck sitting on the Omarchy face when the host connects is not blank until the operator slides away and back | — |
| `channel{agent(slot), seq}` | slot → agent id; `last_channel_seq = seq`; `focus = {agent, source:"device"}`; `focus.changed`; ask `desktop::focus` to activate the terminal (async, best effort) | `focus{agent, ack:seq}` **immediately** (P0), not after the activation result — the ack is about ordering, not the PC's success (P:118) |
| `ptt{act:1, agent, ctx, card?}` | PTT SM start (§7.4); barge-in cut first | `transcript` partials |
| `ptt{act:0, abort?}` | PTT SM finish/abort | `transcript final:1`, then `name` / answer / order |
| `answer{agent, card, choice}` | card → `answering`; 4 s timer; owner delivery per `deliver` (§4.8); `choice` maps through the card's page offset; `Ok` → `delivered` + `card clear`; `Err`/timeout → `failed` (no clear; the device reverts with "not delivered", G:84-85). A `focus` card → `op.focus`, then `card clear` when the activation returns, card retired on the device. A Ready card with a step → `op.order{text:label}` by `push`/`poll` → `card clear`. A group card → clear at once, next card enqueued (§4.6). An allow on a permission card is accepted because the principal is `device` — a line that arrived on the HID link is the only way to be `device` (trusted, §4.2) | `card{agent,id,clear:1}` (P0) |
| `snooze{agent, card}` | card → `snoozed`; **release any held hook** with no decision; `card.snoozed`; the adapter re-pushes the **same id** with the degraded `⌨ ` content (`deliver:"focus"`) so the drawer entry stays answerable, `card.degraded` | `card{agent,id,…}` same id (P1, no new notice on the device) |
| `key{k, act, shift, f}` | `device.key` event (opt-in); macOS v1: nothing else | — |
| `up` | as above | — |
| `macro{f, i, act}` | resolve `f` → layout: `state.json.macropad_bindings[f]`, else the current strip's `faces[].n` for `f` matched against `[macropad."<name>"]`; key `i`; run `action` on `act:1` (or `2` for wheel); `device.macro` event; unbound `f` → event only and the GUI's Macropad editor offers the binding | — |
| `ws{go}` / `ws{win}` | macOS v1: `win` → `op.focus` on that row's agent; `go` → event only; Linux: hyprctl | — |
| `doclines{agent(slot), d, id, first}` | the deck scrolled towards part of a body it does not hold: wrap that document (§6.8) and `Put(DocText(slot,d,id))` the chunk **starting at** `first`. A request naming a body that is no longer the one on that slot is **dropped silently** — it raced a replacement, and unlike a lost answer nothing was lost | `doc` |
| `refresh{what:"usage"}` | `usage.refresh_requested`; feeders re-push; if nothing arrives within 1.5 s re-send the cached `usage` **unchanged** so the device's "updating…" ends (G:120-121) — `period` is capped at 12 B (P:121), so no suffix; staleness is shown by the `c` tone (`gray`) on each item and in the GUI | `usage` (P3) |
| `set{k, v}` | persist to `state.json`; speaker/mic → mixer/capture gain; announce/chime → policy; `device.set` event; never echoed (P:102-104) | — |
| `faces{list}` | persist; recompute live `macro`/`ws` keys; `device.faces` | `macro` for a new Macropad face with a known layout |
| `face{f}` | `visible_face = f`; start/stop `ws`; `macro` for a Macropad | `ws`/`macro` |
| `err{why}` | counter + `error` event with the last line's `m` (writer keeps a 16-line history); `range` right after a `card`/`reply` ⇒ re-send that agent's full `agent` line then the card/reply once, then mark the card `failed{reason:"device"}`; `size`/`parse` ⇒ bug: log the shadow line **redacted** (`m`, key, byte length, blake3 prefix of the line) unless `[privacy] log_content` — the shadow holds card text, reply text and transcript tails | — |

### 6.5 Outbox: keyed, prioritised, coalescing, with a sync barrier

```rust
enum Lane { P0, P1, P2, P3 }
enum Key { Hello, Focus, CardClear(Slot,u16), Transcript, Name, Set(SetKey), Faces,
           Card(Slot,u16), AgentState(Slot), AgentTitle(Slot), Reply(Slot), Ws, Usage, Macro(FaceId), AgentGone(Slot) }

/// What core sends the hid thread. `Line` is the typed `deck_proto::HostLine` value (e.g. `HostLine::Agent{slot, kind, name, state, ..}`),
/// never wire bytes: encoding, byte caps and chunking happen on the hid thread. Core says *what* the device should show; the hid thread says *how and when*.
enum DeviceOp { Put(Key, Line), Remove(Key), Sync { boot: bool, reason: SyncReason }, Control(Control /* Hello, CardClear(Slot,u16), Focus{agent, ack?}, Transcript, Name, Synced */) }

/// Owned by the hid thread: the device's desired state, one entry per key, kept across link drops and sids.
struct DeviceView { entries: BTreeMap<Key, Line> }          // §6.5 barrier order is the natural BTreeMap order of `Key`
struct Shadow     { sid: u32, written: HashMap<Key, Line> } // what this sid has actually been sent; cleared on every new sid
```

The **`DeviceView`** is the answer to "who owns the device-side desired state": the core projects its `Snapshot` into `DeviceOp`s (`project(&Snapshot)` at start and after an overflow, incremental `Put`/`Remove` from every reducer effect), the hid thread folds them into the view **before** touching the outbox, and the view — not the core, not the shadow — is what a barrier dumps. The **shadow** is a different thing: the lines this `sid` has actually received, used for diffing and suppression, and empty exactly when a barrier is needed. Both live on the hid thread; the core never formats a line and never sees a `sid`.

| Lane | Keys | Merge rule |
|---|---|---|
| P0 control | `Hello`, `Focus`, `CardClear`, `Transcript`, `Name`, `Set`, `Faces` | keep-latest per key; `Focus` keeps the highest `ack`; an unsent `Card` followed by its `CardClear` cancels both |
| P1 attention | `Card` (set/update), `AgentState` (kind/name/state/since), `AgentGone` | keep-latest against the view; on the wire, `AgentState` is sent as the full record when the shadow has no entry for that slot under this `sid`, else as the diff between `view[AgentState(slot)]` and `shadow[AgentState(slot)]` — only changed fields (P:55-57) |
| P2 content | `Reply`, `AgentTitle` (token bucket 4/s/slot, burst 2) | keep-latest |
| P3 faces | `Ws` (while an Omarchy face is visible; while `visible_face` is still unknown and the strip contains one, ≤1 per 2 s), `Usage`, `Macro(f)` (only when face `f` exists) | keep-latest |

Strict priority between lanes; an entry older than 1 s in P2/P3 is promoted one lane so a title storm cannot starve `usage` forever. `AgentTitle` identical to the shadow is suppressed; the shadow is cleared on every new `sid`. Byte caps enforced at encode time on the hid thread (names 24, titles 64, card text 128, choices 32×4, reply 200, transcript tail 120, `ws.t` 48, `name` 16, `usage.n` 20/`r` 24, `period` 12), cut on char boundaries with `…`; a line > 1000 B is a bug: dropped with an `error` event, never sent.

Chunking: a line is emitted as `ceil(len/61)` reports on consecutive pace ticks; the outbox does not start a new line until the current one is fully written (fragment atomicity, P:33-35).

**Sync barrier (the judges' fix).** `synced` is not a lane key. When the session enters `Syncing` (new `sid`, or `DeviceOp::Sync` from core after an overflow), the hid thread switches the outbox to barrier mode: the barrier is an **ordered dump of the `DeviceView`** — `agent` for every slotted agent (full record, ascending slot) → `card` for every pending/snoozed/degraded card (Questions first; retired `focus` cards are not in the view) → `reply` per agent → `focus{agent}` (no ack) **only if** the view's focus entry is marked PC-sourced (`desktop`/`api`) after the last device `channel` — a `focus` without `ack` always applies (P:118) and would otherwise override the selection the device kept across the link drop (P:86), and after a daemon restart the host has no focus to push at all → `macro` for each Macropad face in the strip with a bound layout → `usage` → `ws` if an Omarchy face is visible **or** `visible_face` is unknown and the strip contains one → (`boot:1` only) `faces` then every persisted `set` key (≤8) → `synced` — written in that order at the pace, nothing else interleaved. Coalescing during the barrier falls out of the view: a `Put` to a key the barrier has not written yet simply updates the view entry that will be dumped; a `Put` to a key already written goes to the lanes for after `synced`. Ordering guarantees `card` after its `agent` (else `range`, P:66-67) and `synced` last (else the device purges what the sync had not yet refreshed, P:86-89). A full sync of 8 agents is ≈80 reports ≈ 0.7 s.

Card ids: per slot, monotonically increasing u16, **`last + 1` with wrap-around skipping 0**, allocated by core; the last id per slot is persisted in `state.json.card_ids` (debounced 500 ms, but written synchronously before the `card` line is emitted, so the persisted value is never behind the device). There is no random base: only the current device card id per slot matters (one card per slot on the device), and `last + 1` is guaranteed not to be it. This matters because the device keeps the previous session's cards and drawer notices until `synced`, and a new question landing on a stale `(slot,id)` would be taken for a re-push (no chime, inheriting the old sent/snoozed/acknowledged flags, `model.rs:604-616`). If `state.json` is corrupt the counters restart at 1 and the exposure is one card that arrives before the first `synced` after that specific restart — logged, accepted. The per-slot shadow and counter are reset on `AgentGone` so slot reassignment cannot collide either. A new `sid` re-sends the same ids for cards that are still live so the device's "(agent,id) is new?" rule raises no duplicate notices (P:88-89).

### 6.6 Channel 2

- Channel 2 is the device's own system RPC (`sys.version`, `sys.reboot` with `mode: recovery`, `device.status`), answered by the firmware itself, for `tools/flash-usb.py` and the bench tools as for `deckd`. The Codex Micro emulation (Codex app RPC on channel 2, icon sync on channel 3, the Codex face, `ptt ctx:codex`) has been removed.
- **`deckd` writes on channel 2 (corrected 2026-11-12; this section said it never did).** Since the companion app the hid thread asks a live deck for its charge, `device.status`, every `[device] status_poll_s` (30 s by default, never more often than every 5 s), and once per link for its board, `sys.version` (`device::status`). The two requests carry ids of their own (47301, 47302), each fits one report, one is in flight at a time, and one goes out only when the outbox has nothing due (`Link::pump_writer`), outside its pace and barrier as a voice frame is. The replies, read by their line feed, become the battery, charging and board of the `device.status` event, beside the transport and link drops the hid thread reports itself. Nothing is asked before the session is live or while an install holds the device; a reply with another id is another program's conversation and holds our requests back for 10 s; no reply within 2 s is given up; `-32601` stops that method for the rest of the link. **`[device] status_poll_s = 0` is the kill switch**: the deck is asked nothing on channel 2, its board included. Read at start.
- The firmware updater (`firmware::transport`) also speaks on channel 2, from a handle of its own, as id 47201, during a check or an install: `sys.version`, `sys.reboot{mode:"recovery"}` and the bootloader migration's `boot.*` calls.
- The open stays non-exclusive (`set_open_exclusive(false)`) so a bench tool can share the device with a running `deckd`; IOKit delivers every input report to every non-seizing client (§16 Q3, answered).

### 6.7 Latency budget (targets; unmeasured items flagged)

The SCO/A2DP voice measurements in this table are historical Classic results
from 2026-09-21 (`transport = "coreaudio"`); current BLE bench results are in §7.9.

| Path | Budget | Composition |
|---|---|---|
| Device edge → core | ≤20 ms | BLE interval 7.5–15 ms + hidapi queue + ≤2 ms poll |
| `channel` → `focus{ack}` on the wire | ≤30 ms | P0, one report |
| Hook arrival → toast on the device | ≤100 ms | core → P1 → ≤5 reports × 8 ms + BLE |
| Device `answer` → `card clear` | typical ≤50 ms, hard cap 4 s | delivery confirmation (HTTP response ≈ ms) + one P0 report |
| PTT press → TTS silent **in the mixer** (barge-in) | ≤35 ms | core ≤1 ms → atomic lane cut → next render quantum 11.61 ms (512 f @ 44.1 k, measured V18) + 10 ms fade. What the operator hears is ≈0.5 s later: the link is a ≈470 ms delay line (V6, §7.3) |
| PTT press → first **non-zero** mic sample | **1031 ms median** (815–1246, n=16, measured 2026-09-21, V1) — the estimate here had been 150–500 ms | mitigations §7.5; the first *callback* is ~28 ms and means nothing (V2) |
| STT partial → `transcript` on the device | partial cadence (~200–400 ms) + 24 ms | P0 keep-latest, ≤4/s |
| PTT release → final dispatched | On completed decode | Preview text is never dispatched; a failed or timed-out decode aborts |
| PTT release → TTS audible | A2DP resume **2307 ms median** (2281–2447, n=16, 2026-09-21) + first synth buffer 100–300 ms; or at once at the SCO rate with `speak_over_sco` (§7.5) | queue-and-hold in the mixer; `Reopening` opens at the current rate rather than waiting for 44100 (V3) |
| Full sync after `sid` mismatch | ≈0.7 s | ≈80 reports |
### 6.8 Host-side layout: documents the deck scrolls

**deckd is the server, the deck is the browser.** The host owns the whole text, lays it out with the
deck's own metrics, and serves **chunks**; the deck buffers a run of them, scrolls inside it and asks
for the next one before it runs out.

Every long lane used to be cut with an ellipsis before it was sent: `CAPS.card_text` 128 B for a
question body, `CAPS.reply` 200 B for a reply, `CAPS.transcript` 120 B for speech. A real agent reply
measured **2347 characters**, so the deck showed 8% of it, and then cut again on screen at 2–3 lines.

Streaming pixels was considered and rejected with the numbers: `PAYLOAD_MAX = 61` B a report at
`pace_ms = 8` is **7,625 B/s**, against 460,800 B for one 480×480 RGB565 frame ≈ **60 s**. Pixels are
~450× too expensive. Only the *layout* moves to the host; the deck still draws.

**The wrap is the firmware's own.** `deck-wrap` mounts `src/ui/font.rs` and its glyph atlas rather
than reimplementing greedy word wrap, because the two sides have to break lines in the same places
and a second implementation is one that will one day disagree — the shape that made week 5's
resolver bug invisible.

| Direction | Line | Shape |
|---|---|---|
| host → device | `doc` | `{agent, d, id, first, total, font, lines[]}` |
| device → host | `doclines` | `{agent, d, id, first}` |

- **`d`** is `c` \| `r` \| `w` \| `t` — card, reply, workflow, transcript. **Single letters, measured**: `doclines` is a
  device→host line and must fit one 61-byte report, and `"d":"reply"` makes it 63 B, which the framer
  drops. `"d":"r"` makes it 59.
- **`id`** is the card id for `c`, and a per-slot **revision** for `r`/`w`/`t`; the `reply` line carries
  the same `rev`, so a chunk of a body that has since been replaced is dropped rather than drawn over
  the new one. `Key::DocText` is declared **after** `Card` and `Reply`: the enum's order is the
  barrier's order, and a chunk arriving before the line carrying its id would be dropped as belonging
  to a body the deck is not showing.
- Caps: `doc_line` 48 B a line, `doc_lines` **17** a chunk. **17 is the ceiling, not a preference**:
  the widest `doc` line is 961 B at 17 and 1012 at 18, past `CAPS.line` (1000). Chunks are cut by
  **encoded** length (`fit_doc_window`), because escaping costs a byte per `"` and `\` and the outbox
  *drops* an over-cap line rather than truncating it.
- The host serves the chunk **starting where the deck asked**. It used to centre it on the requested
  line; that was overscan the deck no longer needs.
- The flat `card`/`reply`/`transcript` lines are unchanged and still capped, so a deck that never
  scrolls behaves as it always did, and a body that fits its lane *and* its flat line gets no chunk.

**The deck buffers, it does not hold one chunk.** `DOC_HELD` is 40 lines and `Doc::merge` widens the
held span when a chunk touches it. This is the difference between working and not: a chunk takes
**2.0–2.5 s** on the real link (measured) and a brisk scroll is a line every 200 ms, so one 17-line
chunk left ~1.2 s of scroll against a 2.5 s refill and the wheel was dead **66% of the time**, in
runs of 2.2 s. Buffering 40 takes it to **1%**, and `the_wheel_keeps_up_with_the_link` fails if the
buffer is shrunk back. The deck asks for the chunk beyond its buffer — the lines it is *missing* —
when fewer than `WINDOW_MARGIN` (14) lines are left ahead, which is the 2.8 s the refill needs.

A request expires after 5 s so a lost chunk is asked for again, and it is keyed by document **and
agent and revision**: keyed only by line number it swallowed a sibling's request as a duplicate, and
at the edge of the buffer — where the offset stops advancing, so the same line is asked for every
detent — that swallowed request never came again and the wheel went dead for good.

Measured on the device with `DOC_HELD` 40: `Doc` 2,008 B, `Card` 2,288, `Deck` 40,312, `UiState`
41,204 — 12.7% of the 318 KB the main core reports free.

**Text size is the operator's** (`Settings → Display → Text size`, small/medium/large, default
medium). It is a `set` key, `text`, and the **host** is told because the host is what wraps: a size
the two sides disagreed about would break every line in a different place. The face ramp and the
lines-per-lane counts live in firmware source (`DocFont::for_size`, `reply_lines`, `question_lines`)
and `deck-wrap` reads them through the mount rather than copying them. Changing the size re-lays out
and re-sends every document; until the new chunks land the deck draws in the face its buffer was
measured with, so the two never disagree mid-flight. Line heights: Row 20 px → 25 (7 reply lines),
Text 24 px → 30 (6), Title 26 px → 32 (6).

**A scroll bar has to be inside the region its signature hashes.** `CHANNEL_REPLY` ends at x 442 and
`REPLY_BAR` is at 444, so the first reply bar was drawn outside every damage region: the thumb was
painted once and then never moved while the text scrolled under it. `REPLY_LANE` now covers both, as
`QUESTION_LANE` always did for the card, and the partial-refresh render test fails without it.

**Known trade.** A sync barrier re-sends chunks from line 0, so a resync while scrolled returns the
view to the top. On the two common resyncs — daemon start, device reboot — the deck has lost its
buffer anyway.

**Not yet done.** The deck draws plain text: there is no markdown anywhere, so `**bold**` renders its
asterisks. Adding it needs styled runs on the wire and a bold atlas at the body size — there is none
today (Body is 17 px Regular; the nearest bold is Macro 18 px SemiBold) and no monospace at all.

## 7. Voice (`deckd::audio`, `deckd::voice`)

Owned by the daemon; the deck's PTT edges and the API's `voice.listen` are the same entry.

**Two transports, one engine (2026-10-04).** The Rev A deck is **no longer a Bluetooth Classic audio device**: no HFP, no A2DP, no CoreAudio devices at all. Its microphone and speaker travel as our own 16 kHz IMA ADPCM frames over a GATT service on the same BLE link as its HID side (`docs/deck-protocol.md`, "Voice"), and `deckd` reaches that service with CoreBluetooth. `[voice] transport` picks the devices `deckd-audio` is built around: `"ble"`, the default, is §7.9; `"coreaudio"` is everything this section says about CoreAudio UIDs, SCO and A2DP, kept for a deck still running Classic firmware. The lanes, the mixer, the route machine, the PTT machine and the engines are shared, and so are their tests — the two transports differ only behind §7.6's `CaptureSource` and `OutputSink` seams. Where a paragraph below is about SCO, A2DP or a CoreAudio UID, it describes the `"coreaudio"` transport.

**Historical Classic addressing (`transport = "coreaudio"`).** Every device is selected by CoreAudio UID — the deck is two devices both named "Codex Micro": input `C2-2D-81-AB-88-81:input` (1 ch, 16 kHz mSBC) and output `C2-2D-81-AB-88-81:output` (2 ch, 44.1 kHz A2DP, 16 kHz while SCO holds it) (`host-research/voice-macos.md:5,22`). Measured 2026-09-21 (V8), `AvailableNominalSampleRates` is `{16000}` on the input and `{16000, 44100}` on the output, so the 8 kHz CVSD path stays as defensive code and is unreachable in this configuration. AudioDeviceIDs change per connection — 101/95 at the research, 99/93 at the week-4 probes — so **UID addressing is mandatory**; the system default device is never touched. `[voice] device_uid = "C2-2D-81-AB-88-81"` is discovered rather than guessed, and cpal cannot do all of it: `Device::name()` is gone (it is `description()` now) and on macOS the description is thin — every device, the deck included, reports `device_type: Unknown, interface_type: Unknown, manufacturer: None` — so "the first **Bluetooth** device whose UID ends in `:output`" is resolved through `kAudioDevicePropertyTransportType` via objc2-core-audio, plus the `:output` suffix and the name (V12).

### 7.1 Capture

cpal 0.18.2 selects the input **by UID** with no help: `Device::id()` reads `kAudioDevicePropertyDeviceUID`, `Host::device_by_id` round-trips it and a `DeviceId` renders as `"coreaudio:<uid>"` — verified live returning `coreaudio:C2-2D-81-AB-88-81:input` (V11). `[voice] device_uid` holds the bare pair without a side suffix and the engine appends `:input`/`:output`. `objc2-core-audio` is still needed, but not for the lookup: it is for the property listeners that run while no stream is open — `kAudioDevicePropertyTransportType` for the discovery above, `kAudioDevicePropertyDeviceIsRunningSomewhere` for §7.2's `ScoExternal` (V11). The open requests exactly the device's own nominal config and never another rate — cpal would otherwise try to set `kAudioDevicePropertyNominalSampleRate` and a BT endpoint cannot honour it (`host-research/voice-macos.md:7`); f32 mono frames go into an `rtrb` SPSC ring; the STT feeder wraps them in `AVAudioPCMBuffer`s on the main thread (§7.6, V13); `set mic` (0..10) scales samples before STT; an RMS meter emits `voice.level` (opt-in) for the GUI. The input stream exists while a PTT session is live and while a Question card is pending: **`[voice] mic_warm = "card_pending"` is the default** (`"never"` and `"always"` are the other two), because SCO bring-up measured **1031 ms median and 1246 ms at worst** (n=16, 2026-09-21) against the 300 ms threshold §16 Q2 set for switching it, and a pending Question is precisely when the operator is about to press PTT. If the deck's input UID is absent (BLE HID only, Classic audio not connected), `[voice] fallback_input` decides: `"none"`, the default since the companion app (2026-11-11), answers the press with silence and `no_device`, because a press that records the room instead of the deck is not what anyone pressed for; `"system_default"`, the default before it, captures from the Mac's mic and the route event says so. The BLE capture falls back the same way when the deck's microphone is not there at open.

**On the BLE transport `mic_warm` is moot** and the engine treats it as `"never"` whatever it says: the deck powers its microphone and starts streaming on the press itself, so there is no bring-up to hide, and an input held open "warm" would only hold the speaker shut. The capture keeps a pre-roll of the stream from its START instead (§7.9), which is what the warm input bought on Classic.

**Since acted on** — `deckd-audio` reads all three keys (`Engine::apply_warm`, `Engine::may_speak` and the `slow_sco_ms` report, §7.5); the paragraph after this one is the 2026-09-21 state, kept for the record.

**Not yet acted on (2026-09-21).** `mic_warm`, and with it `speak_over_sco` and `slow_sco_ms` (§7.5), are parsed, validated, hot-reloaded, pushed to the engine as `AudioCmd::Policy` and reported applied by `config.reload` — and **read by no code**. The capture opens on the press and only on the press, whatever `mic_warm` says. The defaults above are the measured ones and are what the engine is to honour; until it does, this paragraph describes an intention rather than a behaviour, and a reader implementing from it should treat the keys as the contract and the current engine as the gap.

### 7.2 Output route state machine

```
 NoDevice ──output UID appears──▶ A2dp(44.1 k, stream OPEN and kept open)
    ▲                                │ ptt press (ours) → hold lanes, close output, open input    │ nominal-rate listener says 16000 with no PTT of ours
    │ DeviceNotAvailable             ▼                                                             ▼  (V7: the ROUTINE path here — the deck is the system default input, so Zoom, dictation or the Codex app opens SCO under us)
    │                            ScoOurs(capture running, output closed)                       ScoExternal(lanes held, output closed)
    │                                │ ptt release/abort → close input                             │ listener says 44100
    │                                ▼                                                             │
    └───────────────── Reopening(open at the CURRENT rate at once, ≤3 s, ×2) ◀────────────────────┘
                            │ ok → A2dp (resume held Question/Ready, flush stale Chatter)
                            │ fail ×2 → ScoStuck: open output at the current rate, resample 44.1→16 k, voice.route{stuck:true}; recovers on the next rate change
```

**`speak_over_sco` (V1/V5).** A2DP resume is a fixed ~2.3 s macOS timer (2307 ms median, n=16), so `[voice] speak_over_sco = true` (the default) plays the post-release reply into the SCO tail at the device's rate rather than waiting it out; `false` holds the announcement in the queue until the route is back to A2DP, and `ttl_s` is what decides when waiting has gone on too long. `ScoStuck` speaks either way — A2DP is not coming back and §7.2's own answer there is "play at 16 k". **Neither setting may play while a capture of ours is live**: V5 measured the deck's speaker→mic loopback at ≈ +17 dB with no AEC, so `speak_over_sco` is about the tail *after* the microphone closes and is not a licence to talk over it.
