- **The blur** (the 2026-10-04 window only). gpui's `Blurred` window uses the `Selection` material, which macOS 26+ no longer blurs; switching gpui's effect view to `UnderWindowBackground`, as Zeron's fork does, was the fix.
- **Translucent fills leave alpha squared** (the same). A fill of alpha `a` on a transparent window ends up as `a²` for the window server, so Zeron's 80 % glass came out at 64 % (`#303030` instead of near-black). The opaque window has neither problem.
- **Give page columns a width in pixels.** A flex column sized `w_full` + `max_w` lays some descendants out against the *uncapped* width in a pass gpui keeps: cards at the cap with paragraphs beside them at the full window, or the whole column overflowing its own centred box. `ui::page` takes a definite width from the room the shell measures each frame instead.
- **gpui text paints the lines of its last measurement**, which need not be the width it was finally laid out at. `ui::para` (used by `ui::hint`) shapes again at prepaint when the two differ.
- **A block `div` around a flex row can shrink the row to its content.** Rows, cards and strips are `w_full`, and card rows sit in flex wrappers.
## 9. Config and persistence

All under `~/Library/Application Support/OpenWalkieTalkie/` (dir 0700; Linux: `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-~/.config}/openwalkietalkie/` for `config.toml`, `tokens.toml` and `status.json`, `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/openwalkietalkie/` for `state.json` and `logs/`, `${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-~/.cache}/openwalkietalkie/` for the caches, and `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/openwalkietalkie/`, else that cache directory, for the socket, the lock and `port`; on either platform `DECKD_DATA_DIR` puts all of it in that one directory) via `directories::BaseDirs` (`config::Paths::detect`).

| File | Mode | Owner of truth | Contents |
|---|---|---|---|
| `config.toml` | 0644, user-edited, hot-reloaded (notify 8.2 + 300 ms debounce, validate whole file → `ArcSwap`, `config.changed`; invalid → keep the last good, copy to `config.toml.invalid`, `error` event, GUI banner with the TOML position) | user | everything the operator sets on purpose (below); `config.set {patch}` edits **managed** keys only (below), whole or not at all, via `toml_edit` — comments, order and formatting preserved, and nothing but the named keys touched |
| `state.json` | 0600, daemon-written (atomic rename, 500 ms debounce; `card_ids` written synchronously before the card line is emitted) | daemon/device | `faces` (last adopted strip), `settings` (all eight `set` keys, restored on `boot:1`), `slots` `{key → {slot, last_seen, kind, cwd}}`, `card_ids` `{slot → last id}` (next = `last + 1`, u16 wrap skipping 0, §6.5), `macropad_bindings` `{f → layout name}`, `voices` `{key → voice id}`, `terminal_map`, `usage` last snapshot, `sid` history; corrupt → renamed `state.json.corrupt`, start empty (faces adopted from the device on the next `boot:0`; card ids restart at 1, §6.5) |
| `tokens.toml` | 0600 | daemon | `[[token]] name, hash (blake3), scopes, created, expires?, embedded_in:["claude","codex"]` — hashes only; **no plaintext token is ever written by the daemon** (the old `token` file is gone: a long-lived all-scope secret at a documented path is readable by every same-uid agent) |
| `port` | 0600 | daemon | the bound port (`47201`), empty while degraded and while the TCP door is off (§4.1) |
| `deckd.sock`, `deckd.lock` | 0600 | daemon | UDS; `flock` single instance (second `deckd run` exits 0 "already running" so launchd does not loop — which needs the plist's `KeepAlive = {SuccessfulExit: false}`, corrected in week 6; it had been `true`, which restarts on any exit) |
| `macropad/<src>.toml` | user | user | `[[key]] l, b?, action?` ×9 per Macropad instance; `src` is shown in the face header (P:120) |
| `backups/<file>.<ts>.bak` | | installer | pre-edit copies of the four integration files, pruned to 10 per file |
| `~/Library/Logs/OpenWalkieTalkie/deckd.log`, `app.log` | | | `tracing-appender` daily rolling, 7 kept; content redacted unless `[privacy] log_content` |
| `~/Library/Logs/OpenWalkieTalkie/deckd.launchd.log` | 0600 | `deckd run` itself | stdout/stderr of the agent (panics, pre-`tracing` output). launchd does not expand `~` in plist paths and an SMAppService plist inside the signed bundle is identical for every user, so the plist carries **no** `StandardOutPath`/`StandardErrorPath`; instead the first statements of `deckd run` open this file (`O_APPEND\|O_CREAT`, 0600, rotate at 10 MB) and `dup2` it onto fds 1 and 2, and a `std::panic::set_hook` writes panics through `tracing` with payload redaction (an `unwrap` on a hook value must not print the value). launchd spawn failures never reach any file: `deckd doctor` reads `launchctl print gui/$UID/ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie.deckd` for the last exit status and spawn errors |
| `~/Library/Caches/OpenWalkieTalkie/` | | | rendered chimes, engine caches |
| `~/Library/Preferences/ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie.plist` | | GUI | the window's own preferences, never told to the service: the deck's case colour, whether first run is done, a language picked before any service answered, and whether a deck has been seen on this Mac (nothing is added before one is, §8.2) |
| Managed blocks in `~/.claude/settings.json`, `~/.codex/hooks.json`, `~/.codex/config.toml`, `~/.gemini/settings.json`, `~/Library/Application Support/Claude/claude_desktop_config.json` | user's files, user's modes | installer | §5.7 |

`config.toml` (v1 keys, all optional, defaults shown):

```toml
[api]
port = 47201
allowed_origins = []              # e.g. ["http://localhost:5173"] for a dev dashboard; empty = reject any browser Origin
uds_default_scope = "observe"     # what a UDS peer gets without asking
uds_max_scope = "admin"           # the most a UDS peer may ask for (agent-descended peers are capped at observe,agent,voice,hooks:ingest)
mailbox_ttl_s = 600               # lifetime of a logical HTTP session (token + client.name) and of undelivered poll answers
max_held_hooks = 64               # parked hooks at once, across every CLI and both doors; past it a hold is
                                  # refused -32008 and that hook fails open to the CLI's own dialog
# tcp = true                      # the TCP door (§4.1). Left out: off, unless the pre-plugin Claude HTTP hooks
                                  # may still post to it (in Claude's settings, or per deckd's own pre-plugin
                                  # record in integrations.json); an explicit value always wins.
                                  # Read at start. Removing the door and its tokens is planned for the next release

[codex]
daemon = true                     # the client of Codex's shared app-server daemon (§5.2); off leaves Codex to the hooks
# daemon_socket = "…"             # default: $CODEX_HOME/app-server-control/app-server-control.sock, else ~/.codex/…

[sources]                         # what may put sessions on the deck (the Integrations page); every key is hot
paused = false                    # every source at once: its sessions stay registered but hold no slot, a parked
                                  # question goes back to its tool's dialog, nothing is spoken, hooks answer {}
claude = true                     # Claude Code: its hooks and the roster seed
# claude_channels = false         # the Claude Channels bridge, an opt-in (§5.1); left out, it follows the installed
                                  # plugin: one from before the switch keeps its helper, a new install has none
apps_paused = []                  # connected apps paused one by one, by the name `sources.list` shows them under

[trust]
require_trusted_for_allow = true  # permission allows and inject orders only from the deck or the verified GUI
confirm = "sheet"                 # "sheet" | "touch_id" — how the GUI confirms a guarded call from an untrusted principal
gui_requirement = 'anchor apple generic and identifier "ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie" and certificate leaf[subject.OU] = "TEAMID"'
# dev_requirement = 'cdhash H"…"' # ad-hoc dev builds only; logged loudly

[privacy]
keep_tool_input = "pending"       # "pending" | "never" | "always"
keep_prompts = false
keep_replies = "last"
transcript_log = false
log_content = false               # also gates: device shadow lines, device.raw, panic payloads, prompt/tool titles in logs
event_ring = 4096

[agents]                          # pins; key is "<kind>:<name>" or a cwd
"claude:cohdl-api" = { slot = 1, voice = "com.apple.voice.enhanced.en-US.Samantha" }
"/Users/tony/projects/openmicro" = { slot = 3, name = "openmicro" }
grace_s = 300                     # orphan grace
evict = "lru_idle"

[hooks]
enabled = true                    # hot (week 6): `Adapters::enabled()` reads the `ArcSwap` cell on every
                                  # hook request, so the tray's "Pause hooks" takes effect at once. It was
                                  # missing from `HOT_KEYS` and reported "restart needed" although it was
                                  # already live — the mirror of B14's `[voice]`
[hooks.claude]
hold_s = 120                      # the ADAPTER's budget: it rides on every held card as `timeout_s`
                                  # (the store honours it over its own default and shares it across a
                                  # question group), so the two CLIs really do get different budgets
tool_titles = false
settle_s = 6                      # a permission ask waits this long, unseen, for an auto-approver or the
                                  # terminal to decide it; `permission_prompt` shows it sooner; 0 = at once
report_via = "http"               # "http" (sync, timeout 2 s) | "command" (v1.1: exec-form `deckd hook claude --async`, async: true)
desk_presence = false             # v1.1: shorten the hold to 2 s while the operator is active at the Mac
[hooks.codex]
hold_s = 120                      # as above; per-CLI because the adapter carries it, not the store
[hooks.gemini]
permission = "notify"             # "notify" | "beforetool"

[cards]
paging = false                    # v1: >4 choices are truncated to 4 on the deck (GUI shows all, PTT free text); v1.1 default true
paging_back = true                # middle pages 2 + Back + More…; false = 3 + More… and no Back
ready_ttl_s = 900                 # a Ready card expires (card clear) after this, or on the next prompt/state change

[voice]
transport = "ble"                 # "ble" (the deck's GATT audio service, §7.9) | "coreaudio" (a Classic deck); restart to change
device_uid = "C2-2D-81-AB-88-81"  # legacy CoreAudio UID only; BLE discovers the connected GATT service
fallback_output = "none"          # "none" | "system_default"
fallback_input = "none"           # "none" (a press with no deck microphone gets silence and no_device) | "system_default"
                                  # (the Mac's own input; the default until the companion app)
mic_warm = "card_pending"         # "never" | "card_pending" | "always"; V1 measured 1031 ms of bring-up; moot (never) on ble
speak_over_sco = true             # play into the SCO tail rather than wait out the 2307 ms A2DP resume; no effect on ble
slow_sco_ms = 1500                # legacy Classic press → first NON-ZERO sample above this → voice.route{slow_sco_ms}
commands = true                   # report/status/stop grammar and allow/deny intent
announce_questions = false
[voice.stt]
engine = "apple"                  # "apple" (on-device, the default) | "whisper" (a local model; opt-in, its weights downloaded by `deckd stt install` or the app)
locale = "en-US"
allow_server = false
model = ""                        # whisper: large-v3-turbo (default) | large-v3-turbo-q8_0 | large-v3-turbo-q5_0 | small | base.en
language = "auto"                 # whisper: "auto" detects per press (Chinese + English in one utterance); "en"/"zh" pins it; "" follows locale
threads = 0                       # whisper: decoder threads, 0 = the machine's own count
prompted = false                  # the app's one first-start question about a local model has been answered
[voice.tts]
engine = "cloud"                  # "cloud": provider's model with your key, Apple's without one (hot) | "kokoro": a local voice, downloaded on opt-in (hot) | "mlx": the Qwen3-TTS sidecar (built at start) | "apple"
announce_max_chars = 120
provider = "openai"               # "openai" | "elevenlabs"
api_key = ""                      # bring your own key: `deckd tts key set --provider openai`; empty = Apple speaks; never logged, config.get shows a hint
model = ""                        # empty = the engine's default: gpt-4o-mini-tts / eleven_flash_v2_5 / kokoro-multi-lang-v1_1 (or kokoro-int8-multi-lang-v1_1) / mlx-community/Qwen3-TTS-12Hz-1.7B-CustomVoice-8bit
voices = []                       # the engine's voices agents are assigned from; empty = its default four (kokoro: af_maple, zm_010, bf_vale, zf_001)
lang = "auto"                     # language code; "auto" lets the model detect it (ElevenLabs: sent as ISO 639-1)
# instruct = "calm and clear"     # a delivery note for models that take one (gpt-4o-mini-tts, Qwen3-TTS CustomVoice)
# python = "/path/to/venv/bin/python"   # mlx only; default: the environment `deckd tts install` makes under Caches/OpenWalkieTalkie/tts
interval_s = 0.32                 # mlx only: seconds of audio per streamed chunk
threads = 0                       # kokoro: decoder threads, 0 = a sensible default
[voice.voices]
default_pool = ["Samantha", "Daniel", "Karen", "Rishi"]

[desktop]
inject = ["ghostty"]              # v1.1 adds "channel", "tmux", "cgevent"
focus = true

[device]
pace_ms = 8
hello_s = 5
clock = "local"                   # hm in hello
usage_period = "5h / 7d"
status_poll_s = 30                # channel 2's charge poll (§6.6), never under 5; 0 asks the deck nothing on
                                  # channel 2, its board included. Read at start

[macropad."Build"]                # keyed by the face's spoken name (faces[].n); bound to a face id in state.json.macropad_bindings
keys = [ { l = "build", b = "left", action = { shell = "cargo build" } },
         { l = "test",  b = "right", action = { shell = "cargo test" } },
         { l = "deploy", action = { say = "deploying" } } ]

[usage]
providers = ["codex"]             # the bars, in this order: "claude" = the /api/oauth/usage probe with the Claude Code login (and a chained statusLine's rate_limits), "codex" = the app-server's account/rateLimits; a provider left out is never probed or drawn. Claude is the owner's opt-in since the companion app (the Usage panel writes this list): its probe reads the Claude Code login, from the Keychain on a Mac, which asks; a file that already names it keeps it
poll_s = 300                      # the Claude probe's cadence (60 at least); ENTER on the face probes at once

[scripts]                         # v1.1. optional user script hooks, async, rate-limited 20/s, event JSON on stdin
scope = "observe"                 # "observe" | "observe:content" — the event is redacted by the same fan-out rules as any subscriber
on_event = { types = ["card.set", "agent.changed"], command = ["/Users/tony/bin/deck-notify"] }

[log]
level = "info"
```

**The managed keys — what `config.set {patch}` may write** (week 6, `core::dispatch::MANAGED`): `api.port`, `hooks.enabled`, and the `[voice]` preferences `announce_questions`, `commands`, `device_uid`, `fallback_input`, `fallback_output`, `mic_warm`, `slow_sco_ms`, `speak_over_sco`, `stt.companion`, `stt.engine`, `stt.language`, `stt.locale`, `stt.model`, `stt.prompted`, `tts.announce_max_chars`, `tts.api_key`, `tts.engine`, `tts.provider`, `tts.model`, `tts.voices`, `tts.lang`, `tts.instruct`, `voices.default_pool` — each named, none taken as a `voice.*` wildcard. The companion app's pages added seven more, each in the change that added its control: the Integrations page's source switches, `sources.paused`, `sources.claude`, `sources.claude_channels` and `sources.apps_paused`, and its Codex switch, `codex.daemon` (`codex.daemon_socket` stays the operator's); the Service page's Local API switch, `api.tcp`; and the Usage panel's `usage.providers`, whose Claude switch says where it is offered that it reads the Claude Code login, the rule `voice.tts.api_key` joined under. `sources.claude_channels` is an opt-in rather than a pause: it decides whether the Claude plugin carries the channel helper and the channel door serves it, both local, and Claude still asks each session to opt in itself. `tts.api_key` is what sends the announcements off the machine, and it joined the list in the same change as the Voice screen's **Spoken replies** control, which says so where the key is entered (the rule `voice.stt.allow_server` still waits on, below). `config.get` publishes it as a hint (`…abcd (51 chars)`, `""` for none), never the value, and `config.set`'s took-check compares a written key by its hint. Everything else in this file is the operator's own to edit and is refused `-32602`, because `config.toml` is where the trust boundary is declared: `[trust] require_trusted_for_allow` is the whole of §4.2's guarded operations, `[trust] gui_requirement`/`dev_requirement` is what a peer is verified against (and is hot, so a write would land on the next connection), `[desktop] inject` is which terminals may be typed into, `[api] allowed_origins` and the two `uds_*_scope` keys are who gets in and with what, `[privacy] *` decides what of the operator's prompts and tool inputs is kept on disk, and `[voice.stt] allow_server` sends their speech off the machine. An `admin` caller that could write those would be granting itself trust rather than changing a setting. The list is a **whitelist** on purpose: a list of forbidden keys fails open the next time `[trust]` grows a field, and fails silently. A patch is applied **whole or not at all**, and the file that results is validated whole before it is published.

`voice.stt.allow_server` is the one `[voice]` key held back rather than forbidden outright, and it is a **deferral with a condition**: §7.6 requires the GUI to say, wherever that key is offered, that turning it on sends the operator's speech off the machine. The Voice screen's one settings surface is the **Spoken replies** control (2026-10-11: provider chips, a masked API-key field with Save/Remove, a model field — `voice.tts.provider`/`api_key`/`model` through `config.set`, one key per call, with the paragraph over the field saying that a key sends the announcements' text to the provider; the key is emptied from the field once saved and is read back only as `config.get`'s hint); there is still no control that could carry `allow_server`'s warning, so that key stays hand-edited until one exists. It should join `MANAGED` in the same commit that adds that control, and not before.

**Hot keys** (`config::HOT_KEYS`, applied without a restart): `api.allowed_origins`, `api.uds_default_scope`, `api.uds_max_scope`, `api.mailbox_ttl_s`, `api.max_held_hooks`, all of `[codex]`, all of `[trust]`, `privacy.log_content`, all of `[agents]` (pins included), `cards.ready_ttl_s`, `hooks.enabled`, `hooks.claude.hold_s`, `hooks.claude.settle_s`, `hooks.codex.hold_s`, `hooks.gemini.hold_s`, all of `[liveness]`, all of `[sources]`, all of `[voice]` but `voice.transport` (and, while the MLX sidecar is the synthesiser, the `[voice.tts]` keys it is started with), `log.level`, `usage.providers`. Everything else is applied to the cell and logged "restart needed", and `config.reload`/`config.set` name it under `restart_needed` rather than reporting it applied.

## 10. Packaging, install, updates, TCC, uninstall

### 10.1 Bundle layout

```
OpenWalkieTalkie.app/Contents/
  MacOS/OpenWalkieTalkie                          gpui GUI (CFBundleIdentifier ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie)
  MacOS/deckd                                  the daemon + CLI (same bundle, same signature; NOT in Resources — Apple DTS, host-research/voice-macos.md:102)
  Library/LaunchAgents/ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie.deckd.plist
        Label ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie.deckd, BundleProgram Contents/MacOS/deckd, ProgramArguments [deckd, run],
        RunAtLoad true, KeepAlive {SuccessfulExit false}, ThrottleInterval 10, ProcessType Interactive (audio latency),
        ExitTimeOut 5 — no StandardOutPath/StandardErrorPath (launchd does not expand `~`, and the plist is per-bundle,
        not per-user; `deckd run` dup2s its own log file onto fds 1/2, §9)
  Info.plist   NSMicrophoneUsageDescription, NSSpeechRecognitionUsageDescription, NSAppleEventsUsageDescription,
               NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription (CoreBluetooth: the deck's voice link, §7.9),
               LSMinimumSystemVersion 13.0 (SMAppService), LSUIElement false (dock toggled at runtime like openmicrokbd),
               SUFeedURL / SUPublicEDKey (Sparkle 2.9.x pinned by sha256 as in openmicrokbd fetch-sparkle.sh)
  Frameworks/Sparkle.framework   2.9.6, pinned by archive SHA-256, linked through the
               main-thread Objective-C bridge in packaged builds. Developer ID builds enable
               signed-feed checks and archive verification; source/ad-hoc builds disable installation.
  Resources/   licences (Sparkle's and the project's), plus verified Rev A/Rev B
               firmware images in `firmware/`. **Corrected 2026-09-22** — this line named icons,
               `lucide.ttf`, chime PCM and hook templates, and none of the four is a resource: the GUI's icons are
               SVG `const &str` built by an `icon!` macro in `openwalkietalkie-app/src/assets.rs` and live in the
               binary; the chimes are *synthesised* at the lane rate by `deckd::audio::tts::chime_pcm` and were
               never files; `assets/lucide/lucide-ui.ttf` does exist but is the **firmware's** glyph font
               (`tools/generate-ui-glyphs.py`), not the app's; and `deckd::integrations` writes its hook entries
               from code with no on-disk template. (No .mcpb either — the Claude Desktop shim is the bundle's own
               deckd registered by path, §4.16.)
```

Entitlements (hardened runtime, one `deck.entitlements` file applied to **both** Mach-Os): `com.apple.security.device.audio-input`, `com.apple.security.automation.apple-events`. No App Sandbox (UDS in Application Support, hidapi, AppleScript to Ghostty). Built by a copy of openmicrokbd's `scripts/package-macos.sh` + `release.yml` with one deliberate change: `Contents/MacOS/deckd` and `Contents/MacOS/OpenWalkieTalkie` are each signed with `codesign --options runtime --timestamp -i ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie --entitlements deck.entitlements` (the copied script has no `-i` and applies `--preserve-metadata=entitlements` only to Sparkle parts, so `deckd` would otherwise get identifier `deckd` — and TCC keys grants to the identifier/designated requirement, making the daemon's Microphone/Speech/Automation grant invalid for the GUI and vice versa, and hardened-runtime capture from `deckd` would fail even with a grant). Then the app, notarise, staple, UDZO DMG, Ed25519 appcast, `release-manifest.json` (`host-research/openmicrokbd.md:40-41`). **CI asserts** `codesign -dv` reports the same `Identifier` and `TeamIdentifier` for both binaries and that both carry the two entitlements. In the bundle `deckd` takes its identity from the enclosing app. **Corrected 2026-10-04:** the `__TEXT,__info_plist` embedding (embed_plist 1.2.2, `host/crates/deckd/Info.plist`, the bundle's usage strings word for word) is in **every** macOS `deckd`, not behind a `dev-plist` feature, and there is no `deckd install --dev`: the bare dev binary is `tools/install-host.sh`'s, built by `tools/build-host.sh` with no features, and since the voice link it touches CoreBluetooth, which ends a process with no `NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription` instead of asking. Embedding it unconditionally is harmless to the bundle because its `CFBundleIdentifier` is the same `ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie` that `codesign -i` gives both Mach-Os, and inside the bundle the app's own `Info.plist` is the one macOS reads; `tests/info_plist.rs` fails if the identifier or a usage string drifts from `packaging/macos/Info.plist.in`, or if the built binary does not carry the section. Ad-hoc dev builds re-prompt TCC on every rebuild — documented as expected (`host-research/rust-service-stack.md:97`).

### 10.2 Install and first run

1. Download the DMG, drag to `/Applications`. The GUI refuses to install hooks or register the service from a translocated or non-`/Applications` path (the absolute path of `Contents/MacOS/deckd` is embedded in three config files; `host-research/openmicrokbd.md:15`).
2. First launch → first run (§8.2; the 3-step Welcome sheet this item described went with the console on 2026-11-11). Nothing runs in the background and nothing is installed until a deck has been seen and the owner presses **Add These**: then `SMAppService.agent(plistName: "ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie.deckd.plist").register()`, a wait of up to 20 s for the service to answer (while macOS holds it for the owner's approval, **Open Login Items** goes there), and the Claude Code plugin's install through the service. **Not Now** installs nothing; the Deck page offers **Set Up…** afterwards.
3. Legacy migration (the rename from OpenMicroDial): an install from before it is adopted. `tools/install-host.sh` and `Paths::detect` move `~/Library/{Application Support,Logs,Caches}/OpenMicroDial` to `…/OpenWalkieTalkie` when the new directory does not exist yet; the Claude installer reads a legacy `_openmicrodial` inventory key and removes it on its next write; `deckd uninstall` and `tools/install-host.sh` boot out `com.conol.openmicrodial.deckd` and remove its plist, and `tools/install-host.sh` the older `com.conol.openmicrodial.icon-sync` too, so two writers never share the link.
4. Pairing is in System Settings → Bluetooth; the Deck page shows the deck linked on `device.link`. No Input Monitoring prompt: vendor usage page `ff00` does not trigger it (verified for the icon-sync agent of the time, `host-research/rust-service-stack.md:33`).

### 10.3 Updates (Sparkle → daemon restart)

Sparkle checks on launch and every 6 h and provides its standard signed download,
installation, and relaunch UI. The packaged app defaults to automatic downloads and
installation; Sparkle retains the user's preference. The status item's **Check for
Updates…** and the Service page's updates both offer manual checks. Source/ad-hoc builds do not install app updates.

On launch the app registers its bundled `SMAppService` agent. It reads
`service.info.daemon` and `executable`; only a different version at its own bundled
`deckd` path triggers `service.restart {when_idle:true}`. The daemon refuses this
restart while PTT, final recognition, TTS, or firmware work is active. The GUI retries
later. Once idle, exit **75** makes `KeepAlive {SuccessfulExit:false}` start the new
bundle's daemon; the deck receives a new `sid` and resyncs.

`firmware.status` is observe-only; `firmware.check` and `firmware.install` require
admin. Checks retrieve the stable release manifest at startup and every six hours
while the app runs. Installation is an explicit confirmation in Service → Updates,
then a press on the deck when the deck can show a card (below).
The daemon selects the board image, verifies its layout, size, SHA-256, identity,
and vector table, pauses its HID writer, and uses the USB OWTB application recovery
protocol. The selected USB serial is retained through recovery and reboot. Both
Bluetooth stores are outside the writable update ranges. Normal application
transfers also exclude the bootloader; a separately acknowledged migration can
replace it through the temporary board-specific updater.
Protocol-2 recovery supports the upper application bank through `0x12FFFFFF`;
the updater skips `0x12700000..0x1271FFFF` and verifies its image padding is FF.
An old recovery bootloader needs a one-time migration before images using upper
flash. `firmware.status.bootloader_warning` requests a power-loss warning and
`firmware.install` requires `allow_bootloader_update:true` before migration. The
release supplies a lower-bank bootstrap and recovery image per board, with hashes
and board identities checked before writes. The daemon backs up and reads back
the bootloader, verifies protocol 2, then installs normal firmware. An uncertain
bootloader write never causes an automatic reboot; retries verify or repair it
first. The `bootloader` phase is busy. See [RELEASING.md](../RELEASING.md) for the
full sequence and pending Rev B hardware gate. Legacy small images remain supported. Recovery
mode and older firmware without board identity require a board selection.

**A press on the deck (2026-11-12, the owner's answer to decision 4).** On a deck
that has said hello on its link, `firmware.install` starts nothing. Any refusal the
install itself would give comes at once; otherwise the reply is the status in the
phase `confirming` ("Press ENTER on the deck to start the install."), and the deck
shows "Install firmware {version}? Press ENTER." in its own language, with one choice,
Install, from an agent named for the firmware in that language. The card goes after
60 s. Only ENTER pressed on the deck itself starts the install: CANCEL, the 60 s
running out, words said to the card, or an answer from anywhere but the deck (an
`op.answer` from the app or a script) leave the updater idle with "Not confirmed on
the deck. Nothing was installed." A deck in recovery cannot show a card: the app asks
the owner to confirm the board, and the install starts at once, as before. A deck
that is not in recovery and has not said hello is asked to link first, and nothing
starts. After ENTER the install still waits for the voice to go quiet (no press held,
no transcription on its way, nothing being said); if it is not quiet 60 s after the
press, the install is given up with `firmware.install`'s own refusal, "Finish talking
and wait for transcription before updating." `confirming` counts as busy, like
`checking`, `downloading`, `installing`, `reconnecting` and `bootloader`: while a card
waits, no check or second install starts, and `service.restart`, `service.stop` and
`care.cache.clear` are refused. RELEASING.md does not describe the press yet.

See [RELEASING.md](../RELEASING.md) for workflows, credentials, versioning,
recovery, and the required hardware release check.

### 10.4 TCC matrix

| Grant | Needed for | Requested by | When | Never by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bluetooth | the deck's microphone and speaker over its BLE audio service (`[voice] transport = "ble"`, the default, §7.9): CoreBluetooth | the first CoreBluetooth call in `deckd`; the usage string is `NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription`, in the bundle and embedded in the bare binary (§10.1) | when the daemon starts its voice link | — (a probe run from a terminal uses the terminal's grant, V9) |
| Microphone | PTT capture from the HFP input (`transport = "coreaudio"`), and on either transport the Mac's own input (`fallback_input`, `voice.listen{input:"mac"}`) | `deckd` (`AVCaptureDevice.requestAccess`), with the GUI fallback if the launchd-spawned prompt does not surface (§7.8) | the Voice page's permissions (first run's step 3), or the first capture that needs it (`perm.changed{needed}` → GUI sheet) | MCP shim, hook helper (attributed to Claude/Terminal, `host-research/desktop-apps.md:30`) |
| Speech Recognition | `SFSpeechRecognizer` (crashes without the usage string, `host-research/voice-macos.md:54`) | `deckd` (+ GUI fallback) | with Microphone | — |
| Automation → Ghostty | focus + `input text` injection | `deckd` (a benign `tell application "Ghostty" to get name of windows`) | when `deckd` first scripts Ghostty, optional (no page of the app asks for it since 2026-11-11) | GUI |
| Accessibility | v1.1 CGEvent/AX injectors, Macropad `keys` | `deckd`, opt-in (`AXIsProcessTrustedWithOptions`) | only when such a feature is switched on | — |
| Input Monitoring | not needed for vendor-page HID over hidapi (`host-research/rust-service-stack.md:33,165`); hidapi needs no Bluetooth grant either — only the voice link above does | — | — | — |

The GUI's Grant buttons never trigger prompts themselves; they call `perm.request`, the daemon prompts, and `perm.changed` updates the screen. If a prompt cannot be shown, the daemon replies `prompt_not_shown` and the GUI requests the same grant (same bundle, same signing identifier and TeamID, §10.1 — therefore the same grant) — both paths are implemented. Week-7 gate: `tccutil reset Microphone ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie` → grant via the daemon → `perm.status` shows `granted` in the GUI and capture works; reverse (grant via the GUI, capture from the daemon); then a Sparkle update keeps the grant.

### 10.5 Diagnostics and uninstall

**Built in week 7.** Where this section and an older paragraph elsewhere disagree, this one is the one that matches the code.

- `deckd doctor [--json]` (its rows are also the Service page's **Check my setup**, `care.checks`, with two more: a launch agent left from before the rename, and the Codex client): launchd state, socket/port/token presence, port conflict owner, HID enumeration (VID/PID/usage page, open mode), CoreAudio devices with UIDs and current nominal rates, TCC status per grant, integration files (managed block present? path matches? token matches? last event?), CLI binaries + versions (`claude --version`, `codex --version`, `gemini --version`), last 50 log lines.
- **Launchd's last exit, and what it means** (`deckd::launchd`). `launchctl print gui/$UID/ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie.deckd` is the only place launchd records the last exit status and a spawn that never happened, and `doctor` reads it into a sentence rather than a number: **75** (`EX_TEMPFAIL`) is "it asked to be restarted" — `service.restart`, what a Sparkle update calls; **0** is "stopped on purpose" — `service.stop`, or a second instance finding the lock held; anything else, and any terminating signal, is a crash. Those readings hold **only** under `KeepAlive = {SuccessfulExit: false}`, which week 6 put in the installer's plist and §10.1 requires of the bundle's: under the plain `KeepAlive = true` it had before, launchd restarted the job on every exit and both codes described nothing. `launchctl print` says which is in force — `{SuccessfulExit: false}` prints `semaphores = { successful exit => 0 }`, a plain `KeepAlive` prints no `semaphores` block at all — so `doctor` reads it and says "the code says nothing, re-run tools/install-host.sh" instead of reading a meaning in. (Measured on 2026-09-22 against a throwaway agent: exit 75 → `last exit code = 75: EX_TEMPFAIL`, restarted; exit 0 → `state = not running`, stayed stopped; `SIGKILL` → `last terminating signal = Killed: 9` and **no** exit-code line; a missing program → `state = spawn scheduled`, `last exit code = 78: EX_CONFIG`, retried for ever.)
- **Dangling-agent detection**, runnable from **any** copy of the binary — a fresh download in `~/Downloads` diagnosing the registration the trashed copy left. A LaunchAgent whose `program`/`BundleProgram` no longer exists, or which is running from App Translocation, or whose own plist is gone, is the state that makes a service look installed (`launchctl print` succeeds, `SMAppService.status` is `enabled`) and never start — and nothing in our logs can report it, because the daemon never ran. `doctor` names the app that carried it and offers the `launchctl bootout` line. Both halves are covered: the loaded job (from `launchctl print`'s resolved `program`) and a plist on disk that launchd has not loaded (`Program`, `ProgramArguments[0]`, or a `BundleProgram` resolved against the enclosing `.app` when the plist sits inside one — and said to be unresolvable when it does not).
- `deckd diagnostics [--out DIR] [--stdout]` → `~/Desktop/OpenWalkieTalkie-diagnostics-<ts>.txt`, 0600: **one file the operator can send** (also the Service page's **Export Support File**, `care.support_file`). Versions, paths, the doctor reading of launchd, `config.toml`, `[privacy]`, `status.json`, `state.json`, `tokens.toml` without its hashes, the three logs (`deckd.log.<date>`, `app.log`, `deckd.launchd.log`, last 2000 lines each), `codesign -dv --entitlements -` for **both** Mach-Os of the bundle (§10.1's identity claim is half of every "the daemon did not get the grant the GUI has"), `launchctl print` for both labels, and `integrations.status`. It is a **text** file, not a zip: one file is what matters, the workspace pins every dependency and a zip crate is not among them, and plain text is the only shape in which "none of these strings is in here" is a claim the operator can check with `grep`.
  - **What it redacts, stated at the top of the file itself.** Secrets by pattern over the whole rendered text, as the last step so no section can opt out: `dk_…` and anything after `Bearer `, plus any bearer plaintext found in the CLIs' own files (read so it can be removed, never printed). The CLIs' config files are **never copied in** — they are the one place a working install certainly holds the `hooks` token — only `integrations.status`. `config.toml` values whose key names a secret. `tokens.toml`'s hashes. And, while `[privacy] log_content` is off, content **structurally**: a log line keeps its timestamp, level, target and its field *names*, with numbers and booleans (a pid, a port, a count, an exit code — what a diagnosis is made of) and nothing else, and a line with no log header at all — a panic payload, a backtrace — is dropped whole, because §11 counts a panic payload as content. That is blunter than redacting the fields we know about, deliberately: this file exists for the case where something nobody predicted got written down. With `log_content` on, the log text is carried and the header says so; the secrets are never part of that bargain.
- Uninstall (Service → Footprint → **Uninstall…**, which runs `deckd uninstall --purge --yes`, or `deckd uninstall [--purge] [--dry-run]`; the GUI says "uninstall before trashing the app"): remove the managed hook blocks and restore the backups byte for byte, boot the LaunchAgent out and remove a `~/Library/LaunchAgents` plist if one is there, boot out the pre-rename `com.conol.openmicrodial.deckd` agent and remove its plist, **keep the logs**, and tell the operator to trash the `.app` themselves. **The order is load-bearing and is the §15 exit test**: `<data>/backups/` is inside the data directory, so the CLIs are put back *before* `--purge` deletes it — a purge that ran first would throw away the only copy of the operator's `settings.json`. `--purge` asks (or `--yes`), then deletes `~/Library/Application Support/OpenWalkieTalkie/`, `~/Library/Caches/OpenWalkieTalkie/` and `~/Library/Preferences/ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie.plist`; the Keychain item and `tccutil reset All ai.cohdl.openwalkietalkie` are **printed, not run** — a CLI that silently revokes a microphone grant is one nobody can undo. `SMAppService.unregister()` for the agent and the `mainApp` login item stays the GUI's call and the command says so rather than pretending a `launchctl bootout` is the same thing.
- `docs/deckd-api.md` is **generated** from `GET /v1/schema` (`deckd api-doc`) and cannot drift: `deckd`'s `the_checked_in_api_document_is_what_the_schema_produces` compares the checked-in file with the generator's output and its failure message is the command that regenerates it, and `api::http`'s door test asserts that what the endpoint serves is the object the generator was handed.

## 11. Security and privacy

- **Bind**: `127.0.0.1` only (`[api] bind` is not configurable); UDS dir 0700, socket 0600; `Host` allowlist; `Origin` present-and-not-allowlisted → 403 (browser CSRF / WebSocket hijack, `host-research/rust-service-stack.md:57`; MCP MUST, `:61`); tokens only in `Authorization` or `session.hello`, never in query strings; `GET /v1/info` leaks only versions, capability names and paths; body cap 1 MiB; 64 concurrent held hooks.
- **Threat model** (D19): the daemon supervises processes that run as its own uid and that the operator has put in "full autopilot" (`host-research/spec.md:21`). Same uid is therefore *not* trust. The powers the permission prompt exists to withhold from an agent — approving its own tool call, and typing into a terminal — are held back from every principal except the deck (`device`) and the GUI verified by code signature (§4.2). The blast radius of a compromised or merely over-eager agent is: register itself, set `custom` cards on its own agent, speak on its own lane, read redacted state.
- **Trusted principal verification**: UDS peer → v1 `LOCAL_PEERPID` → `SecCodeCopyGuestWithAttributes(kSecGuestAttributePid)` → `SecCodeCheckValidity` against `[trust] gui_requirement` → `proc_pidpath` ends in `/Contents/MacOS/OpenWalkieTalkie`, re-checked right after the validity call (the pid-reuse window is documented, Q26); v1.1 swaps the pid for the race-free `LOCAL_PEERTOKEN` audit token once verified on the macOS 27 SDK. `deckd ctl` shares the signature but not the path, so it is untrusted by design; `[trust] dev_requirement` is the only loophole and it logs at `warn` on every hello.
- **Guarded operations**: permission/elicitation allows, `inject` orders and `voice.listen{ctx:"answer"}` on a permission/elicitation card from untrusted principals → `-32002 {need:"trusted"}` + `op.confirm_requested`; the GUI sheet (optionally Touch ID) re-issues them. Held hooks always fail **open to the terminal** (a release is "no decision", never an auto-allow). Deny/snooze are never guarded.
- **Voice is not a side door**: the answer or order a `voice.listen` produces is attributed to the caller of `voice.listen`, never to `device`; only a `ptt` line that arrived on the HID link is `device`. So the `mcp` token (`agent,voice`, in the agent's own MCP session) cannot `voice.say("yes")` on the deck or Mac speakers and `voice.listen(ctx:answer, input:mac)` its own permission card: the listen is refused before capture, and even a transcript obtained some other way would be an untrusted allow → `-32002`.
- **Ancestry defence in depth**: a UDS peer whose ppid chain contains `claude`/`codex`/`gemini`/`node` is capped at `observe,agent,voice,hooks:ingest` — catches the accidental case (an agent's script poking the socket) without pretending to catch a determined one; the guarded operations above are what actually hold. `hooks:ingest` is in the cap because `deckd hook` is always agent-descended and the scope grants nothing an agent could not already do by running the CLI's own hooks.
- **Sessions as the ownership unit**: on the UDS every local process is the same uid, so ownership is `{principal, session}` (§4.2). Two scripts, or the GUI and a script, cannot share or steal an agent by accident: a live key conflicts (`-32004`) and `agent.claim` after lease expiry is the only takeover. A token's agents are likewise per logical session `(token, client.name)`: the `mcp` token can only touch agents its own session registered, which is now enforceable because the check compares session ids, not uids.
- **Tokens**: 32 random bytes base64url with a `dk_` prefix, blake3-hashed at rest, scoped, revocable, optional expiry, shown once, **never written in plaintext by the daemon**; `token.rotate hooks` rewrites the managed blocks atomically; `integrations.status` flags `token_mismatch` after 401s. The `hooks` token in `~/.claude/settings.json` is readable by every session of that uid, so it carries **`hooks:ingest` only** — it can post hook payloads to the adapters and nothing else; it cannot clear, release, reply for, wait on or speak as any agent (the adapter owns them, not the token). On `hooks_hijack_risk` the token is rotated by `integrations.repair` (v1.1; in v1 the operator rotates it from the Integrations card).
- **Card integrity**: choices carry no hidden `value`; an order derived from a card choice is exactly the label the operator saw and is delivered only `push`/`poll` to the card's owner, never typed into a terminal; `origin.kind` `permission`/`question`/`elicitation` and `deliver:"hold"` are reserved for the built-in adapters, everything else is `custom` and visibly badged (`» ` on the deck).
- **Scopes as privacy** (§4.2-4.3): hook payloads reach only `observe:content`/`operate` principals and the owning adapter; prompt- and tool-derived titles are content; `observe:raw` is a deliberate tick; the ring is redacted at fan-out; `deck_list` returns name/kind/state/slot only; `[scripts] on_event` is a subscriber with `scope = "observe"` by default and receives the same redacted view.
- **Retention**: tool inputs dropped when the card resolves, prompts after title derivation, nothing content-bearing on disk unless `log_content`/`transcript_log` — including device shadow lines, `device.raw`, panic payloads (redacting panic hook) and prompt/tool titles in logs and diagnostics; STT audio is never written; cloud STT/TTS are off by default and need an explicit engine plus a Keychain item.
- **Rate limits** apply to **external principals only** (`uid:*`, `token:*`): `agent.register` 30/min, `agent.update` 10/s/agent, `voice.say` 4/s, `op.order` 2/s, hook ingestion 50/s, `[scripts] on_event` 20/s → `-32008`. Adapter-originated updates (`adapter:*`, e.g. a `PostToolUse` storm driving titles) are never rejected: the core coalesces them keep-latest per field and the outbox bucket enforces the wire's ≤4/s/slot — an adapter erroring instead of coalescing would lose titles.
- **Injection safety** (§5.5): only operator-originated text from a trusted principal; refused while a permission/question card is open for that agent or Claude reports `waitingFor` (typed keys would confirm the highlighted dialog choice); refused unless the CLI pid is alive and is the foreground process group of its tty (otherwise Return runs the text as a shell command); order text is sent literally (`input text`), Enter is a separate `send key`, never through a shell; control characters rejected; the exact string is logged to the order history before it is typed; `[scripts]` commands and Macropad `shell` actions run as the user and the GUI shows a fingerprint of the command on first run (Gemini's pattern).
- **Port squatting** (§4.1): a foreign listener on 47201 receives hook payloads and the `hooks` token and stalls Claude for the hook timeout; detected by the `/v1/info` probe, surfaced as a banner naming the listener (tray alert in v1.1), mitigated by changing the port and re-installing (v1) and by pausing the managed http hooks and rotating the token (v1.1).
- **Supply chain**: `Cargo.lock` committed, `--locked` builds, Sparkle Ed25519 appcast, notarised DMG, GitHub attestations (openmicrokbd CI).
- **No AX scraping, no `CGWindowList` titles (Screen Recording), no pasteboard reads** for state (`host-research/desktop-apps.md:57`).

## 12. Failure modes and recovery

| Failure | Detection | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| Daemon crash | launchd `KeepAlive` | Restart ≤10 s; new `sid` → resync from the freshly projected `DeviceView`; device shows `offline` only if the gap exceeds 15 s; held hooks end with the connection → Claude shows its own dialog; agents re-registered by the next hook event or `agent.claim`; orphans grace 300 s; `[liveness] seed` (on by default) re-seeds Claude sessions from `claude agents --json` — with the inbox socket from `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` and the last reply from the transcript tail — and `deckd::codexd` re-lists Codex threads from the app-server daemon |
| Held hook's client goes away (Claude's own timeout, Ctrl-C, connection reset) | the parked handler future is dropped → drop guard sends `HoldGone{agent, card}` | Hold released at once (no decision), card degraded to `⌨ Focus terminal` under the same id immediately — not after `hold_s`; Retry on such a card is refused (`hold_gone`, §4.8) |
| Core → hid channel overflow (hook storm) | `try_send` fails | Op dropped, `needs_resync` set; when the channel drains below half the core re-emits `project(&Snapshot)` + `Sync{reason:"overflow"}` and the hid thread writes a barrier from its `DeviceView`; `metrics.get.outbox_overflows` |
| Hid → core: slow core | never blocks | Unbounded channel; device edges are wire-limited (≥4 ms apart), the actor reads device lines before API commands |
| Device link drop / sleep / out of range | hidapi removal callback, read error, `Ok(0)` streak | `Absent` (§6.3): release keys, abort PTT, `answering` cards → `failed{link}`, lanes held 30 s then flushed; re-enumerate every 800 ms; resync on return |
| Hello replies missing | 2 s per hello, 3 misses | `Degraded`: handle kept, hello continues; a late reply returns to `Live`/`Syncing`; reopen HID only on I/O error or 3 further misses (§6.3); GUI banner |
| Firmware queue overflow | drop-after-burst signature ×2 in 5 min | pace 10 ms for 10 min; `error` event; sync order unchanged; `deckd device flood 2000` verifies against hardware |
| `err range` after `card`/`reply` | correlation window | re-send `agent` then the line once; then `failed{device}` |
| `err size/parse/unknown` | counter | bug: log the shadow line; `unknown` for an enum → feature-flag that field off for this `fw` |
| Answer delivery > 4 s or error | timer | no `card clear`; for a chosen answer the device reverts + "not delivered" toast; for a PTT `ctx:answer` (which the device does not lock as "sent") the daemon speaks "not delivered to <name>" on the `Question` lane and badges the GUI card; store `failed`; GUI Retry (push/poll with a live owner session) / Release (any). A `poll` owner that shows up late picks the answer up from the mailbox and the daemon then sends `card clear` → `delivered`; the entry is dropped if the operator answers again first (§4.8) |
| Hook fires while the daemon is down | helper connect timeout (500 ms) | `deckd hook` prints `{}` and exits 0 for both CLIs; the operator loses the deck for that prompt, never the terminal |
| Held hook exceeds the operator's patience | — | CANCEL/snooze releases it; `hold_s` cap → `degraded` card; GUI "Release to terminal" |
| Hook arrives for an unknown session (daemon restarted mid-session, or the session started first) | no `SessionStart` seen | create the agent from the payload (`session_id`, `cwd`); with the plugin every event is a command hook, so the terminal and the inbox come with it; `[liveness] seed` registers Claude sessions the roster lists before any hook arrives (inbox from `sessions/<pid>.json`, last reply from the transcript); Codex threads come from the app-server daemon regardless |
| Duplicate/late hooks (two settings levels, redelivery) | idempotent keys; `turn_id` for Codex, and for Claude `(session_id, tool_name, blake3(tool_input))` in a 5 s window — the event has no `tool_use_id` (§5.1) | no-op, `rev` unchanged; two principals for one key → `-32004` logged once, Integrations shows "duplicate hook config" |
| Owner disappears with a card pending | lease expiry / owning session closed | `push` → `focus` if the agent has an adapter-resolved terminal, else `none`; `poll` answers wait in the mailbox for `mailbox_ttl_s` (a reconnecting session `agent.claim`s and `agent.wait`s them); device "not delivered" on answer; GUI "agent gone"; card removed with the agent after grace |
| Port 47201 taken, nothing answering | bind error, `/v1/info` probe fails | UDS + device keep working; `service.degraded{port_in_use}`; hooks fail open (connection refused → Claude proceeds); GUI one-click change port + re-install hooks (D8) |
| Port 47201 taken by a foreign listener | bind error, `/v1/info` probe answers but is not ours | `hooks_hijack_risk`: v1 shows the banner and the listener (`lsof`) and offers change port → `integrations.repair`; v1.1 adds the tray alert, "Pause hooks now" (managed http entries removed, backups kept) and token rotation inside `repair`. Held hooks would block Claude for 130 s and payloads + `hooks` token go to the squatter until the operator acts |
| `inject` while a dialog is open / CLI not foreground | card state, `claude agents --json`, `tcgetpgrp` | `-32006 undeliverable{dialog_open\|not_foreground}`; nothing typed; order kept on the GUI with Copy; TTS "no route to <name>" |
| App trashed before uninstall | `deckd doctor` from any copy, or launchd retry loop | SMAppService entry with a missing `BundleProgram` detected → `unregister`; documented "uninstall before trashing" |
| Card id collision after restart | — (prevented) | ids persisted per slot, next = `last + 1` (u16 wrap skipping 0), no random base; shadow/counter reset on `AgentGone` |
| Two sessions register the same key | live lease | `-32004 conflict{owner:{principal,session}}` for the second, whoever it runs as; `agent.claim` only after the lease expires; GUI shows the owner |
| Hook token mismatch / path drift | 401s, `current_exe` check | Integrations `token_mismatch` / `path_drift` → Repair |
| Mic or speech denied | TCC status / silence | `perm.changed`; PTT sends `transcript final:1` with "(microphone not allowed)"; GUI sheet |
| SCO never comes up (legacy Classic) | Arming 1.5 s without samples | `voice.state{error}`, deck transcript "(no mic)" |
| A2DP does not resume after PTT (legacy Classic; macOS bug, `host-research/voice-macos.md:96`) | `Reopening` ×2 timeout | `ScoStuck`: play at 16 k; GUI "audio route stuck — reconnect the deck"; recovers on the next rate change |
| Codex app owns the mic concurrently | nominal-rate listener | `ScoExternal`: hold lanes; no capture conflict |
| Deck audio absent (HID only) | UID lookup | `fallback_output`/`fallback_input` policy; deck Settings › Host still says connected (audio is not part of `hello`) |
| Ghostty Automation denied / terminal ambiguous | AppleScript `-1743` / resolver | injector skips / `order.failed{ambiguous}`; GUI prompt to pick once |
| Config invalid on reload | validation | keep the previous config; `.invalid` copy; `error` event; GUI banner |
| `state.json` corrupt | parse | rename `.corrupt`, start empty; faces re-adopted on the next `boot:0` |
| Two daemons | `flock` | second exits 0 "already running"; `deckd status` reports the owner pid |
| Sparkle update mid-PTT | `service.restart` waits ≤3 s | utterance finalised first, then exit 75 |
| GUI disconnected | UDS/WS closed | yellow bar with Start (SMAppService) and launchd log; last mirror greyed |
| Slot pressure (>8 agents) | register | unslot the longest-idle `idle` agent, never one with a pending card; GUI "not on deck" rows |
| Device clock wrong after DST | hello every 5 s carries `hm` | self-healing |
## 13. Linux / Omarchy port plan (designed for now, built in v2)

**As built (2026-11-12): Linux with the Omarchy bridge is a supported target** (the owner's answer to decision 1). What ships differs from the plan below, which is kept as the design. `deckd` runs on Linux as a systemd user service (`host/omarchy/systemd/deckd.service`, installed by `host/omarchy/install.sh`, with `DECKD_DATA_DIR=~/.local/share/openwalkietalkie`); its HID is hidapi's default hidraw backend; its voice is the deck's BLE audio service through BlueZ (`ble::bluez`, on `bluer`), recognised by the local Whisper model and spoken by a speech program (`espeak-ng` unless `[voice.tts] command` names another); and there is no window. The Omarchy face, Hyprland, the sessions' windows and the orders typed into them belong to the Python bridge (`host/omarchy/`, a second user service), a client of the public socket API: `device.ws.set`, the `device.ws`, `device.key` and `device.link` events, and the `agent.*` methods. CI's "Linux host" job builds the host workspace for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, runs the tests of every crate but the Mac app, and runs the bridge's Python tests; `host/omarchy/README.md` is the user-facing description. The socket is `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/openwalkietalkie/deckd.sock`, else `${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-~/.cache}/openwalkietalkie/deckd.sock` (`config::Paths::detect`); the Omarchy unit's `DECKD_DATA_DIR` makes it `~/.local/share/openwalkietalkie/deckd.sock`. **On Linux the ancestry cap and the trusted principal are not implemented** (§4.2; `trust.rs` reads a peer's signature, path and ancestry through macOS calls, and off macOS finds none): every same-user process, one started by Claude Code or Codex included, may ask the socket for `admin` (`config.set`, `integrations.*`, `care.*`, `token.*`), and no client is ever trusted, so a guarded allow from one is refused `-32002`. A firmware install still needs the press on the deck (§10.3), unless the deck is already in recovery.

The API, envelope, adapters, card model, session state machine and outbox are platform-independent. The platform seams are exactly five modules with `cfg(target_os)` halves: `hid` (backend), `audio::route` + engines, `desktop`, `service`, `integrations` paths.

| Area | macOS v1 | Linux (v2) |
|---|---|---|
| HID | hidapi IOKit, non-exclusive | hidapi `linux-native` (hidraw) + udev rule `KERNEL=="hidraw*", ATTRS{idVendor}=="1209", ATTRS{idProduct}=="5754", MODE="0660", TAG+="uaccess"`; same thread/outbox code |
| Service | SMAppService / launchd | `systemd --user` unit `openwalkietalkie-deckd.service` (`Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec=2`, `WantedBy=graphical-session.target`); no TCC |
| Paths | `~/Library/Application Support/OpenWalkieTalkie/` | `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/openwalkietalkie/config.toml`, `$XDG_STATE_HOME/openwalkietalkie/{state.json,logs}`, `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/openwalkietalkie/{deckd.sock,deckd.lock,port}`, else `${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-~/.cache}/openwalkietalkie/` for those three (as built, `config::Paths::detect`); peer creds via `SO_PEERCRED` (same tokio API) |
| Audio | CoreAudio HAL by UID | PipeWire (`pipewire-rs`): select nodes by `api.bluez5.address`; HFP via the BlueZ/WirePlumber profile switch (`headset-head-unit` ↔ `a2dp-sink`) driven **explicitly** around PTT instead of implicitly by IO start; mixer unchanged (f32 lanes); mSBC capture must be verified first |
| STT / TTS | Apple Speech / AVSpeech | `whisper-rs` small.en (finals + sliding partials) or Parakeet via `sherpa-onnx`; TTS via Piper/Kokoro — behind the existing traits |
| Roster source | hooks | hooks (same) **plus** `[desktop.hyprland] title_agents = true`: windows whose title matches the Claude/Codex/Gemini patterns (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:105`) are registered as virtual agents owned by the Hyprland adapter with `caps.answer:none`, so a machine with no hooks installed still gets the roster the DHH demo needs (`host-research/spec.md:29,39`) |
| Focus both ways | Ghostty AppleScript out; in = v1.1 | `.socket2.sock` events `activewindow`/`windowtitle` → `focus.report` → device `focus`; device `channel` → `hyprctl dispatch focuswindow address:0x…` matched by the pid tree from the helper's resolved CLI pid or by title (`host-research/critic.md:26`) |
| Omarchy face | degraded `ws` | real `ws` from `hyprctl workspaces -j`/`clients -j` (active, count, ≤6 windows coloured by agent state); device `key` → `hyprctl dispatch` per the spec's key map (`host-research/spec.md:98-106`: RIGHT/LEFT → `workspace e+1/e-1`, wheel → `cyclenext`, ENTER → `exec $terminal`, SHIFT+ENTER → scratchpad, SHIFT+CANCEL → screenshot); `ws go/win` → `workspace N` / `focuswindow`; channel binds 1–4 ↔ `Ctrl+Alt+Shift+1..4` shipped as Hyprland binds |
| Injection | Ghostty AppleScript | `wtype`/`ydotool` or Hyprland `sendshortcut`; tmux `send-keys -l` when `TMUX_PANE` is known |
| GUI | gpui Metal | gpui Wayland; tray via the GTK thread pattern (`host-research/openmicrokbd.md:27`) |
| Packaging | DMG | Omarchy module `omarchy-deck` (PKGBUILD): `deckd`, the GUI, the unit, the udev rule, default Hyprland binds, `config.toml` with `title_agents = true`, hook installers; `deckd integrations install omarchy` writes the bind snippet (`host-research/spec.md:133`) |

Unverified and scheduled first in the port: BlueZ/PipeWire HFP mSBC capture from Rust, hidraw hotplug from a user service, Hyprland `windowtitle` event rate (title spinners) vs the 4/s coalescer.

## 14. Testing strategy

| Layer | What | How |
|---|---|---|
| Wire contract | Every host line the daemon can emit survives the firmware's own assembler at every chunk size; device lines parse; per-edge sizes | `deck-proto` `#[path]`-includes `src/deck/proto.rs`/`json.rs`/`msg.rs`; a `tests/host_contract.rs` in the workspace reuses the fixtures and helpers of `tests/host/deck_protocol.rs` (`every_message_survives_framing_and_reassembly_at_every_chunk_size`, `several_lines_share_one_report_and_one_line_straddles_several`, `other_channels_interleaved_at_report_granularity_are_ignored`, `an_overlong_line_is_reported_once_and_the_next_line_is_intact`, `every_per_edge_event_fits_one_report_with_its_lf`) against the daemon's encoder and chunker |
| Framing parity | The daemon's reports are byte-identical to the reference host's | `tools/deck-sim.py --dry-run --send '<json>'` prints framed reports and runs them back through a copy of the device's assembler (P:194-196); a `deckd device raw --dry-run` mode prints the same layout; CI diffs both on the mockup session |
| Pure state machines | Session SM, `DeviceView` folding + outbox lanes/aging/shadow/**barrier ordering**, card status machine incl. the `failed` exits, delivery modes incl. `focus` and the late `poll` pickup, hold/snooze/degrade/`HoldGone` re-push, question groups and the group `hold_s` budget, truncation (v1) / paging (v1.1), session ownership (conflict, claim, HTTP logical sessions), lease/orphan/claim, slot policy, card-id allocation, PTT SM incl. principal attribution, route SM, intent grammar, redaction | `deck-model` unit tests with a simulated clock via `Command::Tick` (≥80 tests), every effect asserted as data; property tests: "a `card` is never written before its `agent` in a barrier", "`synced` is always last", "a barrier is exactly the ordered `DeviceView`", "a `Put` during a barrier to an unwritten key is dumped once, not twice", "no title line exceeds 4/s/slot", "no line > 1000 B", "an unsent card followed by its clear emits nothing", "**every intermediate group answer is cleared or replaced within 4 s**", "no card has more than 4 choices on the wire", "a `focus` card is never re-sent after its clear", "a snooze re-push keeps the card id", "no card id repeats on a slot across a restart (`last+1`, wrap skips 0)", "`focus` is in the barrier only after a PC-side focus", "a late poll pickup emits exactly one `card clear` and a second answer drops the mailbox entry", "two sessions, one key → `-32004`; a `voice.listen{ctx:answer}` from an untrusted session never reaches `Arming`" |
| Hook fixtures | Every adapter row of §5 | Recorded JSON payloads per event per CLI under `tests/fixtures/hooks/<cli>/<event>.json` (from real runs, content scrubbed), replayed through `Adapter::ingest`; golden `Ingest` values; the sequential AskUserQuestion group, **`PermissionRequest{tool_name:"AskUserQuestion"}` → `{}` with no card, and the same for 30 s after a released group (the event carries no `tool_use_id`, §5.1)**, the >4-option truncation, `Allow always` only with suggestions, `turn_id` dedupe, `Notification permission_prompt` suppression, `terminalSequence` at top level with ESC/BEL framing; **`deckd hook` parent-walk fixtures for both spawn forms** (exec: parent is `claude`; shell: parent is `sh -c` whose parent is `claude`) and for an unknown ancestry (`pid_kind:"unknown"`, no liveness polling); a held handler whose future is dropped sends `HoldGone` and the card degrades at once |
| API | Scopes, redaction (incl. prompt/tool titles), envelope, errors, `since` replay, `gap`, Origin/Host checks, rate limits (external only; adapter storms coalesce), `agent.wait` long-poll, held-hook release on snooze, **sessions**: the same uid on two UDS connections gets two session ids and a key conflict; an HTTP client with `client.name` keeps one logical session across `POST /v1/rpc` calls and `push` caps are `-32009` for it; **trust**: guarded operations from a token / plain UDS peer / agent-descended peer → `-32002` + `op.confirm_requested`, including `voice.listen{ctx:"answer"}` on a permission card from the `mcp` token; `deckd hook` (agent-descended) is granted `hooks:ingest`; the `hooks:ingest` token cannot call `agent.*`; a `custom` card cannot claim `origin.kind:"permission"`; `agent.focus` works for the owner without `operate`; the UDS rejects a non-`hello` first line and an HTTP request line | integration tests over an in-memory UDS (NDJSON) and axum TCP with three tokens and a fake peer-verifier; a browser-CSRF test (foreign `Origin` → 403); `GET /v1/schema` validated against every fixture; the 20-line Python virtual agent over TCP (register with `client.name` → card → `agent.wait` → clear) run in CI |
| Packaging | Signing identity and entitlements | CI runs `codesign -dv --entitlements -` on both Mach-Os and asserts equal `Identifier`/`TeamIdentifier` and the two entitlements; a plist lint asserts no `StandardOutPath`/`StandardErrorPath` |
| Voice fixtures | STT feeding, finals, grammar; mixer priorities, ducking, cut timing; route SM | `tests/fixtures/audio/*.wav` at 16 k (start from the a 16 kHz mono TTS sample) through a `FakeSttEngine` that returns scripted partials; the mixer driven by a fake render callback (512-frame quanta) asserting "PTT cut reaches −60 dB within 35 ms", "Question ducks Chatter to −18 dB", "Ready held across a hold"; route SM driven by scripted nominal-rate events incl. the external-SCO and stuck cases |
| GUI reducer | Mirror folding, gap → `state.get`, effects | `HostState::detached()` with scripted `ApiEvent`s (openmicrokbd's 20-test pattern); the tokio bridge is exercised by one test that round-trips `ApiClient::call` through a fake UDS server on the runtime thread |
| Hardware gate (manual, checklist in `docs/deck-device-checklist.md` extended with host items) | Week 1: hello/sync/heartbeat against the real deck; **30-min title-storm soak** (`deckd device flood`) with no link drop; sid resync after a device reboot; non-seizing HID soak (both clients, both channels, under load) | `deckd doctor --json` before and after; `metrics.get` captured |
| Hardware gate, later weeks | answer → `card clear` < 1 s from the deck; snooze releases the terminal dialog and the drawer entry becomes `⌨ Focus terminal` under the same id (no chime); a 3-question AskUserQuestion answered card by card with no "not delivered"; ENTER on a degraded card focuses Ghostty and the card clears; SCO bring-up / A2DP resume timings logged (week 4); PTT → Ghostty injection, **refused with a permission dialog open and with the CLI exited to a shell prompt** (week 5); TCC identity round trip daemon↔GUI and across a Sparkle update (week 7); 8 h and 24 h soaks with link drops and sleep/wake; every row of §12 exercised | recorded in the checklist |

## 15. Milestones

### v1 — 8 weeks, one experienced Rust engineer, macOS, cut scope

The reviewers priced the full design at 13–15 weeks (week 1 alone held the shared framing, all of `deck-model`, the hid thread and three hardware spikes; week 2 the core, the whole API surface and the Security.framework FFI; weeks 4 and 6–7 were each 3+ weeks of work). v1 therefore keeps what carries the real risk — the **Codex-only regression**, the **held-hook spike** and the **TCC gate** — and defers everything the deck does not need on day one (D18): MCP server + stdio shim + Claude Desktop, the Gemini adapter, card paging (truncate to 4 + PTT free text instead), `[scripts] on_event`, Macropad actions, the degraded Omarchy `ws`, usage feeders beyond the Claude statusLine, `device.raw`, the hijack pause/rotate flows (banner + change port stay), `report_via = "command"`, and the audit-token trust path (`LOCAL_PEERPID` + `kSecGuestAttributePid` first). Every deferred item is designed above so the v1 API does not change when it lands.

| Week | Deliverable | Exit test / acceptance |
