Two clauses the suite did not hold are now pinned by tests that fail when they are deleted. **Soak: 24 minutes**, not 8 h/24 h — zero link drops, zero decode/encode/parse/range/size errors, zero write stalls, zero overflows, `ch2_reports` 0 → 38 with a second HID client attached. **Not run:** the 8 h, 24 h, sleep/wake and 8-agent eviction soaks. **Open questions:** 1 answered (week 3's S1 — holding the hook does *not* suppress the terminal dialog; a held hook survives 130 s), 2 answered with numbers (first non-zero sample 838–883 ms, A2DP resume 2344–2411 ms), 3 answered for its second half only (no channel-2 starvation, 38 reports), **5 still open** — `perm.request` is stubbed and a TCC prompt needs a person at the keyboard. |

v1 acceptance, in the user's words: "the deck works with my terminals" — roster within 100 ms, questions answered from the deck with a truthful clear, replies announced in the agent's voice, PTT orders typed into Ghostty, a script can be an agent, `curl` can do what the GUI does, install/update/uninstall are one click each.

Explicitly **out** of v1 (built later): the D18 deferrals above; Codex app-server, Claude channels, Elicitation, tmux/CGEvent/AX injection, inbound tab-level focus sync, desk-presence hold shortening, Macropad `keys`, cloud engines shipped, LLM summaries, Linux, ChatGPT, broadcast, streaming TTS.

### v1.1 (≈6–8 weeks)

First the deferred design (all specified above): MCP server at `/mcp` + `deckd mcp --stdio` + the Claude Desktop registration and `mcp` token (§4.16, with the modern-era identity rule; client compatibility tested against Claude Code / Codex / Gemini, Q21); Gemini adapter (§5.3) with the `BeforeTool` spike; card paging (§5.6); `[scripts] on_event`; Macropad actions with face-name layout binding; degraded Omarchy `ws`; Codex/Gemini usage feeders; `device.raw`; hijack tray alert + "Pause hooks now" + token rotation in `repair`; `report_via = "command"`; the `LOCAL_PEERTOKEN` audit-token trust path (Q26); the remaining GUI (Device, full Voice/Service, Settings, About, Touch ID confirm, per-agent tray rows, 5-step Welcome). Then: Codex app-server attach (`deckd codex` wrapper; approvals incl. `Allow session`, `requestUserInput`, `turn/steer`, streaming replies, `account/rateLimits` usage) after the two-subscriber spike; Claude channels on the same MCP server (`deckd claude` wrapper, permission relay — terminal and deck both live, first answer wins); Claude `Elicitation` cards; tmux and CGEvent injectors + iTerm2/Terminal.app; Ghostty `selected tab` poll → `focus.report`; `[hooks.claude] desk_presence` (adaptive 2 s hold while the operator is active at the Mac) after the `CGEventSourceSecondsSinceLastEventType` spike; Macropad `keys` (Accessibility); `phone` token profile + documented Tailscale `serve` recipe; Claude Desktop AX injection (experimental, opt-in).

Acceptance: Claude Desktop Chat `deck_ask` answered from the deck and a `deck_done` step arriving on `deck_wait` as its label; a Codex TUI launched through the wrapper shows choice questions and takes `turn/steer` orders from PTT; a wrapped Claude session receives orders natively with no Ghostty dependency; a phone WS client shows the roster over Tailscale with an `observe,operate,voice` token.

### v2

Linux/Omarchy port (§13) and the DHH module; daemon-hosted sessions (`claude -p --input-format stream-json --permission-prompt-tool stdio`, `gemini --acp`, ACP adapters) launched from the GUI's "New agent"; streaming TTS from `MessageDisplay`/`agentMessage/delta`; SpeechTranscriber (Swift helper) or Parakeet for zh-CN/accuracy; cloud STT/TTS shipped; optional external LLM summariser sample client using `agent.reply{announce}`; ChatGPT via Secure MCP Tunnel; SHIFT+PTT broadcast only if the one-channel discipline proves too strict (`host-research/spec.md:136`).

## 16. Open questions, each with a recommended default

1. **Does holding a Claude `PermissionRequest`/`PreToolUse` http hook suppress the terminal dialog, and does `updatedInput.answers` answer AskUserQuestion interactively?** (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:233-234`, `host-research/critic.md:116`) — Default: assume yes (the docs imply it); week-1 spike on the installed build. If the dialog is *not* suppressed, switch to "first answer wins" and drop the hold timeout; if `updatedInput` fails interactively, AskUserQuestion cards become informational (`⌨ Focus terminal`) until confirmed. *Answered 2026-09-18 (Status, spikes S1/S2): the dialog is not suppressed → "first answer wins"; `updatedInput.answers` works interactively → question cards stay real.*
2. **SCO bring-up latency and lost first syllables; A2DP resume time on macOS 27** (`host-research/voice-macos.md:182`, `host-research/critic.md:114`) — Default: ship `mic_warm = "never"`; measure in week 4 (press timestamp vs first non-silent buffer; release vs nominal 44 100); switch the default to `"card_pending"` if bring-up > 300 ms; if A2DP resume > 1.5 s, add `[voice] speak_over_sco = true` to play the post-release reply at 16 k instead of waiting. *Answered 2026-09-21 (week 4, n=16 on macOS 27.0 (26A428), arm64, against the connected deck): bring-up — press → first **non-zero** mic sample — is **1031 ms median** (815 min, 1246 max); A2DP resume — close input → output nominal back to 44100 — is **2307 ms median** (2281–2447). **Both thresholds are blown, so both defaults flip**: `[voice] mic_warm = "card_pending"` and `[voice] speak_over_sco = true` (§7.1, §7.5, §9). Two riders came with the numbers. The first input **callback** arrives at ~28 ms with about a second of exact digital zeros behind it, so the question "has the mic produced audio?" may never be answered by counting buffers — §7.4's liveness check and §7.5's `slow_sco_ms` both key off the first non-zero sample. And the 2.3 s resume is a fixed macOS SCO-teardown timer that cannot be hurried, which is why `Reopening` opens at the device's current rate instead of waiting for it (§7.2).*
3. **Does IOKit deliver every input report to both non-seizing clients (Codex app + deckd), and can our poll starve channel 2?** (`host-research/critic.md:115`) — Default: week-1 soak with the Codex app open; if reports are split, `deckd` cannot proxy channel 2 (the app opens IOKit itself), so document "Codex face and deck host are exclusive on macOS" and add a GUI toggle that pauses the deck link — a v1 blocker to learn early. *Answered 2026-09-18 (Status, spike S3): every report reaches every client, no toggle; the BLE-acknowledged synchronous write is the real pacer on macOS. Moot since the Codex Micro emulation was removed.*
4. **Codex app-server request routing with two subscribers** (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:236`) — Default: v1 uses hooks only; a one-day spike with `codex --remote unix://` at the start of v1.1 gates the app-server work.
5. **Do TCC prompts triggered from the launchd-spawned daemon surface on macOS 27, or must the GUI request Microphone/Speech?** (`host-research/voice-macos.md:185`) — Default: both paths implemented (§7.8); daemon-first, GUI fallback after 5 s. *Still open after week 4 (V9, 2026-09-21): the week-4 probes ran from a terminal and a plain ad-hoc-signed binary with no `Info.plist` opened the microphone and got real audio, because `responsibility_get_pid_responsible_for_pid` attributed it to the terminal app (measured: `Zed.app`, whose plist carries `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription`). A launchd-spawned `deckd` is its own responsible process with its own TCC row and inherits none of that, so **nothing measured in week 4 says anything about the daemon's grant path**. Finding out means installing a LaunchAgent, which is a system change and needs its own authorised spike; §10.4 already puts it in the week-7 gate, and `perm.request` is stubbed at the prompt until then.*
6. **Gemini `BeforeTool` allow/deny bypassing the interactive prompt** (`host-research/agent-cli-hooks.md:8`) — Default: `permission = "notify"` (informational cards); the spike runs with the v1.1 Gemini adapter and flips the default.
7. **`CGEventSourceSecondsSinceLastEventType` without Accessibility on macOS 27** — Default: not load-bearing in v1 (desk presence is v1.1); fallback is the GUI reporting its own last-input time plus `NSWorkspace` activation events.
8. **Title source per kind** (`host-research/critic.md:117`) — Default: hook-derived (`session_name` / prompt head / thread name), never window titles on macOS; `tool_titles = true` if operators find Claude titles stale; the spec's "verbatim window title" is honoured on Linux via `title_agents`.
9. **Summarisation for announcements / next steps** (`host-research/critic.md:118`) — Default: heuristic (D14); an external client sets `announce` via `agent.reply` — documented as the extension point; `[summarize]` in-daemon never.
10. **multiSelect, >4 options, `isOther`** (`host-research/critic.md:119`) — Default: §5.6 (single pick; v1 truncates to 4 with the recommended option first and PTT free text; v1.1 pages with the repeated-chime cost; comma-separated labels accepted by PTT for multi-select); `metrics.get.cards_truncated` in v1 and `cards_paged` in v1.1 decide whether a firmware "more" card is worth it.
11. **Voice-command grammar scope and languages** (`host-research/critic.md:120`) — Default: exact-match `report|status|stop|quiet` on `talk` and the allow/deny intent on permission cards, English only, `[voice] commands` switch; no NLU in v1.
12. **Codex "Allow always/for session" via hooks** (`host-research/critic.md:121`) — Default: Allow/Deny only on the hooks path; `acceptForSession` appears as a third choice only for app-server-attached sessions (v1.1).
13. **Omarchy face on macOS** (`host-research/critic.md:122`) — Default: in v1 the face is dead on macOS and the Welcome sheet suggests removing it in the face manager; v1.1 sends the degraded agent-terminal `ws`; Mission Control keystrokes are a later opt-in.
14. **Inbound focus sync on macOS** (`host-research/critic.md:123`) — Default: v1.1 Ghostty poll at 500 ms under Automation; AX later.
15. **Multiple sessions sharing one cwd in Ghostty** (`host-research/critic.md:21`) — Default: title stamps (`terminalSequence`, `tui.terminal_title thread-id`); still ambiguous → inject into none, `order.failed{ambiguous}`, one-time GUI pick persisted in `state.json.terminal_map`. *Sharper after week 3's S3 (§5.5): the cwd is the ambiguous key on this Mac and the beacon is the discriminating one, so the resolver matches the nonce first and then pins the tab; `terminal_map` keys on the surface UUID.*
16. **Audio fallback when only BLE HID is up** (`host-research/critic.md:124`) — Default: silent output, Mac mic input, surfaced in the GUI/tray; `"system_default"` available for solo users. *Since the companion app (2026-11-11) the input defaults to `"none"` as well: the Mac microphone is opened only when the owner names it in `[voice] fallback_input` (§7.1).*
17. **Slot persistence and eviction** (`host-research/critic.md:125`) — Default: §4.15 (`state.json` by key, then `(kind, cwd)`, pins override, never evict a pending card).
18. **API scopes / remote access** (`host-research/critic.md:126`) — Default: scoped tokens as §4.2; no remote access in v1; the v1.1 recipe is Tailscale `serve` in front of the loopback listener with a `phone` token, never a public bind.
19. **Gemini CLI vs Antigravity** (`host-research/critic.md:127`) — Default: keep the thin adapter; add Antigravity when its hook schema is documented.
20. **Should the `hooks` token be embedded literally in the CLI config files?** — Default: yes, because it is now worth almost nothing: scope `hooks:ingest` lets it post hook payloads to the adapters and nothing else, so a session that reads it out of `~/.claude/settings.json` gains no `agent.*` power over other sessions (§4.2, §11); `token.rotate` exists and `integrations.repair` rotates it after a port hijack.
21. **rmcp client compatibility (2026-07-28 vs 2025-11-25 sessions)** (`host-research/rust-service-stack.md:163`) — Default: the server is stateless for modern-era clients by construction (§4.16: agent key `mcp:<token>:<_meta.session_id | legacy Mcp-Session-Id | "default">`, lease refreshed by every call), and only legacy `initialize` clients get a session-keyed row; test Claude Code, Codex, Gemini against the dual-era server at the start of v1.1. Open sub-question: whether any modern client will pass `_meta.session_id` unprompted; if none does, the per-token agent is the only row and the `deck-operator` prompt documents how to opt in.
22. **Claude Desktop's timeout for a long-blocking MCP tool** (`host-research/desktop-apps.md:160`) — Default: `deck_ask` timeout 540 s with progress notifications every 15 s; measure and lower if the app cuts earlier.
23. **gpui pin** (`host-research/rust-service-stack.md:161`) — Default: stay on crates.io `=0.2.2`/`=0.5.1` so openmicrokbd patterns port verbatim; revisit only when a needed API is missing.
24. **Log rotation and where crash output goes** (`host-research/critic.md:129`) — Default: `tracing-appender` daily × 7 for `deckd.log`/`app.log`; `deckd.launchd.log` is opened and `dup2`'d onto fds 1/2 by `deckd run` itself (launchd cannot be given a per-user path from a bundle plist, §9), rotated at 10 MB, with a redacting panic hook; launchd's own spawn failures are read from `launchctl print` by `deckd doctor`; the diagnostics bundle collects all three.
25. **Which "Codex simulation mode" the Codex face key set mirrors** (`host-research/openmicrokbd.md:155`) — Removed: the Codex face is gone with the Codex Micro emulation.
26. **Is `LOCAL_PEERTOKEN` available on the macOS 27 SDK, and does `SecCodeCopyGuestWithAttributes(kSecGuestAttributeAudit)` accept it?** (critique; unverified in the research) — Default: v1 ships the pid path (`LOCAL_PEERPID` + `kSecGuestAttributePid`, `proc_pidpath` re-checked immediately after `SecCodeCheckValidity`), accepting the small pid-reuse window and logging it as a known gap; the audit-token path replaces it in v1.1 once the SDK spike passes. The v1 window is narrow (two syscalls on a peer that is already connected) and the operation it guards is a permission allow the operator is watching on the deck.
27. **Confirmation UX for guarded operations from untrusted principals** — Default: a GUI sheet (`[trust] confirm = "sheet"`) that names the principal, the agent and the exact card/order text; `"touch_id"` (`LAContext`) as an option for operators who script `op.answer` and want a stronger second factor. Rejected: a `[trust] require_trusted_for_allow = false` default — the critique's blocker stands, and an operator who wants scripts to allow permissions flips it knowingly in `config.toml` (the GUI shows a permanent warning badge while it is off).
28. **Does Ghostty's tab `name` keep the `deck:` stamp while Claude Code rewrites the title on state changes?** — Default: re-emit the stamp on `UserPromptSubmit` and `Stop` responses; week-3 spike. If Claude's own title wins anyway, drop the stamp and rely on the cwd match plus the one-time GUI pick (`state.json.terminal_map`), and ask Ghostty for a tty/pid property upstream. *Answered 2026-09-20 (Status, spike S3): **no** — `name` mirrors the live title and the next OSC 2 wipes it, the shell's prompt included, so re-emitting cannot work. The stamp becomes a one-shot beacon and deckd pins the tab with `perform action "set_tab_title:…"` (§5.5); no upstream property is needed, because window, tab and terminal already carry stable ids.*
29. **Paging shape** (v1.1) — Default: `paging_back = true` (first page 3 + `More…`, middle pages 2 + `Back` + `More…`, last ≤3 + `Back`); if `metrics.get.cards_paged.extra_presses` shows operators mostly answer on page 1, switch the default to `false` (3 per middle page, no `Back`). v1's `cards_truncated` count says whether paging is worth building at all.
30. **`report_via = "command"` for report-only Claude events** — Default: `"http"` with `timeout: 2` (the daemon answers `{}` before doing work, so the round trip is ≈ms); the `"command"` alternative is built in v1.1 only if the week-3 measurements show the synchronous http round trip is visible in Claude's turn latency.
31. **Logical HTTP sessions for `curl`-grade clients** — Default: `(token, client.name)` with `mailbox_ttl_s = 600` (§4.2), `"default"` when the client names nothing, `push` refused for them. Rejected: a server-issued session token returned by `session.hello` that the client must echo — it is what a WS connection already gives, and a stateless `curl` script would have to persist it; naming the client is the same one-liner and survives the script restarting.
32. **Corrupt `state.json` and card-id reuse** — Default: counters restart at 1 (§6.5) and the one-card exposure before the first `synced` is logged. Rejected: the earlier random per-start base — it could land on the persisted live id and made the allocator non-deterministic for no gain, since only the current device card id per slot can collide.

## 17. Why this shape and not the alternatives

- *GUI owns everything* (openmicrokbd's shape) fails the binding requirement and pins the TCC identity and audio lifetime to a window the operator will close.
- *A private GUI protocol plus a separate "public" API* doubles every feature and guarantees the public API rots; the GUI as a first-class consumer of the same JSON-RPC surface is the cheapest way to keep the API honest.
- *Integrations as special cases* would each need their own answer routing, slot and lease logic; treating every source as a virtual-agent owner makes each adapter ~200 lines and gives a third party the same power with a token.
- *Slots as identity* leak the device model into the API; agents outlive slots, so ids do too.
- *A lane for `synced`* can purge the roster; a sequenced barrier cannot.
- *Clearing the card on `answer` receipt* hides delivery failures; the device's own revert is the honest signal, and the store now agrees with it.
- *Ephemeral port fallback or exit on conflict* either rewrites the operator's hook files behind their back or takes the deck link down with an unrelated HTTP collision; degrade-and-offer does neither.
- *Scopes later* never happens; hook payloads are tagged from day one.
- *In-daemon LLM summariser*: keys, cost, privacy — solved by the open API instead.
- *HTTP over the UDS* (Tailscale's LocalAPI shape, the research's `axum::serve(UnixListener)`) would have made `curl --unix-socket` work, but the hello-first trust check, the line-oriented `deckd hook` helper and the stdio MCP shim all want a framed request stream, and one `dispatch` function serves both doors anyway; the UDS speaks newline-delimited JSON-RPC and `deckd ctl` is the shell path.
- *Per-principal ownership* looked sufficient until the UDS made every local process the same principal; sessions are the smallest unit that can own a mailbox, so they own the agent too.
- *Adapters that run their own effects* (holding oneshots, timers, delivering answers) put the same state in two places; the reducer emits effects as data and the core executes them, which is also what makes the 4 s and `hold_s` rules testable without a clock.
- *A single `deckd-audio` worker* doing synthesis and route control serialised the PTT `Capture::Open` behind a TTS buffer; splitting control from synthesis costs one thread and removes the only latency path the design could not measure ahead of time.
- *The full design in 8 weeks*: the reviewers were right that it is a 13–15 week build; cutting MCP, Gemini, paging and the long tail of GUI screens keeps the three risks the schedule exists to retire and gives every deferred item a designed slot instead of a rewrite.
